# Nebula CPU cost ledger

Target: a 2-core i7-6500U (4 MB L3) at 60 fps, 480p-720p. Every per-frame
CPU cost found in Nebula's own code (translator output, generated-code
helpers, runtime) gets one entry.

**How it was produced.** One read-only survey agent ran per area: translator
calls, translator float, translator memory and control flow, runtime
simulation thread, GX work on the simulation thread, render thread,
audio/input/OS, host SDK. A completeness critic then commissioned extra gap
surveys. The verification and synthesis stages of that workflow did not run
before the session ended, so treat these entries as **unverified survey
output**. Status reflects this branch.

**Estimates.** `est_ms` is the share of a thread times that thread's
projected time on the 6500U (CPU_GAP_REPORT: simulation 8.9-16.9 ms,
render 6-10 ms). Each entry gives its basis. Evidence classes: `measured`
cites a doc; `derived` is arithmetic from code; `hypothesis` is an
assumption.

**Rank.** Midpoint estimate divided by a risk weight (low 1, medium 2,
high 4).

**Translator measurements on this branch** (x86 per guest instruction, asm
probe loop): see docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS.md.

## Entries

### 1. G-IGPU-PRESET: No adapter-detected integrated/low-power preset: desktop post-process and enhancement defaults run unchanged on the HD 520

- **Status:** open. Area: HD 520 GPU frame time / presets. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_IGPU_PRESET (0 = today's behaviour by default; auto keys on VendorId 0x8086 or the unified-memory test)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** The adapter is enumerated and its VendorId, DeviceId and unified-memory status are known (renderer_d3d12.cpp:4233-4324 and 4386; adapter_is_unified_memory at 3075-3084). These are used only for logging and ring sizing. Every quality default stays the desktop one: post_process=1, aa_mode=2 (SMAA high), upscaler=1 (EASU+RCAS), sharpness=20, anisotropic 8, enhanced_mipmaps=1, per_pixel_lighting=1, draw_distance=2 (4x), Match Output on. This entry is the delivery vehicle for G-MATCHOUT-2X, G-SMAA, G-FSR1, G-PPL, G-AF and G-DRAWDIST, and its estimate is their sum. Do not add it to theirs.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:4231-4324, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:4382-4445, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3075-3084, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:75-87, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:110-116, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:149, runtime/src/gx/render_config.cpp:252-298
- **Why paid:** The defaults were tuned on the 9950X3D/RTX 5090 and the Arc 185H. RenderFeatureCaps is filled after load_render_config_from_file and apply_env_overrides, so nothing ever revisits the defaults once the adapter is known.
- **Frequency** (derived; Code: defaults in render_config.h and GraphicsSettings.cs; there is no preset path in render_config.cpp:252-298.): Every frame.
- **Design:** Add GALAXY_IGPU_PRESET=auto|0|1. In RendererD3D12::initialize, after adapter selection, if (VendorId==0x8086 || adapter_is_unified_memory()) and the preset is auto or 1, overwrite only keys that config.ini or the environment did not set explicitly. This needs a 'set by user' bitmask in RenderConfig, because the launcher writes every key, so it also needs a preset=auto key the launcher leaves alone. Values: efb follow cap 1, aa=3 (FXAA) or 0, upscaler=0 and sharpness=0 when output is at most 1.5x the source, anisotropic=2, per_pixel_lighting=0, draw_distance=0, enhanced_mipmaps unchanged (it lowers GPU texture bandwidth). Then set_render_config(). Everything after that is live-hot (sync_post_process_settings at renderer_d3d12.cpp:3886-3896).
- **Risk** (low): Visual downgrade on Intel only, back to the original Wii look. The risk is user confusion if explicit choices are overwritten, which the user-set bitmask avoids. A desktop with an Intel iGPU and a discrete GPU uses the discrete adapter (HIGH_PERFORMANCE preference) and is not affected.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 1.5-20 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame. Sum of the component entries at runtime defaults (854x480 window, 1x): SMAA 0.4-1.0 + EASU/RCAS 0.5-1.0 + PPL 0.3-1.2 + AF 0.2-1.0 + draw distance GPU share 0.1-0.3, less the replacement cost (FXAA/bilinear 0.15-0.3) gives about 1.5-4 ms. At the launcher defaults (720p, Match Output 2x) add G-MATCHOUT-2X's 5-14 ms, for about 7-20 ms. Not additive with the component entries.

### 2. G-MATCHOUT-2X: Launcher 'Match Output' default raises the internal EFB to 2x at 720p and 3x at 1366x768/1080p fullscreen on the HD 520

- **Status:** open. Area: HD 520 GPU frame time / internal resolution. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_IGPU_EFB_SCALE_CAP (0 = no cap, today's behaviour; 1 = cap auto-follow at 1x)`. Already listed: False PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:536-538 suggests validating '480p internal with a 1080p output blit' on the HD 530 but does not note that the shipped Match Output default picks 2x/3x
- **What:** The launcher writes internal_follow_window=1 (MatchOutput=true), a 1280x720 window and EfbScale=2. The renderer then picks the smallest EFB scale that covers the picture: ceil(max(w/640, h/456)). That gives 2x (1280x1056, 4x the pixels) for a 1280x720 window and 3x (1920x1584, 9x) for the 1366x768 or 1920x1080 panels these laptops ship with. Every TEV pixel, every full-size scene capture (640x456 scaled) and the XFB copy grow with the square of the scale. SMAA then runs on a 1280x960 source, because a 1280-wide source is not 1.5x a 1280-wide window, followed by a box resample and RCAS.
- **Where:** installer/src/Launcher/GraphicsSettings.cs:51-58, installer/src/Launcher/GraphicsSettings.cs:120-121, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3866-3883, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3898-3910, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:31-34, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:99-103, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:108-127
- **Why paid:** A desktop-oriented quality default: the launcher matches the internal resolution to the output so the picture is sharp. No adapter class is considered, and the only cap is display_efb_scale_cap() plus a memory-fit check.
- **Frequency** (derived; Code: renderer_d3d12.cpp:3875-3878 and GraphicsSettings.cs:51-58 defaults. Not confirmed which config a fresh install writes when GALAXY_SKIP_LAUNCHER=1.): Every frame once a window of 1280x720 or larger, or fullscreen, is used with the launcher defaults. The scale formula (renderer_d3d12.cpp:3875-3878) gives 2 at 1280x720 and 3 at 1366x768 and 1920x1080.
- **Design:** Add an adapter-class cap. When DXGI VendorId==0x8086 or adapter_is_unified_memory() (renderer_d3d12.cpp:3075-3084) is true, clamp the follow-window result to 1 inside apply_pending_efb_scale (the existing min with display_efb_scale_cap at :3906), and let FSR1 or bilinear do the upscale. The launcher shows 'Match output (capped at 1x on integrated graphics)'.
- **Risk** (low): The picture is softer at 720p and above (1x EFB upscaled). There is no correctness risk because 1x is the native Wii path. A user who explicitly sets efb_scale keeps it, because the cap applies only to the auto-follow value.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 5-14 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame, not CPU. The 1x TEV fill is assumed 1.5-4 ms on the HD 520: 338k pixels x 3-5 overdraw x 30-80 ALU ops on about 173 G lane-ops/s (24 EU x 8 lanes x 0.9 GHz), plus about 27 MB of color/depth read-write at 15-25 GB/s. At 2x that becomes 4x: 6-16 ms. The extra over 1x is 4.5-12 ms. Add full-size copies at 4x pixels (+0.2-0.6) and SMAA on a 1280x960 source instead of 640x480 (+0.8-1.5). Total 5-14 ms more GPU per frame at 720p; about 2.25x that at 3x.

### 3. G-POWER: Shared 15 W package: GPU load lowers the CPU clock below the 3.0 GHz the projections assume

- **Status:** open. Area: Shared 15 W budget. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_IGPU_PRESET (delivery); GALAXY_TRACE_PACKAGE_POWER=0 (new diagnostic)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:134 (as a hypothesis, unquantified)
- **What:** The i7-6500U's two cores and the HD 520 share PL1 = 15 W. CPU_GAP_REPORT's 8.9-16.9 ms simulation and 6-10 ms render projections assume both cores at 3.0 GHz. Two loaded cores at 3.0 GHz draw about 9-12 W, the uncore and memory about 2 W, and a GPU busy 40-70 % at 0.9-1.05 GHz about 3-7 W. That exceeds 15 W, so after PL2 (about 28 s) the PCU lowers the core clock, typically to 2.2-2.8 GHz under combined CPU and GPU game load on this SKU. Every GPU ms removed by the entries above lowers GPU residency and voltage and returns clock to the simulation thread.
- **Where:** docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:35-40, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:131-135, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:58-64, installer/runtime-env.json:287-290
- **Why paid:** Hardware: one package power limit. Nothing in the runtime is power-aware. The adapter class is known but never used, and there is no GPU-cost budget.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:134 notes it as a hypothesis. No RAPL or clock trace exists for the 6500U.): Continuous, once the PL2 turbo window expires (about 28 s into play).
- **Design:** (1) Ship G-IGPU-PRESET so the GPU idles more of each 16.7 ms. (2) Add a measurement: log package power and core clock (RAPL PKG_ENERGY via a user-mode-readable source such as Intel Power Gadget, or QueryPerformanceFrequency against RDTSC/APERF where available) with [present-stats], so the next laptop recording shows GHz against GPU-ms. (3) Keep max_fps 60 with frame pacing (today's default) so the GPU never races ahead.
- **Risk** (low): The preset only lowers GPU work. The measurement is diagnostic. No correctness exposure.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.8-3.5 ms/frame. Simulation-thread ms per frame, formula est = sim_ms_6500U x (3.0/f_actual - 3.0/f_after). With sim 12.9 ms at 3.0 GHz: throttled from 2.8 GHz to 3.0 GHz is 12.9 x (1.071 - 1) = 0.9 ms; from 2.4 to 2.8 GHz is 12.9 x (1.25 - 1.071) = 2.3 ms; from 2.2 to 2.6 GHz is 12.9 x (1.364 - 1.154) = 2.7 ms. The render thread (8 ms) gains in proportion, about 0.6x those figures, on the other core. Range 0.8-3.5 ms on the critical path.

### 4. R-DRAWDONE-TAIL: GXDrawDone blocks the simulation thread until the render thread has consumed the frame's tail (queue backlog plus last chunk)

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: FIFO streaming handoff. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_STREAM_QUEUE_BYTES (default 0 = today's chunk-count admission); existing GALAXY_GX_FIFO_STREAM_KB`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md render section (queue-full attempts become a frame-boundary wait); docs/PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P1.5 step 3 (queue depth)
- **What:** At GXDrawDone the simulation thread hands the remaining FIFO to the renderer (submit_gx_at_draw_done_wait) and sleeps until the PE finish arrives at consumption. That is everything still queued plus the parse and record of the final chunk. Streaming hands off 32 KB chunks mid-frame, but only when render_queue_has_space() reports fewer than GALAXY_RENDER_QUEUE_DEPTH non-present chunks. That limit counts chunks, not bytes, and the installer sets it to 2. When the queue is full, streaming defers by 8 KB, the bytes pile up, and the final chunk grows.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:17037-17096, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:17106-17155, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:33221-33234, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:6273-6286, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5703-5738, runtime/src/gx/render_config.cpp:448-452
- **Why paid:** The renderer reads guest RAM live (GALAXY_RENDER_LIVE_MEMORY=1, no snapshot), so GXDrawDone must not return before the render thread's CPU reads of this frame are done. The game relies on that to reuse display lists and vertex buffers. Chunk-count admission bounds latency and working set.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:287 (draw-done submit to PE finish 1.19 ms mean, p90 2.4 ms, 9950X3D title route); docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md (3,171 queue-full attempts)): Once per frame. 3,171 mid-frame streaming attempts found the render queue full, against 15,551 that went through (package run).
- **Design:** (1) Admit streamed chunks against a byte budget of outstanding FIFO (e.g. 256 KB) instead of a chunk count, so small mid-frame chunks are never refused while the render thread is busy. (2) On two-core machines lower GALAXY_GX_FIFO_STREAM_KB to 16, so the chunk left at GXDrawDone is at most ~16 KB plus the deferral. (3) Submit immediately at the GXCopyDisp/GXSetDrawDone mark rather than at the next 4 KB flush. All three need only runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp and the backend's admission predicate.
- **Risk** (medium): A deeper byte-based queue raises worst-case input latency and working set when the render thread falls behind. Smaller chunks multiply the fixed per-submission cost (R-SUBMIT-FIXED) and render-side per-chunk setup. This needs the timeline.bin draw-done to PE-finish histogram on the laptop.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 1.5-6.7 ms/frame. Wall-clock time on the simulation thread's critical path, not CPU. It costs a frame only when sim work plus the wait exceeds 16.7 ms. Measured desktop wait: 1.19 ms mean. Scaled by the render-thread ratio 6500U/desktop = (6..10)/(2.3..1.8) = 2.6..5.6, giving 3.1-6.7 ms. The low bound of 1.5 ms allows for render improvements since Native11. The design is expected to remove about half.

### 5. R-THP-DECODE: THP video: one-frame-ahead scalar decode, plus a simulation-thread wait and a 353 KB plane copy per video frame

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: THP video. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_THP_DECODE_AHEAD (default 1 = today; 2 = ring of 3) and GALAXY_THP_SIMD_IDCT (default 0)`. Already listed: True PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P6; PERFORMANCE_README.md item 11
- **What:** NativeThpAsyncDecoder holds one pending job. The simulation thread joins it before every GX frame submission and before the next decode (condition_variable wait), then copies the Y/U/V planes (about 353 KB for 640x368) into guest RAM through address_space->copy. The decoder has an 8-bit Huffman lookup but a scalar float AAN IDCT and scalar 8x8 tile writes. The source payload is copied into a std::vector for every job.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:650-754, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:23459-23476, runtime/src/native_thp_video.cpp:541-610, runtime/src/native_thp_video.cpp:612-632, runtime/src/native_thp_video.cpp:650-704
- **Why paid:** Decode-ahead was the first step off the inline decode (4-6 ms on the simulation thread). The planes must be in guest RAM, with renderer dirty tracking, before the GX frame that samples them.
- **Frequency** (measured; PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P6: decode 2.5-2.8 ms, wait 2.0-2.3 ms per 640x368 frame on the 185H; take_finished blocked 5.5 % of samples): Per video frame (about 30/s) during the intro and cutscenes only; zero in gameplay
- **Design:** (1) A ring of 3 jobs: decode two frames ahead and join only the frame the game asks for. (2) An SSE2 integer IDCT (the libjpeg-turbo jidctint-sse2 scheme) with the row-sparsity shortcuts kept. Write 8-byte rows straight into the tiled layout. (3) Decode into a buffer already in guest RAM layout and publish by marking dirty ranges at join. Better, decode directly into the guest destination, since the game never reads the planes on the CPU, and cut the 353 KB copy. (4) While the simulation thread waits on the decoder, raise the decoder's priority above render (see R-TWO-CORE-PLACEMENT).
- **Risk** (low): An IDCT rounding difference changes pixels by ±1 (the THP decode has a pinned quirk constant, kBuggyQuarterIdct, which must be kept). Direct decode into guest RAM must not race a GX read of the previous frame's planes; use double-buffered destinations if the game reuses them.
- **Estimate** (derived): 1-3.1 ms/frame. Video playback only. 185H decode 2.5-2.8 ms * (PassMark 3,711/1,686 = 2.2) = 5.5-6.2 ms per video frame on the 6500U, which is 2.75-3.1 ms of decoder CPU per 60 Hz frame on the shared cores. The simulation thread's wait, 2.0-2.3 ms per video frame measured on the 185H, scales to about 2-5 ms per video frame, or 1-2.5 ms per 60 Hz frame. Range 1.0-3.1 ms/frame while a movie plays; 0 in gameplay.

### 6. G-SMAA: SMAA 1x high (3 passes plus a stencil target) on every presented frame

- **Status:** open. Area: Presentation post-process. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_AA=3 via GALAXY_IGPU_PRESET (existing GALAXY_AA, GALAXY_SMAA_QUALITY; default unchanged)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** aa_mode=2 (auto) runs SMAA whenever the XFB is not 1.5x the window on both axes. At 1x with an 854x480 or 1280x720 window it always runs. It records 3 full-screen passes at the source size: edge detection writing RG8 and a stencil mask, weights reading the edges plus the AreaTex and SearchTex lookups through the stencil, and the neighborhood blend. It also does two clears and five render-target switches with barriers. smaa_quality=2 (HIGH) has a longer search than LOW or MEDIUM. Each pass creates 3 SRVs on the CPU (post_process.cpp:522-546).
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:110-116, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:98-129, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:516-557, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:559-612, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:797-840, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:9892-9907
- **Why paid:** An image-quality enhancement for the native D3D12 path. The 'auto' mode skips it only for a supersampled XFB, which never happens at 1x.
- **Frequency** (derived; post_process.cpp:109-116: supersampled is false for a 640-wide source and an 854- or 1280-wide target.): Once per presented frame (60/s).
- **Design:** In the iGPU preset, set aa=3 (FXAA: one pass, no stencil, no lookups, about a quarter of SMAA) or aa=0. Also allow smaa_quality=0 (LOW). Existing env: GALAXY_AA, GALAXY_SMAA_QUALITY.
- **Risk** (low): FXAA is softer on text and HUD edges, since AA runs before the HUD is separated at hud_output_resolution=0. No correctness risk. The SMAA self-test fallback path already exists.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.4-2.5 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame. The SMAA paper gives SMAA 1x at 1080p as about 1 ms on a GTX 470 (1.1 TFLOPS, 134 GB/s). The HD 520 has about 0.4 TFLOPS and 15-34 GB/s shared, so 3-6x slower: 3-6 ms at 2.07 MP. Scaled to a 640x480 source (0.31 MP): 0.45-0.9 ms. Use 0.4-1.0 at 1x. At 2x (1280x960 source, 1.23 MP): 1.2-2.5 ms. FXAA replacement about 0.1-0.25 ms.

### 7. G-PRESENT-SERIAL: Render thread serialised on GPU completion: 2 frames in flight counted per chunk, plus swap-chain latency 1

- **Status:** open. Area: Presentation / CPU-GPU overlap. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT (default 2); GALAXY_SWAP_CHAIN_FRAME_LATENCY=2 plus GALAXY_WAIT_FOR_FRAME_LATENCY=1 in the iGPU preset`. Already listed: False PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:486-489 proposes frames in flight 2 to 3 but does not note that slots are consumed per chunk
- **What:** kFramesInFlight = 2 slots are consumed per render chunk, not per game frame. Each chunk does begin_frame and end_frame, and begin_frame blocks in wait_for_fence_value on the slot's previous fence. With about 3.25 chunks per game frame, the CPU can record at most about 2 chunks (about 0.6 game frame) ahead of the GPU. Present runs with SetMaximumFrameLatency(1), the waitable object is not waited on (GALAXY_WAIT_FOR_FRAME_LATENCY=0), vsync is 0 and tearing is off. On a GPU-bound HD 520 frame, Present and begin_frame block the render thread for the GPU. Profiles show this as kernel/driver time or condvar wait, and the simulation then waits on a full render queue at the frame boundary.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/gx/renderer_d3d12.h:60-63, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5638-5662, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:6368-6408, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5995-6045, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:4497-4526, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:8263, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:9030-9045, installer/runtime-env.json:243-246, installer/runtime-env.json:287-290
- **Why paid:** Input latency: swap-chain latency 1 is a stated goal (PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:486-489). The 2-slot ring bounds upload-ring memory. Chunked streaming added more begin/end pairs per frame without raising the slot count.
- **Frequency** (derived; Measured 16,778 mid-frame streaming submissions in 124 s = 135/s = 2.25 extra chunks per frame (CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:128), assuming one streamed submission is one chunk. Slot logic from code.): Every chunk (about 3.25 per frame) and every Present (60/s). It blocks only when the GPU trails.
- **Design:** (1) Count fences per game frame: in begin_frame wait on the fence of the last chunk of game frame N-2 rather than chunk N-2. This needs ring segments per game frame, which RingMode::Interp already implements (renderer_d3d12.cpp:5748-5773), or kFramesInFlight 3 at 1.5x ring reservation (reservation, not residency, per :4657-4665). (2) On iGPU use swap-chain latency 2 with the waitable object waited at the top of the frame. Gate both behind GALAXY_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT (default 2) and the existing GALAXY_SWAP_CHAIN_FRAME_LATENCY.
- **Risk** (medium): Up to +1 frame (16.7 ms) of input latency. The upload ring reservation grows by 50 %, and the 64 MiB vertex ring per slot is already at its measured 16.1 MiB peak. Readback consumers that key on the slot fence (pointer depth, async peek) must be rechecked.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.5-5 ms/frame. Render-thread wall ms per frame lost to non-overlap: est = GPU_ms - overlap. At runtime defaults (480p, 1x) the GPU is estimated at 4-9 ms; with only about 0.6 frame of slack, the hypothesis is that 10-50 % of the GPU time shows up as render-thread blocking, giving 0.5-4.5 ms. At 2x EFB (G-MATCHOUT-2X) the GPU exceeds 12 ms and the blocking grows to the whole excess. Desktop 'waiting/condvar spin' is about 10 % of the render thread (CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:125); 10 % x 8 ms = 0.8 ms on the 6500U before Intel GPU time is added.

### 8. G-FSR1: FSR 1 EASU plus RCAS through a window-sized intermediate on every present

- **Status:** open. Area: Presentation post-process. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_UPSCALER=0 and GALAXY_SHARPNESS=0 via GALAXY_IGPU_PRESET (existing; default unchanged)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** With upscaler=1 and sharpness=20, every enlarging present runs EASU (12 Gather4 fetches and an FP32 ALU-heavy kernel, post_process.hlsl:75-77) into a window-sized RGBA8 intermediate 'scaled_', then RCAS from that into the backbuffer. That is two full-window passes, one extra RT write plus read, two RT switches and one barrier. The XFB (640 wide at 1x) is always smaller than an 854x480 or 1280x720 window, so EASU always runs. needs_scaled forces the intermediate whenever RCAS is on.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:62-90, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:117-128, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:851-912, runtime/src/gx/post_process.hlsl:75-77, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:110-116
- **Why paid:** An image-quality enhancement chosen on desktop: FSR1 instead of the bilinear blit for upscaling, plus mild sharpening.
- **Frequency** (derived; post_process.cpp:119-127: enlarging is true for a 640-wide source and an 854+ wide target, and rcas is true for sharpness 20.): Once per presented frame (60/s).
- **Design:** In the iGPU preset, set upscaler=0 and sharpness=0 so plan.resample_to_target draws one footprint/bilinear pass straight into the backbuffer, with no intermediate. A cheaper middle option is EASU straight to the backbuffer with RCAS skipped (sharpness=0 removes the scaled_ target). Existing env: GALAXY_UPSCALER, GALAXY_SHARPNESS.
- **Risk** (low): Softer upscale. No functional risk; both paths already exist and are selectable live.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.5-2.2 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame. EASU is about 150-200 FP32 lane-ops per output pixel. At 854x480 (0.41 MP) that is 61-82 M ops / 173 G/s = 0.35-0.47 ms; at 1280x720 (0.92 MP) 0.8-1.06 ms. RCAS is about 40-60 ops: 0.1/0.27 ms. The scaled_ round trip is 8 B/px: 3.3 MB / 7.4 MB at 15-25 GB/s = 0.13-0.5 ms. Totals: 480p 0.5-1.0 ms, 720p 1.2-2.2 ms. The bilinear replacement costs 0.05-0.15 ms.

### 9. G-DRAWDIST: Default draw distance is 4x the game's, and LOD distances are scaled 4x as well

- **Status:** open. Area: Shared CPU/GPU budget / scene content. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_DRAW_DISTANCE=0 via GALAXY_IGPU_PRESET; new GALAXY_DRAW_DISTANCE_ASPECT=0 (1 = scale by aspect)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** draw_distance defaults to 2, a scale of 4.0. Once per frame apply_draw_distance multiplies the ClippingJudge distances of all 7 clipping levels and the shadow far clip by 4. The LodDistance site hook divides LodCtrl's distance by 4, so high-LOD models are kept 4x farther away. More actors pass clipping and are drawn with their particles, light glows and shadows. Assuming the clipping-driven execution model of the SMG decompilation (Petari), more actors also run movement/calcAnim on the simulation thread. The render thread gets more draws and vertices, and the GPU gets more fill: alpha-blended particles and glows are the expensive pixels on an iGPU.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:146-149, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:23966-24049, runtime/src/site_hooks.cpp:54-56, runtime/src/site_hooks.cpp:115-120, installer/src/Launcher/GraphicsSettings.cs:58
- **Why paid:** Wider pictures (aspect follows the window, 16:9 and up) showed the game's cut-offs at the screen sides, so the runtime extends them.
- **Frequency** (derived; native_runtime.cpp:23978-23986 runs per frame (called at :24163). The size of the effect is not measured.): Every frame: both the scaled clipping and the extra actors and draws.
- **Design:** Set draw_distance=0 (or 1 for 16:9) in the iGPU preset. Better: scale by aspect, using scale = max(1, aspect/(4/3)) instead of a fixed 4x, which gives 1.33 at 16:9. Existing env: GALAXY_DRAW_DISTANCE.
- **Risk** (low): Pop-in at the screen sides on widescreen at the original distances. 0 is the shipped Wii behaviour. A gameplay difference is possible only if clipping affects actor simulation, and then 0 is the original.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.5-2.2 ms/frame. CPU ms per frame, formula est = share_of_thread x thread_ms_6500U. Simulation: game code is 62.5 % x 12.9 ms = 8.1 ms; assume 3-15 % of it is extra actors beyond 1x distance, giving 0.24-1.2 ms. Render: the draw-proportional items (state 16 + parse 13.5 + validation 13 + driver 11.5 + vertex decode 8 = 62 %) x 8 ms = 5 ms; assume 5-20 % extra draws, giving 0.25-1.0 ms. CPU total 0.5-2.2 ms. GPU adds 0.1-2 ms of extra fill (particles and glows), not included above. The measured desktop profile ran at this default, so the 6500U projections already contain this cost.

### 10. G-INTEL-UMD: Intel Gen9 D3D12 driver CPU per call and per submission versus the NVIDIA 11.5 % baseline

- **Status:** open. Area: Intel driver CPU on the render thread. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_D3D_CALL_COUNTERS=0 (diagnostic); GALAXY_GX_CONSTANT_DEDUPE=0`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:35 (driver excluded from the render range; 'costs more CPU on Intel graphics', unquantified); NVIDIA 11.5 % at :124
- **What:** The render-thread profile attributes 11.5 % to the NVIDIA driver (desktop). The 6500U projection scales that share by CPU speed only. Intel's Gen9 D3D12 UMD does more CPU work per state call: it translates root CBV/descriptor-table changes into binding-table and surface-state updates, inserts PIPE_CONTROL flushes for barriers and RT changes, and tracks resolves. draw() issues up to 11 calls per draw (SetPipelineState, 2 root CBVs, 2 descriptor tables, root SRV, OMSetRenderTargets, scissor, viewport, topology, VB, IB, Draw), elided when redundant. CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:35 already flags that Intel graphics drivers cost more CPU.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7424-7589, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5550-5565, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:35, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:120-127
- **Why paid:** A D3D12 draw-per-GX-draw model with per-draw root CBVs for VS and PS constants, plus full rebinds after each EFB copy or overlay (invalidate_gx_bindings).
- **Frequency** (derived; Call sequence from renderer_d3d12.cpp:7424-7589. The draw count per frame is not measured: 0.21-0.26 ms of NVIDIA time on the desktop at an assumed 0.05-0.1 us per call implies about 2,500-5,000 calls per frame.): Per draw (about 3-6 calls after elision); per EFB copy about 28 calls (G-EFBCOPY-CPU); per chunk about 12 calls plus one ExecuteCommandLists (G-CHUNK-SUBMIT).
- **Design:** No single switch removes this. Reduce the call count: (a) G-EFBCOPY-CPU (no root-signature switch per copy); (b) G-CHUNK-SUBMIT; (c) the listed 'rebuild constants and descriptor tables only when inputs change' (CPU_GAP_REPORT item 6), so the two root CBVs are skipped for identical content: a content hash over the 1-2 KB constants, with the existing pointer compare at :7433-7442 then eliding; (d) G-DRAWDIST cuts draws. Add per-frame D3D call counters (calls/draw, barriers, RT switches) under [gx-frame-timing] so the laptop recording gives the real Intel multiplier.
- **Risk** (low): The counters are diagnostic. Constant dedupe risks stale constants if the hash is wrong, which frame dumps would catch.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.35-2.3 ms/frame. Formula est = share_of_thread x thread_ms_6500U x (Intel/NVIDIA multiplier - 1). NVIDIA share 11.5 % x 8 ms = 0.92 ms is already inside the 6-10 ms projection. The Intel Gen9 D3D12 UMD multiplier is assumed 1.4-3.5x, giving extra 0.92 x 0.4 = 0.37 to 0.92 x 2.5 = 2.3 ms.

### 11. G-PPL: Per-pixel lighting: an 8-light dynamic loop with 4 StructuredBuffer loads per light per pixel

- **Status:** open. Area: HD 520 GPU frame time / TEV fill. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_PER_PIXEL_LIGHTING=0 via GALAXY_IGPU_PRESET (existing; default unchanged)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** per_pixel_lighting=true moves GX channel lighting into the pixel shader for every lit specialized draw. ppl_apply loops over up to 8 lights per channel for color and alpha. Each enabled light does 4 mtx_palette StructuredBuffer reads, rsqrt or sqrt, several dots and integer rounding. It also adds 4 interpolants (TEXCOORD8-11) to the VS output. The Wii evaluates these lights per vertex, so the per-vertex path is the exact one.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/shader_gen.cpp:457-579, runtime/src/gx/shader_gen.cpp:823-830, runtime/src/gx/shader_gen.cpp:1023-1027, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:84-87, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:8254-8262
- **Why paid:** A visual enhancement. It applies to specialized triangle pipelines.
- **Frequency** (derived; shader_gen.cpp:536-579 runs per pixel when kVsKeyPerPixelLighting is set. Lit coverage is not measured.): Every lit pixel of every lit draw, every frame.
- **Design:** Set per_pixel_lighting=0 in the iGPU preset (existing GALAXY_PER_PIXEL_LIGHTING). The change re-keys pipelines (gx_backend.cpp:8257-8261), so the preset must apply before PSO prewarm, or the persisted precompile list must hold the per-vertex keys.
- **Risk** (low): Back to the original per-vertex Wii look. The one risk is a first-run PSO miss burst if the cache was warmed with PPL keys: uber fallback plus compile hitches for one session.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.3-1.2 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame at 1x. Assume 0.3-0.8 M lit pixel-shader invocations per frame (338k px, 20-50 % lit, 2-3x overdraw) and 2 lights x about 80 ops + 40 overhead = about 200 ops: 60-160 M lane-ops / 173 G/s = 0.35-0.9 ms, plus data-port loads. Range 0.3-1.2 ms. At 2x EFB it is 4x that.

### 12. G-AF: 8x anisotropic filtering on every mipmapped linear sampler, and on generated-mip textures

- **Status:** open. Area: HD 520 GPU frame time / texture sampling. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_ANISOTROPIC_FILTERING=2 via GALAXY_IGPU_PRESET (existing; default unchanged)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Every sampler whose GX min/mag are linear with mips gets D3D12_FILTER_ANISOTROPIC with MaxAnisotropy 8. This also forces linear mip blending where GX asked for point-mip. enhanced_mipmaps=1 gives authored-mipless linear textures a mip chain, so AF applies to those too. On Gen9 an 8x AF fetch on oblique surfaces (Galaxy's planet ground) costs up to 8 bilinear or trilinear probes through 3 samplers.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3516-3560, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3660-3680, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/render_config.h:75-82, runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:400-404
- **Why paid:** An image-quality enhancement chosen on desktop.
- **Frequency** (derived; renderer_d3d12.cpp:3543-3553.): Every texture sample on anisotropic samplers, every frame.
- **Design:** Set anisotropic_filtering=2 (or 1) in the iGPU preset. Keep enhanced_mipmaps on, because mips lower the texture bandwidth the iGPU pays. Existing env: GALAXY_ANISOTROPIC_FILTERING.
- **Risk** (low): Blurrier ground textures at grazing angles. AF 1 equals the Wii's trilinear behaviour.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.2-1 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame at 1x. Assume 2-4 M texture samples per frame (1-1.7 M PS invocations x 1-3 samples). The HD 520 does about 12 bilinear texels/clk across 3 samplers, so about 0.3 ms at 1x filtering. 8x AF on 20-40 % of samples at a 2-4x average probe multiplier adds 0.1-0.5 ms, plus extra texel bandwidth on UMA. Total 0.2-1.0 ms.

### 13. G-DISPATCH-DENSE: 10.6 MB sparse direct-dispatch table: one TLB page and one cache line per bctrl target

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: dispatch table. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--dense-dispatch (translator, emits GALAXY_DENSE_DISPATCH); runtime always publishes the hash when GALAXY_DIRECT_DISPATCH=1`. Already listed: False CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:82 lists 'dispatch-table checks' only as instruction count at bctrl; PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:575 and :586 discuss the table for interior entries and withheld entries, not its footprint. The local task list has a pending 'direct-mapped bctrl' item that may overlap.
- **What:** build_direct_dispatch_table allocates one 8-byte NativeGameFunction per guest word between the first and last function record. That is about 1.32 M slots, 10.6 MB, about 2,600 4 KB pages. Only the ~160 k published entries (functions plus interior entries) are non-null, so about 88 % is null. Every bctrl/bclrl through fast::call_guest_indirect loads services->direct_dispatch[index>>2]. The hot targets are scattered over the whole guest .text range, so each distinct hot target costs its own table line and, at the table's density (one 4 KB page per 2 KB of PowerPC code), very likely its own page. About 2,000 to 5,000 hot targets touch roughly 1,000 to 2,500 distinct table pages. That is more than the 64-entry L1 dTLB and on the order of the 1,536-entry STLB, which is also shared with 7.7 MB of hot code and the guest data.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15869-15919, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:33481-33486, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:2039-2063, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:484-494, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:8080-8106, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:11469-11478
- **Why paid:** ABI 24 chose the simplest O(1) map from a guest address to a host function: base-relative index, no hash, null for withheld targets. It was sized for correctness (any 4-byte-aligned address in range is answerable), not for footprint. On the 96 MB L3 desktop the table lines and their PTEs stay cached, so the cost never showed up.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Instruction mix and the 8 x86 per guest instruction figure (CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:86-99). bctrl share and IPC are assumptions. The '[direct-dispatch] entries= withheld=' log line (native_runtime.cpp:15917) gives the exact table population.): Once per executed bctrl/bclrl. Estimated 20 k-80 k per frame: desktop game code is 0.625 x 2.1-2.4 ms = 1.31-1.50 ms/frame. At about 5.5 GHz and IPC 1.5-3 that is 10.8-24.8 M x86 instructions; at 8 x86 per guest instruction that is 1.35-3.1 M guest instructions/frame. Calls are 8 % of those (108 k-248 k). The share that are bctrl (assumed 15-35 %) is a hypothesis. Distinct hot table pages per frame: about 1,000-2,500 (derived from table density, 512 guest words per 4 KB of table).
- **Design:** Replace the per-word table with a dense open-addressed hash over the published entries, keyed by guest address. Use 8-byte slots {u32 guest_address, u32 rva-from-module-base}. With 32 k-64 k slots it is 256-512 KB (64-128 pages), small enough to sit in the STLB and mostly in L2/L3. If only the translator's address-taken set (the possible bctrl targets, ppc_dataflow.rs ModuleAbi) is published, the table is about 256 KB. Lookup: multiply-shift hash, at most 4 linear probes, compare the key, call module_base + rva. A miss or empty slot falls back to call_guest_indirect_boundary exactly as a null entry does today. Runtime: build_direct_dispatch_table builds the hash and publishes it in new services fields (direct_dispatch_hash, mask, module_base) next to the old table; the old table stays for old modules. Translator: --dense-dispatch emits '#define GALAXY_DENSE_DISPATCH 1' into functions.h (the same pattern as --assume-paired-singles), and the header's call_guest_indirect probes the hash instead of the table. At startup the runtime verifies that all 160 k entries round-trip (a few ms). Optionally place slots hot-first from the exact profile used for T-LINK-ORDER. Minimal alternative: a 4-byte RVA per word (5.3 MB, ~1,300 pages), which halves the pages but still exceeds the dTLB.
- **Risk** (low): A wrong hash build or RVA would call the wrong host function, a silent miscompile-class failure. The startup round-trip self-check over every published entry and a key compare on every lookup turn that into a fallback to the audited boundary path. Withheld targets and interior addresses must stay absent so they keep the boundary path. The per-site static caches remain the fallback. An ABI field is appended, so older modules keep the per-word table.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.07-1 ms/frame. I ran a synthetic model in the scratchpad (dispatch_bench2.cpp, dispatch_bench3.cpp) on the sandbox Xeon VM, not Skylake, with no perf counters. Each call does 8 random reads over a 24 MB working set, then a dependent lookup over 3,000 hot targets. The 8B/word table added +25.8 ns/call with 4 KB pages and +10.4 ns with THP. A 256 KB address-taken hash added +9.9 ns (4 KB pages) and +3.8 ns (THP). At 2.1 GHz the table costs ~54 cycles per bctrl on a dependent chain. Real code hides part of it because the BTB predicts the target, so I assume 30-70 % is exposed, rising toward 60 cycles on a 4 MB L3. Cost = bctrl/frame x exposed cycles / 3.0e9: low 20 k x 16 / 3e9 = 0.11 ms, high 80 k x 60 / 3e9 = 1.6 ms. The hash removes ~60 %, giving savings of 0.07-1.0 ms.

### 14. G-LARGE-PAGES: Guest RAM section and dispatch table are mapped with 4 KB pages only

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: TLB reach. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_LARGE_PAGES (default 0 = today's 4 KB views)`. Already listed: False CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:147 mentions TLB misses only as a hypothesis for link order
- **What:** MEM1 (24 MB) and MEM2 (64 MB) are pagefile-backed sections (CreateFileMappingW, PAGE_READWRITE, no SEC_LARGE_PAGES), mapped through MapViewOfFile3 into placeholders with 4 KB pages at six guest aliases plus a host view. The dispatch table is a std::vector, also on 4 KB pages. Guest RAM is 22,528 4 KB pages. The translated code's hot data, guest stacks, the render thread's display-list, vertex-array and texture reads, and the table together are several thousand hot pages against Skylake's 64-entry L1 dTLB and 1,536-entry STLB. On a 4 MB L3 that also holds 7.7 MB of hot code, page-walk PTE lines often come from DRAM. The repository has no MEM_LARGE_PAGES, SEC_LARGE_PAGES, GetLargePageMinimum or SeLockMemoryPrivilege code (grep).
- **Where:** runtime/src/flat_guest_memory.cpp:71-87, runtime/src/flat_guest_memory.cpp:89-110, runtime/src/flat_guest_memory.cpp:136-186, runtime/include/galaxy/flat_guest_memory.h:16-19, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15897
- **Why paid:** The flat view adapted from WiiCompiled uses ordinary 4 KB placeholder views. Large pages on Windows need the 'Lock pages in memory' user right (SeLockMemoryPrivilege), which is not granted by default, plus physically contiguous 2 MB frames, so nobody enabled them.
- **Frequency** (measured; Instruction mix, CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:86-99 (static, weighted by samples)): Every guest load and store from translated code (integer loads 15.5 %, stores 8.9 %, float loads and stores 9.5 % of hot guest instructions). Also every render-thread and worker read of guest RAM, and every bctrl table access.
- **Design:** Opt-in GALAXY_LARGE_PAGES=1. Enable SeLockMemoryPrivilege with AdjustTokenPrivileges. Create both sections with SEC_COMMIT|SEC_LARGE_PAGES; the sizes are already multiples of 2 MB (MEM1 12 pages, MEM2 32 pages). Map the six aliases with MapViewOfFile3(MEM_REPLACE_PLACEHOLDER|MEM_LARGE_PAGES), and map the host view with FILE_MAP_LARGE_PAGES. Every alias base is 256 MB aligned. Allocate the dispatch table, or the G-DISPATCH-DENSE hash, with VirtualAlloc(MEM_LARGE_PAGES), 12 MB / 6 pages. If any step fails (privilege missing, fragmentation, a Windows build that rejects placeholder plus large-page views), release everything, fall back to today's 4 KB path, and log '[large-pages] guest=0|1 table=0|1 reason='. The unmapped gaps between aliases stay unmapped, so the fastmem fault handler is unchanged.
- **Risk** (medium): Most users do not hold the privilege (it needs secpol plus a re-login), so the benefit reaches only opted-in users unless the installer grants it. Large-page allocation can fail on an 8 GB laptop with fragmented RAM, which needs the clean fallback. It has not been verified that MapViewOfFile3 accepts MEM_REPLACE_PLACEHOLDER together with MEM_LARGE_PAGES on every supported Windows 10/11 build; the fallback is to map at exact addresses without placeholders, which has a small race against other allocations in the gaps. 88 MB becomes non-pageable. Page protection becomes 2 MB granular, but no VirtualProtect on guest RAM exists today (grep).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.2-1.3 ms/frame. Formula: est_ms = share_of_thread x thread_ms_6500U. I assume DTLB-walk cycles are 2-8 % of the simulation thread and 1-4 % of the render thread on Skylake with this footprint, and that large pages remove most of them: sim 0.02-0.08 x 12.9 ms = 0.26-1.0 ms, render 0.01-0.04 x 8 ms = 0.08-0.32 ms. I lowered the low bound for locality. Synthetic support (sandbox Xeon, not Skylake): random reads over a 24 MB buffer took 47.2 ns/access with 4 KB pages and 35.8 ns with THP (-24 %); the dispatch-table add-on dropped from +25.8 to +10.4 ns. Needs Skylake counters: DTLB_LOAD_MISSES.WALK_ACTIVE, DTLB_STORE_MISSES.WALK_ACTIVE, CYCLE_ACTIVITY.STALLS_L3_MISS.

### 15. R-TWO-CORE-PLACEMENT: No thread placement plan for 2 cores / 4 threads: simulation and render can share a physical core with SMT siblings and helpers

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: threads / scheduling. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_TWO_CORE_PLACEMENT (default 0)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Later 7-10': on two-core CPUs, simulation and render threads on different physical cores
- **What:** The runtime's placement hooks are opt-in and miss SMT on a 2-core part. GALAXY_SIMULATION_LARGEST_CACHE needs multiple L3 clusters; GALAXY_PIN_PERFORMANCE_CORES needs a hybrid CPU and 4 or more logical CPUs in the class. On an i7-6500U nothing is placed. Priorities in force: the process class is ABOVE_NORMAL. Simulation is HIGHEST plus MMCSS Games High; the broker is ABOVE_NORMAL plus MMCSS Games; the audio queue is MMCSS Pro Audio. Render, THP decode and Async Disc are all ABOVE_NORMAL (base 11); texture decode is NORMAL (10, 2 workers on 4 logical CPUs); PSO prewarm/compile/uber/disk writer and telemetry are BELOW_NORMAL (9). The Intel UMD and kernel threads and DWM share the same 4 logical CPUs.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:31865-31910, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:31912-32019, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:32027-32069, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:32297-32311, runtime/include/galaxy/windows_power.h:58-143, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7077-7090, runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:2452-2461, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:3360-3364, runtime/src/gx/pipeline_cache.cpp:2597-2629, runtime/src/gx/pipeline_cache.cpp:3302-3316, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:711-713, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:10480-10482
- **Why paid:** Placement was tuned on the 9950X3D (2 CCDs) and the 185H (hybrid), the two machines measured. The 2-core SMT case was never handled.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Two cores' paragraph (hypothesis)): Continuous; every co-scheduling of a busy thread on the simulation or render thread's sibling
- **Design:** When GetLogicalProcessorInformationEx(RelationProcessorCore) reports 2 or fewer physical cores with SMT: give the simulation thread ideal processor core0-LP0 and the render thread core1-LP0 (SetThreadIdealProcessorEx, soft, no hard mask). Confine texture decode, PSO, uber, disk writer, telemetry, controller-HID and Async Disc to the CPU set {core0-LP1, core1-LP1} with SetThreadSelectedCpuSets, which is soft enough to keep the scheduler free. During THP playback raise the THP decode thread above render, because the simulation thread waits on it. Log the effective layout. An A/B of GALAXY_RUNTIME_MMCSS=0 belongs here too: on 2 cores the realtime-band Games class can starve render, DWM and the driver whenever Windows migrates the simulation thread.
- **Risk** (medium): Ideal processors are hints. Wrong CPU-set masks on unusual topologies (VMs, disabled SMT) could serialize helpers. Gate on the topology being proved and log it. Texture decode confined to the siblings may lengthen scene-load hitches.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.2-1.2 ms/frame. Assume 5-15 % of the simulation thread's run time co-runs with a busy SMT sibling and loses 35-60 % speed there (Skylake SMT gives about 1.2-1.3x throughput for two threads). 12.9 ms * 0.05 * 0.35 = 0.23 ms; 12.9 ms * 0.15 * 0.6 = 1.16 ms. The render thread's gain is not counted. Unmeasured: needs a threads.csv on the 6500U.

### 16. T-LINK-ORDER: DLL code order follows shard size, not address or hotness

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: code size and layout (generated CMake). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--link-order (address | FILE)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md item 2 ('Hot code laid out together'); the size-sorted CMake order is a new detail
- **What:** The generated CMakeLists lists shards largest-first so Ninja starts the slowest compiles early. lld-link places objects in that same order, so neither guest-address locality nor hotness survives into the DLL. The 7.7 MB of hot code in 592 functions is spread over the whole roughly 148 MB image.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:11265-11281, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:10888-10990
- **Why paid:** The compile-scheduling order is reused, accidentally, as the link order.
- **Frequency** (measured; measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md (7.7 MB / 592 functions; 1.5x between CCDs) and docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native22 cache-size runs): Continuous (instruction fetch, iTLB, L2/L3). The same module needs 1.5x the CPU with a 32 MB L3 as with a 96 MB L3.
- **Design:** Decouple link order from compile order:
- Give each translated function __declspec(code_seg(".text$g<rank>")), since COFF grouped sections sort by suffix; or pass lld-link /order:@functions.order.
- The default rank is the guest address, which needs no profile.
- --link-order FILE takes a hot list (symbolized GALAXY_SIM_PROFILE rows, generated locally) and places the hot 7.7 MB first and contiguous.
- **Risk** (low): There is no behaviour change. Failure modes: link errors from colliding section names, or order-file names that do not match mangled symbols (lld warns and continues).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.6 ms/frame. The 6500U projection already carries a 1.5-2.5x cache factor on about 8 ms of game code (12.9 x 0.625, CPU_GAP_REPORT). Recovering 3-10% of game code through fewer iTLB/L2 misses gives 0.25 to 0.8 ms; address order alone gives 0 to 0.15 ms. Overall range 0.05 to 0.6 ms (est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms).

### 17. G-SMT-PLACEMENT: No sibling avoidance: simulation and render threads can share one Skylake core's L1/L2/TLB

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: SMT-shared caches. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_SMT_PLACEMENT (off = today)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:133 (siblings slow each other, hypothesis) and :169 ('on two-core CPUs, the simulation and render threads on different physical cores'); no design or check was given
- **What:** The only placement code is (a) pin_current_thread_to_performance_cores, which acts only on hybrid CPUs (best_class != worst_class) and is opt-in (GALAXY_PIN_PERFORMANCE_CORES), and (b) ScopedSimulationCacheAffinity, which acts only when there is a unique larger L3 cluster (GALAXY_SIMULATION_LARGEST_CACHE). On a homogeneous 2-core/4-thread i7-6500U neither applies. Nothing reads RelationProcessorCore or LTP_PC_SMT, and nothing reports which logical CPUs the two threads ran on. When the simulation thread (THREAD_PRIORITY_HIGHEST, MMCSS Games) and the render thread (ABOVE_NORMAL) co-run on siblings, they split issue width and share a 32 KB L1i, a 32 KB L1d, a 256 KB L2 and the STLB. Together with 7.7 MB of hot code, the render thread's caches and the driver, that slows both.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/windows_power.h:58-143, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:31893-31910, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:31915-32019, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7078-7086
- **Why paid:** Placement was built for the desktop (multi-CCD 9950X3D) and hybrid laptops (185H). The 2-core SMT case was never measured since Native10 (CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:131-135).
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Thread rates from CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:57; placement code read): Continuous. The fraction of each frame during which the two threads run on siblings is unknown; Windows prefers idle physical cores, but with 2 busy threads plus driver, audio, broker (420 wakes/s) and worker threads on 2 cores, co-scheduling on siblings is likely part of the time.
- **Design:** GALAXY_SMT_PLACEMENT=off|report|ideal|sets, default off. report: at each VI the simulation thread samples GetCurrentProcessorNumberEx, and once per chunk the render thread does the same. Both are mapped to cores via GetLogicalProcessorInformationEx(RelationProcessorCore), and '[smt-placement] sibling-frames=% same-lp=%' is printed in the 10 s sampler. This is the check, and it costs two cheap calls per frame. ideal: SetThreadIdealProcessorEx, simulation to core0 LP0 and render to core1 LP0 (a soft hint), applied only when SMT is present and there are 4 or fewer logical CPUs. sets: SetThreadSelectedCpuSets with simulation on {core0 LP0, LP1} and render on {core1 LP0, LP1}; workers (texture decode, PSO compile, broker) are left unrestricted.
- **Risk** (medium): With 'sets', a hard partition can strand a thread on a core that a driver DPC or a higher-priority thread is using, while the other core idles; that shows up as a frame-time spike. 'ideal' is only a hint and is low risk but may do little. It must not run on hybrid or multi-CCD hosts, where the existing policies apply.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-1.2 ms/frame. Formula: est_ms = f x slowdown x thread_ms. With sibling co-run fraction f = 0.05-0.2 of the frame and a per-thread slowdown of 0.3-0.5 while sharing (SMT yields about 1.2-1.3x combined throughput): sim 0.05 x 0.3 x 12.9 = 0.19 ms up to 0.2 x 0.5 x 12.9 = 1.3 ms, render 0.05-0.8 ms. Not all of it is avoidable, because 2 cores must also host the driver and workers, so I take 0.1-1.2 ms. The report mode turns f into a measured number.

### 18. R-BINDTABLES: Texture/sampler descriptor tables and three hash maps rebuilt and re-allocated every chunk

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: descriptor tables / texture cache lookups. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_PERSISTENT_BINDING_TABLES (0 = today's frame-local tables)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT item 6 'Rebuild constants and descriptor tables only when their inputs change'
- **What:** frame_texture_tables_, frame_texture_binding_tables_ and frame_texture_handle_cache_ (std::unordered_map) are cleared in every chunk. The first draw of each material per chunk then does two or three hash probes per sampled map and three node emplaces (heap allocations), allocates an 8-slot SRV ring table with CopyDescriptors, and calls sampler_table_for. That copies RenderConfig under an SRW lock and calls GetDescriptorHandleIncrementSize and Get*DescriptorHandleForHeapStart. Every binding-cache hit also calls touch_cached_handle per texture, which costs two more unordered_map lookups (entries_, decoded_lru_pos_) and a std::list splice.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:9928-9937, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:10531-10719, runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:1383-1402, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3696-3718, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/gx_backend.h:1490-1520
- **Why paid:** Texture tables point into the frame-local shader-visible SRV ring segment, so they cannot outlive the segment (gx_backend.cpp:10557-10560 comment). The decoded LRU is an exact list.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Code: clear at gx_backend.cpp:8297 per chunk; rebuild path at 10563-10712.): Per distinct material binding per chunk (about 5 chunks per frame, measured NATIVE_EXECUTION:287) for the rebuild. Per binding change for the touch. Material counts are not measured.
- **Design:** (1) Persistent descriptor tables. Reserve a non-ring region of the shader-visible heap keyed by TextureTableKey (resources plus SRV indices), LRU-evicted, and drop an entry when texture_cache_ retirement_revision shows a member SRV retired (that machinery already exists at gx_backend.cpp:8288-8296). Do the same for sampler tables keyed by the 8 sampler keys plus a config revision. (2) Replace the per-frame maps with open-addressed flat tables carrying a frame/epoch tag, so 'clear' is an epoch increment and costs no node allocations. (3) LRU by frame stamp: touch writes entry.last_frame (one store through decoded_srv_keys_[srv]) and eviction sorts by stamp instead of splicing a list.
- **Risk** (low): A persistent table that still references a retired SRV slot samples a wrong or freed texture (garbage texture, or a GPU fault on some drivers). The retirement-revision check must run before any table reuse. Behaviour is otherwise identical.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-0.5 ms/frame. ~1-2 us per rebuild on the 6500U (3 allocations, 4-6 hash probes, CopyDescriptors of 8, sampler lookup) x 100-250 rebuilds per frame across 5 chunks = 0.1-0.5 ms, plus touches. Inside the measured state share of 16 % (0.96-1.6 ms) and heap share of 3.4 % (0.2-0.34 ms) at 6-10 ms render thread (CPU_GAP_REPORT).

### 19. T-ABI-RELOAD: Every translated call reloads the whole function-wide 'used' register set afterwards

- **Status:** done (--abi-call-sync). Area: Translator: calls, returns, register residency. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--abi-trusting-registers`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'Calls are the largest single item' and plan item 3; TRANSLATOR_CPU_TASK work item 2
- **What:** At a Sync::Full call (every rmge01::fn_*, call_guest_indirect, call_guest_resolved, unknown helper) the residency pass reloads every local the function uses anywhere (reload_sequence over the function-wide `used` array), including callee-saved r1/r2/r13/r14-r31, LR, CTR and dead volatiles (r0, r5-r12). In a synthetic CodeWarrior caller lowered with the release options (stwu/mflr/savegpr, bctrl, bl, restore, blr; 2 calls in 23 guest instructions) each call reloaded 9 registers (r0,r1,r3,r4,r12,r30,r31,lr,ctr); clang -O2 kept 8 reloads per call (it only drops the provably dead r4). Under the PowerPC EABI only r3 (and r4/f1/f2) carry anything back; here even r3 was dead after the bctrl. CPU_GAP_REPORT counts 11 reloads with stack spills at a bctrl in NameObjCategoryList::execute.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:620-628, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:935-941, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:226-241, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7106-7124, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5988-6046
- **Why paid:** The pass is textual and ABI-agnostic: an unknown callee may write any register, the flat transfers (OSInit) and the old exception-unwind resume needed the full register file in context, and no liveness is computed (guest_cfg_liveness.rs and guest_abi_effect.rs are documented as inert).
- **Frequency** (derived; CPU_GAP_REPORT instruction mix and call census; reload count from compiling the residency output of a synthetic caller in a scratch copy of the crate): Once per translated or indirect call: calls are 8.0 % of hot guest instructions (static mix weighted by samples); about half of the ~8 x86 instructions per guest instruction sit at calls and returns. 8 reloads per call (derived, clang 18 -O2 of the synthetic body).
- **Design:** --abi-trusting-registers, reload half: for calls to translated callees not on the opt-out list, reload only r3 and r4 (and f1/f2 under float residency) and only if used after the call; never reload r1, r2, r13, r14-r31 (callee-saved), LR, CTR, XER, r0, r5-r12. Build the opt-out set from guest_abi_effect/guest_cfg_liveness (functions that read a volatile after a call, read scratch registers at entry, or may read a caller's non-volatile: the 4/14/76 census) and keep Sync::Full for calls to them, for call_guest_resolved/host targets, RSO/Home sidecar code and the four OSInit flat transfers. The resident_entry_* interior stubs keep reloading everything (they are the resume path).
- **Risk** (medium): A callee that does not restore a non-volatile (hand-written asm, exception vectors, OSSaveContext/OSLoadContext, setjmp/longjmp) leaves the caller with a stale local: wrong values with no crash. A flat transfer or flag-transfer resume that re-enters a frame through a call_return_* continuation must still reload from context (unchanged stubs). Mitigation: per-function opt-out from the static census, emitted into the manifest; differential harnesses plus gameplay, Home menu, boot and save routes.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.22-0.95 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G, G = 0.625 x sim_ms_6500U = 0.625 x 8.9 / 12.9 / 16.9 = 5.56 / 8.06 / 10.56 ms. In the compiled synthetic caller, call-dense like the hot mix (8.7 % calls), about 15 of ~150 hot-path x86 instructions are post-call reloads the ABI makes unnecessary (10 %). They are L1 hits, so time weight is taken as 0.4-0.9: f = 4-9 %. Low 0.04 x 5.56 = 0.22; high 0.09 x 10.56 = 0.95; mid about 0.5.

### 20. T-RESIDENCY-FALLBACK: About 38 % of functions fall back to non-resident lowering (every register access through context)

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: register residency coverage. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--residency-census (report), --residency-split-statements (fix)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** apply_register_residency returns None, so the body keeps context->gpr[N] for every operand, when it sees a runtime-indexed register access, a synchronizing call used as an expression, two synchronizing calls on one line, a non-literal float register argument, a body with no register access, or a result over MAX_RESIDENT_BODY_BYTES (384 KiB). That cap is hit by large functions because every call site duplicates a full store/reload sequence. At F5 time 25,937 of 41,941 functions were resident. The hot-sample share of the rest is unknown, and the translator records no per-function reason.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:38-41, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:845-880, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:1014-1025, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3444-3461
- **Why paid:** The pass works on emitted text and refuses any shape it cannot rewrite safely. The size cap keeps shards inside the 512 KiB source budget.
- **Frequency** (measured; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md F5 row (25,937 of 41,941 resident); CPU_GAP_REPORT 1.3 stores/loads per guest instruction in resident hot code): Every instruction of a non-resident function: about 2-3 context memory operations per guest instruction instead of the resident average 1.3 + 1.3.
- **Design:** (1) Make the translator write a residency census into the module manifest: reason code and body size per function. Join it with the exact profile on the Windows side to get the hot share. (2) Fix the top reasons. Split two synchronizing calls on one line into two statements. Lift a synchronizing call in expression position into a temporary statement. Accept non-literal float indices by synchronizing that helper fully. (3) With T-ABI-*, per-site sequences shrink to 1-3 statements, so very few bodies hit the 384 KiB cap; also share the full-sync store and reload text through one cold block per function where possible.
- **Risk** (low): Each newly accepted shape is the same transform as today's; a mistake shows as a miscompiled function, which NEBULA_RESIDENCY_ADDRESS_RANGE bisects. A larger body can push a shard over its source budget (handled by the existing shard sizing).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.53 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G. If 5-15 % of game-code samples run in non-resident bodies (unknown), and residency saves 10-35 % of their time (the integer-logic gain seen at F5), f = 0.5-5 %: 0.005 x 5.56 = 0.03; 0.05 x 10.56 = 0.53.

### 21. R-VTXSPEC: Per-vertex interpreter in the CPU vertex decoder: branch per attribute per vertex

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: CPU vertex decode. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_VERTEX_DECODER_SPECIALIZE (0 = generic decoder)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** decode_vertex_stream loops over vertices and, for each one, re-tests every attribute's VCD type (direct/index8/index16/none) and format. It also re-resolves indexed arrays through IndexedArrayResolver::resolve (range check plus record_read plus a dependency-sink test), re-zeroes absent channels for all 8 texcoords, and assembles a 144-byte struct before a memcpy to the ring. The layout is constant per draw/run (VertexLayoutCacheEntry), but nothing is specialized on it.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/vertex_loader.cpp:755-1019, runtime/src/gx/vertex_loader.cpp:110-145, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:10801-10831
- **Why paid:** One generic decoder handles any VCD/VAT combination (GRAPHICS_ARCHITECTURE: one canonical vertex, 'branch-free per attribute' was the goal, not reached).
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT: CPU vertex decode ~8 % of render-thread running samples): Per decoded vertex on decoded-cache misses and on non-cached draws (on_draw path, prepared and simple runs).
- **Design:** Specialize at layout time. Compute a 32-bit layout signature (per-attribute vcd plus format plus count plus dequant) in cached_vertex_layout and select a decoder function pointer from a table of template instantiations for the 8-16 most common SMG layouts (census them once with a GALAXY_GX_VTX_LAYOUT_CENSUS counter). Fall back to the generic loop otherwise. Resolve each indexed attribute's array base pointer once per draw (array_resolver already caches the range; hoist it to a raw pointer plus a bound). Write absent texcoords once, via a prefilled template vertex.
- **Risk** (low): A template disagreeing with the generic decoder produces wrong vertices for that layout. A test can compare every specialization byte for byte against the generic decoder (vertex_loader_tests.cpp exists).
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.15-0.4 ms/frame. Measured 8 % of the render thread (CPU_GAP_REPORT) is 0.48-0.8 ms (0.08 x 6..10 ms). Removing 30-50 % of it (typical for specializing an attribute interpreter, hypothesis) gives 0.15-0.4 ms.

### 22. R-JAUDIO-MIX-SIMD: Host JAudio mixer runs scalar int64 per-sample code on the simulation thread

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: audio. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_JAUDIO_MIX_SIMD (default 0 = scalar)`. Already listed: False Mixer cost measured in NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native14, but no optimization is listed
- **What:** DSPSendCommands2 is a host call, so each release of channel group 3 mixes one 80-sample subframe inline on the simulation thread (run_jaudio_host_command, then release_halt, then mix_subframe). Per active channel the work is AFC/PCM decode, a 4-tap resampler, FIR/IIR, then 6 bus ramp_mix passes (mix_buses) or 8 (mix_auto). Every passes runs per sample in int64 with sat16/rounded_mid clamps, and record fields go through be16/put16 byte swaps on each access. release_halt also takes two steady_clock::now() reads per subframe for the mix_ns statistic.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:23831-23857, runtime/src/jaudio_host_mixer.cpp:198-219, runtime/src/jaudio_host_mixer.cpp:221-302, runtime/src/jaudio_host_mixer.cpp:393-420, runtime/src/jaudio_host_mixer.cpp:841-922, runtime/src/jaudio_host_mixer.cpp:926-966
- **Why paid:** Native14 replaced the DSP coprocessor with a host restatement of the RMGE01 DSP program. It had to be bit-exact against the DSP: rounding at bit 16, 40-bit accumulators, saturation. The scalar int64 form was the direct restatement, and the result must exist before AUDIOMSG_DSP is posted to JASAudioThread.
- **Frequency** (derived; kSamples=80 at jaudio_host_mixer.cpp:11 and the 32 kHz AI rate; measured mix time 247 ms over 88 s (docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native14 table)): 400 subframes/s (32 kHz / 80 samples), times the active channels in each
- **Design:** Vectorize the three kernels that dominate with SSE2/SSSE3, keeping the scalar path as the reference. (1) ramp_mix: gain = mid_of(volume) is int16 and (gain*src)>>16 is exactly pmulhw, so dst = paddsw(dst, pmulhw(gainvec, src)). The per-pair volume step becomes a precomputed gain vector, which handles 8 samples per few instructions. (2) add_scaled: pmullw/pmulhw with a half-to-even fix for bit-16 ties. (3) resample: pmaddwd with a 64-bit final add, plus a scalar fallback when a tap is -32768. Read each record's hot fields once into locals instead of calling be16 per access. Skip a bus whose dirty flag shows it stayed zero. Keep the two steady_clock reads only when the [jaudio] diagnostics are on. Bit-exactness is checked by the existing galaxy_jaudio_mixer_tests plus a randomized differential test of the scalar against the SIMD kernels. Expected removal is 50-80 % of the mixer time.
- **Risk** (low): An off-by-one in tie rounding, or saturation order differing from the DSP, changes the audio by about 1 LSB. The differential test catches it. No timing or protocol change.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.17-0.37 ms/frame. Measured 0.28 % of a 9950X3D core (docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native14: 247 ms in 88 s). As a share of today's simulation thread (12.7-14.5 % of a core, CPU_GAP_REPORT): 0.28/14.5 = 1.93 % to 0.28/12.7 = 2.2 %. Formula: est_ms = share_of_thread * thread_ms_6500U, giving 1.93 % * 8.9 = 0.17 ms and 2.2 % * 16.9 = 0.37 ms (midpoint 2.06 % * 12.9 = 0.27 ms). This is the current cost. The SIMD saving of 50-80 % of it is a hypothesis.

### 23. T-FTYPED: Scalar guest floats live in PpcContext: every result is stored and every operand reloaded after aliasing stores, calls and loop back-edges

- **Status:** done (residency + --abi-* + --single-float-arithmetic). Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--float-typed-locals`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'Later 7-10: Float registers as typed locals'; TRANSLATOR_CPU_TASK.md:91; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:458-461 and :691 (F5 measured slower)
- **What:** Without float residency (release: guest_resident_float off), every scalar float instruction reads its operands from context->fpr_bits[N] and writes its result back: an 8-byte store per result (9-byte vmovsd with disp32). fmr/fneg/fabs are 64-bit integer load/xor/store pairs on the context, not register renames. Translated stores go through std::byte* under -fno-strict-aliasing, so any guest store between two float instructions makes clang reload every float operand from the context. Measured in a scratchpad clang-18 probe at module flags: a 3-element Vec scale loop (lfs; fmuls f0,f0,f1; stfs) reloads loop-invariant f1 and recomputes its multiplier rounding on every element. It runs 66 hot instructions per iteration, against 42 with typed double locals. A 6-instruction lfs/lfs/fmuls/fmadds/fadds/stfs block runs 56 hot instructions, against 14 with float locals and no write-back.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4481-4495, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4532-4564, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4596-4625, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:241-245, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:805-854, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:880-887, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:610-628, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:665-686, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:935-939, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:458-461
- **Why paid:** The register file is the architectural PpcContext. The fast helpers take (context, index) and write context->fpr_bits so that any callee, exception or OSSaveContext sees the current value. The existing residency pass (guest_resident_float, fpr[32]/ps1[32] locals with _r helpers) was measured slower: 21.1 % against 20.7 % of a core under fast float (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:458-461). At entry it loads every used float slot. At exit and before every full synchronization point (every call) it stores every written slot, and after each call it reloads every used slot (residency.rs:610-628, 935-939). That cost is set by calls (8 % of hot instructions), not by the floats themselves.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Instruction mix from CPU_GAP_REPORT (measured); the reload fraction is a hypothesis; per-op counts derived from the scratchpad probe disassembly): Every float result and operand: float loads 6.3 % + float arithmetic 3.6 % + float stores 3.2 % of hot guest instructions (measured mix). Estimated about 0.24 extra context stores and 0.1 extra context reloads per guest instruction on average (hypothesis: about 50 % of operands need a reload).
- **Design:** Flag --float-typed-locals, built on the existing residency pass. (1) Declare each used FPR as a `double fN` local (bit_cast at sync points only), so fmr becomes a copy and fneg/fabs become vxorpd/vandpd. (2) Replace store-all/reload-all with liveness: guest_cfg_liveness.rs already carries an fpr set; store only slots that are written and live-out, and reload only slots read later. (3) With --abi-trusting-registers, a call stores only the argument registers f1-f8 written since the last sync and reloads only f1 (its ps1 too if read). Volatile f0 and f2-f13 are dead after the call. f14-f31 are never stored or reloaded, because the callee's stfd/psq_st save and lfd/psq_l restore keeps them. Exit stores f1 only. (4) Keep full synchronization for the ABI opt-out list (exception vectors, OSSaveContext/OSLoadContext, the __OSFPUContext save/load at 0x804A3604/0x804A34E0, the 4 flat transfers) and at interior entries. A cheaper first step is liveness-limited sync without ABI trust; it is A/B-able alone.
- **Risk** (medium): Two failure modes. First, a callee that reads an FPR outside the CodeWarrior ABI (OSSaveContext, the eager-FPU save at 0x804A3604, exception and interrupt entry, any hand-written assembly) sees a stale context value. A thread switch or exception then restores corrupted float state: wrong physics or camera, not a crash. Second, without ABI trust the existing measurement says it can be net slower. It needs the opt-out list from the GPR ABI work plus the differential harnesses (diff_sdkmath, diff_tvec3), frame dumps and a route A/B. Stack save slots of f14-f31 may then hold stale values, the same accepted policy as the GPR ABI plan.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.17-0.85 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 (game-code share of sim thread, measured CPU_GAP_REPORT) x f x T_sim(6500U), with T_sim 8.9 (low) / 16.9 (high) ms (CPU_GAP_REPORT projection, hypothesis). f = 3-8 % of game-code time. Derivation: (0.24 stores + 0.11 reloads + about 0.05 fmr/fneg moves) of about 0.4 x86 per guest instruction against about 8 x86 per guest instruction gives about 5 % of instructions. The reloads add store-forwarding latency (about 4-5 cycles) on float dependency chains, and code shrinks (9-byte vmovsd stores) on a 4 MB L3. The probe shows 36 % fewer instructions per loop element. Low = 0.625*0.03*8.9 = 0.17 ms; high = 0.625*0.08*16.9 = 0.85 ms.

### 24. G-UBER: Hybrid uber fallback on first-seen PSO keys, with a single below-normal compile worker on 4 logical CPUs

- **Status:** open. Area: HD 520 GPU frame time / shaders. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_IGPU_PSO_WORKER_BOOST (default 0); ship GALAXY_SHADER_SEED_DIRECTORY`. Already listed: True docs/GRAPHICS_ARCHITECTURE.md:116-118 (uber 2-4x on iGPUs); not in any performance plan
- **What:** In hybrid mode (GALAXY_GX_UBER=1, the default), a PsoKey without a published specialization draws with the uber pipeline while a worker compiles it. GRAPHICS_ARCHITECTURE.md:116-118 states that uber costs 2-4x fragment time on iGPUs (a 16-iteration dynamic TEV loop). On a 4-logical-CPU machine the cache gets 1 compile worker (gx_backend.cpp:3362-3364), and the uber blob thread runs at BELOW_NORMAL. Both compete with the simulation, render, decode and audio threads, so uber windows last longer: FXC level-1 misses were measured at 20-163 ms on a laptop (pipeline_cache.cpp:1000-1003).
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/pipeline_cache.cpp:698-719, runtime/src/gx/pipeline_cache.cpp:900-955, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:3360-3370, docs/GRAPHICS_ARCHITECTURE.md:108-122, installer/runtime-env.json:107-110
- **Why paid:** Avoiding stutter: specialized-only compiles synchronously on a miss (tens of ms) or skips geometry.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; No uber-resolution count from a laptop run; [uber-pipeline] resolutions and sync-misses are printed at shutdown (pipeline_cache.cpp:790-798).): Per new PsoKey until its specialization publishes. Bursty at galaxy load and effect onset; about 0 in steady state with a warm cache (GALAXY_PSO_PREWARM=1, limit 4096, blocking 30 s).
- **Design:** Ship a seeded cache (GALAXY_SHADER_SEED_DIRECTORY exists, pipeline_cache.cpp:721-723) built from a full-route trace with the iGPU preset's keys, so first runs rarely miss. On iGPU, raise the compile worker to NORMAL while any uber draw occurred in the last frame. Keep hybrid; specialized-only after prewarm (GALAXY_GX_UBER=0) would trade GPU time for 20-163 ms CPU hitches.
- **Risk** (low): A seed built for another driver version is invalidated by the LUID/driver check (only time lost). The priority boost briefly takes CPU from the simulation during bursts.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0-0.5 ms/frame. Average GPU ms per frame: in steady state about 0. In a burst, uber draws covering 20-50 % of the frame's fill at 2-4x add 1-6 ms GPU for the 1-60 frames a miss takes to compile. Averaged over play: 0-0.5 ms.

### 25. G-VTX144: Fixed 144-byte decoded vertex fetched by the GPU from an UPLOAD heap over the shared memory bus

- **Status:** open. Area: HD 520 vertex fetch / UMA bandwidth. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_COMPACT_VERTEX (default 0)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:129 (F12, compact per-layout vertex format, not started); this entry adds the GPU/UMA side
- **What:** Every decoded vertex is a 144-byte GxVertexOut: position, normal, tangent, binormal, 2 colors, 8 UV pairs, matrix indices and padding, whatever the layout uses. The vertex ring and the resident decoded-vertex arena are D3D12_HEAP_TYPE_UPLOAD (CPU write-combined system memory) that the HD 520 reads in place. The recorded peak is 117,361 vertices per frame, which is 16.1-16.9 MB written by the render thread and read by the GPU per frame, on DRAM the CPU also uses.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/gx/vertex_loader.h:44-57, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3103-3115, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:2758, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5273-5319, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:11547-11567
- **Why paid:** One canonical vertex keeps every VCD/VAT to a single input layout and PSO key.
- **Frequency** (measured; renderer_d3d12.cpp:3105-3108 comment citing fifo-draw-vertices peak 117,361 in the retained recordings): Per vertex per frame; peak 117,361 per frame.
- **Design:** The compact per-layout vertex format (F12): 32-48 B per vertex, with position f32x3, normal as snorm 10:10:10:2, colors unorm8, only the UVs present as f16x2 or f32 when needed, and packed matrix indices. Input-layout variants are keyed in VertexShaderKey. On UMA this cuts GPU fetch and CPU write bandwidth by 3-4x.
- **Risk** (medium): Precision: half-float UVs on large texture repeats, and normal quantization in lighting. More PSO variants. Needs the vertex_loader_tests oracle extended.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.2-0.8 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame: 40k-117k vertices x 144 B = 5.8-16.9 MB, read at 15-25 GB/s effective (single- or dual-channel DDR4 shared) = 0.23-1.1 ms of memory time, partly hidden behind shading, so 0.2-0.8 ms exposed. A 48 B format removes about two thirds. The CPU write side belongs to the render-thread vertex-decode item (8 %).

### 26. G-JCC-ERRATUM: Skylake JCC-erratum microcode evicts branchy generated code from the decoded-uop cache; no branch alignment flag is used

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake front end: DSB (decoded ICache). Thread: all. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--align-branches-32b (translator); GALAXY_JCC_ALIGN CMake option (runtime build)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** With the 2019+ microcode, Skylake-family CPUs (including the i7-6500U) do not cache in the DSB any 32-byte chunk in which a jump or a macro-fused jcc crosses or ends on a 32-byte boundary, so those chunks go through the legacy decoder. The generated module is compiled with clang-cl using -mavx2 -mfma -mmovbe and no -mbranches-within-32B-boundaries. The runtime (CMakeLists.txt) has neither that flag nor /QIntel-jcc-erratum. Translated code is branch-dense: guest branches are 8.9 % of instructions, plus checkpoint, flat-view and dispatch tests.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:11642-11692, CMakeLists.txt:76-106
- **Why paid:** The build flags were chosen on Zen 5 (9950X3D), which has no such erratum.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Intel JCC erratum white paper (0-4 % typical impact, recoverable by compiler alignment); branch density from CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:89): Every executed 32-byte chunk containing a boundary-crossing branch. This matters for loops and hot functions that fit the 1.5 k-uop DSB. Code that already misses L1i is decoded legacy anyway.
- **Design:** Translator flag --align-branches-32b, which emits target_compile_options(RMGE01_game PRIVATE /clang:-mbranches-within-32B-boundaries) into the generated CMakeLists.
