Runtime CMake option GALAXY_JCC_ALIGN: -mbranches-within-32B-boundaries under clang-cl, /QIntel-jcc-erratum under MSVC. Build a Skylake-targeted module variant and A/B it. Measure IDQ.DSB_UOPS against IDQ.MITE_UOPS on Skylake.
- **Risk** (low): Padding grows code by roughly 1-3 %, which adds i-cache and iTLB pressure on an already footprint-bound module, so the net effect could be negative. It must be an A/B-gated variant and must not become the default without a Skylake measurement. No behaviour risk.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0-0.5 ms/frame. Formula: est_ms = share x thread_ms. Assumed recoverable share 0-2 % of the simulation thread (12.9 ms): 0-0.26 ms. Plus 0-3 % of the render thread's own code (render minus the ~11.5 % driver share: 0.885 x 8 ms = 7.1 ms): 0-0.21 ms. Total 0-0.5 ms.

### 27. R-VSCONST: Per-object VS constant rebuild (204 matrix words) and full 1,280-byte constant upload

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: per-draw GX->D3D state (constants). Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_SPLIT_VS_CONSTANTS (0 = today's single 1,280-byte block)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT item 6 'Rebuild constants and descriptor tables only when their inputs change' (generic; this specific split is not described)
- **What:** A matrix-only flush (the per-object LOAD_INDX case) still runs refresh_vs_inline_matrices. That gathers the position matrix plus all 8 texture and 8 post-texture matrices (12+96+96 words through state.xf()) whatever num_texgens is, then allocates and memcpys a whole 1,280-byte GxVsConstants block and changes the root CBV (one more SetGraphicsRootConstantBufferView per object, which also breaks draw batching).
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:10463-10487, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/vs_constants.h:62-97, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/vs_constants.h:194-213, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/uber_constants.h:151, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7433-7437
- **Why paid:** The VS reads inline matrices from one constant block. The CPU cache (VsConstantsCpuCache) avoids re-deriving material fields, but the upload must be a complete immutable block because recorded draws reference earlier blocks.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; gx_backend.cpp:10463-10466 triggers on inline_matrices_stale_ (set by any kDirtyXfMatrices). Per-object LOAD_INDX noted at gx_backend.cpp:10522.): Once per object with a matrix change (same population as R-PALETTE). Hundreds to thousands per frame.
- **Design:** (1) Refresh only the active texgens (vs_key_.num_texgens) and skip inline_pos_matrix when per-vertex PNMTXIDX is used. (2) Split the constants into a per-material CBV (projection, colors, texgen config: rebuilt on vs_c/xf/vcd dirty only) and a per-object CBV of 48 B + 48 B x active texgens (usually 96-144 B, one 256-byte ring slot) on a new root slot. A matrix-only flush then writes 256 B instead of 1,280 B and leaves the material CBV bound.
- **Risk** (low): Root-signature and VS change (re-key caches). If a field is put in the wrong block, the transform is wrong for the draws that use the field, which is visible immediately. Inactive texgen matrices are never read by the generated VS, but the uber VS must use the same num_texgens bound.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.08-0.4 ms/frame. ~0.15-0.3 us saved per object (204 scattered reads plus 1 KB less WC write plus one fewer root CBV call) x 500-1,500 objects/frame = 0.08-0.45 ms. Bounded by the measured state share of 16 % x 6..10 ms = 0.96-1.6 ms (CPU_GAP_REPORT).

### 28. T-LEAF-CALLS: Calls to tiny translated leaves pay the full call machinery (and full sync, because guest_resident_leaf is off)

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: calls, inlining. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--leaf-contracts (step 1), --inline-small-leaves=8 (step 2)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'Later: inlining small leaf functions across shards (translator or ThinLTO)'; leaf contracts for translated leaves are new
- **What:** A bl to a translated checkpoint-free leaf (getter, setter, flag test) drops only the call-return checkpoint. The site still stores the whole written set, stores pc and LR, makes a cross-shard C++ call (host prologue, entry pc test, flat_base, residency entry loads, exit stores) and reloads the whole used set. Callee contracts are used only for the hand-listed lean bodies (lean_call_contract) and 10 audited wrappers. The WiiCompiled-style leaf-contract emitters (lower_resident_integer_leaf / lower_resident_integer_direct_calls) are limited to addi/ori/lwz-r1 shapes and disabled in the release options (guest_resident_leaf not set).
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1332-1368, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7515-7532, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7126-7251, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1990-2074, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:702-799, crates/nebula-recomp-cli/src/generate.rs:56-84
- **Why paid:** Each guest function is its own C++ function in a 64-function shard without LTO, and the residency pass knows a callee's effects only for lean host bodies.
- **Frequency** (measured; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md 'Leaf calls' row (static); dynamic share is a hypothesis): 21,006 static call sites target checkpoint-free leaves (call-return sites 304,463 to 283,457). Dynamic share of calls is unknown; leaves are small and frequently called.
- **Design:** Step 1 (low risk): compute a GuestAbiContract for every checkpoint-free leaf with decode_guest_ppc_function + analyze_guest_abi, and map it to a LeanCallContract-style Sync, so the site stores only the leaf's read set and reloads only its written set. Step 2: inline leaves of 8 guest instructions or fewer that have no interior entries, are not withheld from direct dispatch and are not hooked: lower the callee's words in place with renamed labels, so residency sees plain register operations and the call, prologue and sync disappear. The callee keeps its own fn_* for indirect calls and records.
- **Risk** (medium): Step 2 grows code at hot sites (cap the size). The profiler attributes the leaf's samples to its caller. Fastmem fault sites inside an inlined leaf report the caller's function. A runtime intercept added later for an inlined leaf would be bypassed: build the exclusion from requires_host_guest_call, direct_dispatch_target_requires_host and the hook lists.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.11-0.85 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G. A leaf call costs ~50-60 x86 instructions of machinery around a 2-8 instruction body (synthetic body). If 10-25 % of dynamic calls go to such leaves, and calls carry ~half of game-code instructions, inlining removes 2-8 % of game-code time: 0.02 x 5.56 = 0.11; 0.08 x 10.56 = 0.85.

### 29. R-CHUNKFRAME: Every streamed FIFO chunk is a full renderer frame: slot fence wait, list reset, caches cleared

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: D3D12 submission / frame structure. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_SEGMENT_PER_GAME_FRAME (0 = today's per-chunk frame)`. Already listed: False Related only: PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11 P1.5 step 3 (frames in flight 2 to 3)
- **What:** render_frame_on_thread calls renderer_.begin_frame for every chunk. That waits on fence_values_[frame_slot_] with kFramesInFlight=2, so chunk n waits for the GPU to finish chunk n-2 mid-frame. It also resets the allocator and command list, maps and unmaps the timestamp readback, rebinds heaps, root signature and RTs, and rewinds all rings. The backend then clears the pipeline memo, all frame texture-binding/table/handle maps and the binding key, so every material's descriptor tables and constants are rebuilt in each chunk. end_frame does Close + ExecuteCommandLists + Signal per chunk.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:8250-8306, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5567-5605, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5642-5760, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:6286-6345, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/renderer_d3d12.h:60-63
- **Why paid:** Streamed submission (P1.5 B, GALAXY_GX_FIFO_STREAM_KB=32) was layered onto the per-frame renderer, so a chunk inherits the frame-slot model. PE-at-consumption needs a submission per chunk, but not a new ring segment or cache epoch.
- **Frequency** (measured; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:287 'about 5 chunks of 38 KB per frame'): Once per render chunk, about 5 per game frame (measured, NATIVE_EXECUTION:287), so about 4 extra frame setups/teardowns per frame.
- **Design:** Use the policy Interp mode already has (ring_advance_pending_: one ring segment per game frame) in Normal mode. Advance the ring segment, clear the frame binding maps and reset the pipeline memo only at the game-frame boundary (XFB copy/present chunk). Per chunk, Close + ExecuteCommandLists + Signal from a small allocator pool (e.g. 8 allocators, each with its own fence) and skip the 2-back slot wait. Re-issue only SetDescriptorHeaps/root signature/RT and the current bindings after Reset. This keeps PE-at-consumption semantics (submission per chunk) unchanged.
- **Risk** (medium): Rings must hold a whole game frame rather than one chunk. The defaults are already sized per frame (64/16/8/32 MB) and exhaustion is fatal and loud, so check peak_bytes_per_frame first. An allocator reused before its fence completes corrupts command memory and causes device removal, so each pooled allocator needs its own fence. Descriptor tables that live across chunks must not be overwritten by a later chunk in the same segment.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.15-0.8 ms/frame. 4 extra submissions x ~30-80 us driver CPU (Close/Execute/Signal/Reset and rebinds; the Intel driver is heavier than the measured NVIDIA 11.5 %) is 0.12-0.32 ms. Re-building material tables and constants for materials re-seen across chunks adds ~0.05-0.3 ms. Mid-frame GPU-paced slot waits on the HD 520 add more but are wait time. Total 0.15-0.8 ms of the 6-10 ms render thread.

### 30. T-HOTCOLD-SPLIT: Cold regions inside hot functions are neither marked nor outlined

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: code size and layout. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--hot-cold-split`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Every hot function contains code that is cold in steady state:
- the shared call-return block and budgeted-branch policy calls;
- resident_entry stubs and the pc-dispatch switch;
- store and float slow paths, and fault defaults.
There is no cold attribute anywhere (only GALAXY_NOINLINE), so LLVM merely moves these blocks to the function tail. Hot functions span more pages than their hot path needs.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:33-43, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:990-1000, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3549-3572, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:6232-6262, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:175-187
- **Why paid:** Footprint never mattered on the 96 MB-L3 desktop.
- **Frequency** (derived; derived from the generated text, e.g. the asm-probe call_direct source in the scratchpad (shared_call_return__ block)): Continuous: every non-trivial translated function contains such regions.
- **Design:** - Add GALAXY_COLD (__attribute__((cold))) to checked_store_slow, guest_store_slow, guest_load_slow, call_return_checkpoint_counted, branch_checkpoint_taken_counted/slow, call_guest_indirect_boundary and guest_execution_fault.
- Emit a cold marker call at the head of each resident_entry stub and of the shared call-return block.
- Compile with -mllvm -hot-cold-split=true so LLVM outlines these regions into .cold functions.
- Combine with T-LINK-ORDER so the outlined functions sort after the hot set.
- **Risk** (low): This is a performance-only change. It could regress if a path marked cold is actually warm (e.g. checkpoint blocks under adaptive cadence). Profiles and stack traces gain .cold fragments.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.4 ms/frame. If cold bytes are 15-35% of hot-function size, the 7.7 MB hot footprint (measured, CPU_GAP_REPORT) drops to about 5-6.5 MB against a 4 MB L3. This is weighted like T-LINK-ORDER on the roughly 8 ms of cache-sensitive game code: 0.6-5% gives 0.05 to 0.4 ms (est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms).

### 31. R-PALETTE: Whole 6,656-byte XF matrix palette copied into the matrix ring for every lit or skinned object

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: per-draw GX->D3D state (constants). Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_PALETTE_CHUNKED (0 = today's whole-palette copy)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Every flush where the palette is required and any palette chunk is dirty allocates a fresh 6,656-byte matrix-ring block and memcpys all of XF 0x000-0x67F into it. The palette is required by lighting (vs_key flags bit 1), per-vertex PNMTXIDX, emboss, or a texmtx index. SMG re-uploads matrices per object via LOAD_INDX (a position and a normal matrix are about 21 words), so most lit objects pay the full copy into write-combined upload memory and pollute the 4 MB L3. The 32 MB/frame default matrix ring (about 5,000 palettes) shows how many are expected.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:10380-10457, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/gx_state.h:88-96, runtime/src/gx/shader_gen.cpp:852-881, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3153-3157
- **Why paid:** A ring allocation has no inherited contents, and draws already recorded reference older blocks, so a partial patch is impossible. The shader indexes one flat StructuredBuffer (mtx_palette[base + w>>2]) for normal matrices (0x400), light objects (0x600) and PNMTX rows.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Code path gx_backend.cpp:10411-10450; ring sizing renderer_d3d12.cpp:3157; LOAD_INDX per object noted at gx_backend.cpp:10522-10524.): Once per object whose matrices changed and whose draw needs the palette. Hundreds to a few thousand per frame (not counted; stat_palette_uploads_ exists but is not reported in the docs).
- **Design:** Chunked palette. Keep the existing 52 x 128-byte dirty chunks (gx_state.h:94-96) and upload only dirty chunks into the ring. Pass a 52-entry chunk-base table (13 uint4) in the VS constants, and have the shader read mtx_palette[chunk_base[e>>3] + (e&7)]. Steady state per object is then 1-2 chunks (128-256 B) plus a 208-byte table instead of 6.6 KB. A simpler variant: three root SRVs (rows 0x000-0x0FF, normals 0x400-0x45F, post and lights 0x500-0x67F), re-uploading only the regions whose chunks are dirty.
- **Risk** (medium): Changes the VS and uber VS and adds a VS-constant field, so the pipeline and shader caches must be re-keyed. A wrong chunk index gives wrong transforms or lighting on every lit draw, which is very visible and caught by frame dumps. Ring exhaustion risk drops.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-0.8 ms/frame. 6.6 KB to WC upload memory at ~5-10 GB/s on a dual-channel 6500U shared with the HD 520 is ~0.7-1.3 us per copy, plus L3 eviction. 300-1,500 copies/frame (hypothesis) saves ~0.1-0.8 ms after the chunked copy. It must fit inside the measured 'GX->D3D state per draw ~16 %' = 0.96-1.6 ms (0.16 x 6..10 ms).

### 32. R-DIRTYSCAN: Per-chunk invalidation scans walk every cache entry instead of the dirty ranges

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: cache invalidation (decoded vertices, textures, binding caches). Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_DIRTY_INTERVAL_INDEX (0 = today's per-entry scans)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Each chunk runs three scans. (1) invalidate_dirty_decoded_packet_run_ranges iterates every decoded_packet_run_cache_ entry and each of its dependencies against the dirty list (sorted mode ships on, page filter off). (2) TextureCache::invalidate_guest_ranges iterates every decoded texture in entries_ whenever the whole-cache envelope overlaps any dirty range, which is almost always. (3) After any eviction, invalidate_texture_binding_cache_range runs once per dirty range over texture_handle_cache_, frame_texture_handle_cache_ and frame_texture_binding_tables_, which is O(R x H). All three are unordered_map walks, which are cache-hostile on a 4 MB L3.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5414-5500, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5006-5030, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:9985-10033, runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:2027-2121, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:8333-8365
- **Why paid:** Each cache is keyed by content identity rather than by address, so overlap is tested entry by entry.
- **Frequency** (derived; Call sites gx_backend.cpp:8346 and 8363 run per render_frame_on_thread call): Once per render chunk (~5 per frame, measured NATIVE_EXECUTION:287) for (1) and (2); (3) per chunk with a texture eviction. Entry counts are not measured.
- **Design:** Keep an address-sorted interval index (a vector of [begin,end) to slot, rebuilt lazily on insert or evict, or a 4 KB-page to entry-list map like display_list_cache_page_map_) for decoded textures, decoded packet runs and texture-handle keys, and walk the R dirty ranges against it: O(R log N) instead of O(N log R). Coalesce dirty ranges once per game frame where possible. Turn on the existing GALAXY_GX_VERTEX_DEPENDENCY_PAGE_FILTER as a cheap first step.
- **Risk** (low): Pure data-structure change with identical half-open overlap semantics. A missed index update leaves a stale entry alive (stale texture or vertices), which unit tests on overlap equivalence can cover.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.4 ms/frame. N = 500-3,000 entries x ~20-40 ns per unordered_map node visit on the 6500U x 2 caches x 5 chunks is 0.1-1.2 ms worst case. It is likely smaller because it is not called out separately in the measured profile, so the range is 0.03-0.4 ms, part of the measured 'lookups' inside the 13 % validation share (0.78-1.3 ms).

### 33. T-ABI-EXIT: Every return stores the whole written set back to context, which also keeps the guest-stack restore loads alive

- **Status:** done, safer variant (--abi-exit-sync: volatile stores dropped, non-volatile kept). Area: Translator: function entry/exit. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--abi-trusting-registers`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT plan item 3 and the 'function entry and exit ~40' row
- **What:** store_before_returns rewrites every `return;` (and the shared cold block's default case) into a store of every written local: r0, r1, r3, r12, r30, r31, LR, CTR in the synthetic function (8 stores). Because the restored r30/r31/r0 values feed those stores, the inline _restgpr/lwz loads from the guest stack (3 here) also stay live. The caller only needs r3, and r1/r14-r31 only because the caller currently reloads them. The sequence is emitted at every return site, which also costs code size.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:688-710, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:972, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:1008, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:6865-6907
- **Why paid:** The caller reloads everything after the call (T-ABI-RELOAD), so the callee must publish every register it may have changed. This is WiiCompiled's MaterializedContextExit rule.
- **Frequency** (derived; compiled synthetic body (.LBB0_36 exit block)): Once per translated function return (one per call); 8 stores plus 3 dependent guest-stack loads in the synthetic body.
- **Design:** With --abi-trusting-registers, a function not on the opt-out list stores at return only r3 and r4 if written (f1/f2 under float residency). Once callers stop reloading r1/r14-r31, those stores become dead, and so do the _restgpr loads and the `lwz r0; mtlr r0` LR restore that only feed them; clang then removes those loads. Keep full stores in opt-out functions and in any function reached through call_return_* resume (the stubs return through the same path, so use full stores whenever the function has external interior entries, until --checkpoint-free removes those).
- **Risk** (medium): Same failure class as T-ABI-RELOAD: a caller that reads a callee's r5-r12/r0/CTR after the call (4 census functions, 3 of them debugger stubs) sees stale values. A flag-transfer unwind returns through frames that no longer publish r14-r31, so the resumed continuation must reload from its saved OSContext, which it does. Gate on the opt-out list.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.17-0.69 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G (G = 5.56-10.56 ms). Synthetic exit: 8 stores + 3 restore loads, of which only the r3 store is needed, so 10 of ~150 hot instructions (6.7 %), discounted for cheap L1 traffic: f = 3-6.5 %. 0.03 x 5.56 = 0.17; 0.065 x 10.56 = 0.69.

### 34. R-TIMEBASE-READ: Time-base read path: shared-line CAS, indirect counter call, census atomic and audio-gate check on every read

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: clock. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_FAST_TIME_BASE (default 0)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 14 (leftovers: clock reads 3.3 %); NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native15/16 clock-read row
- **What:** runtime_time_base_ticks does a g_census fetch_add (a locked RMW), calls update_native_audio_output_gate, then RuntimeTimeline::now_ticks: an indirect call through source_.read (rdtsc >> 3), _umul128, an acquire load of discarded_host_ticks_, and a CAS loop on last_ticks_. last_ticks_ is shared with the broker, which also writes it 3 times per published event. The suspension-candidate bookkeeping follows.
- **Where:** runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:27-39, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:485-534, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5465-5493, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5192-5206
- **Why paid:** Cross-thread monotonic device time, host-suspension detection, census counters for the measurements, and the fast-boot audio mute gate all sit on the one read function.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md rates; clock reads 3.3 % of the simulation thread): 56,000 reads/s, 9,600 of them by the game
- **Design:** Split a hot read for the simulation thread. Inline rdtsc when the TSC source is active (no function pointer). Use a simulation-thread-local monotonic clamp: the invariant TSC is already monotonic per core, and Windows checks TSC sync. Publish to the shared last_ticks_ only at checkpoint bodies and before blocking. Move the census increment to a thread-local counter summed at exit. Move the audio-gate check to the VI boundary. Reuse one sample per checkpoint body across the DEC, AI and VI decisions to cut the 56,000/s toward the 9,600 the game asks for. Expect 50-80 % of the 3.3 % to go.
- **Risk** (medium): If two threads each clamp locally they could observe non-monotonic ticks relative to each other. commit_guest_observation throws on a backward guest observation, so publication must stay ordered before any cross-thread hand-off of ticks.
- **Estimate** (measured): 0.29-0.56 ms/frame. Clock reads measured at 3.3 % of the simulation thread (CPU_GAP_REPORT). est_ms = share * thread_ms_6500U: 0.033 * 8.9 = 0.29, 0.033 * 12.9 = 0.43 (midpoint), 0.033 * 16.9 = 0.56 ms. That works out to about 80 ns per read on the desktop (4.5 ms/s / 56,000), far above rdtsc plus one CAS, so most of it is the wrapper.

### 35. R-DLMEMCMP: Full byte compare of every cached display list on every CALL_DL replay

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: FIFO parse / display-list cache validation. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_DL_FLUSH_VALIDATE (0 = today's byte compare, 1 = flush-tracked with sampled backstop, audit = both and count)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'Render-thread quick wins' item 6 (validate cached display lists by flush tracking); NATIVE_EXECUTION:472-474
- **What:** Every top-level GXCallDisplayList replay validates the cached entry with std::equal over the entire display-list byte range (up to 4 MiB per entry) against guest memory. Both the last-index fast path and the map lookup do this, and the map lookup adds an unordered_map probe plus a remove_if pass over the variant vector. The flush-tracked invalidation that would make the compare unnecessary already exists, but ships off (GALAXY_DISPLAY_LIST_DIRTY_INVALIDATE=0).
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:2154-2194, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:778-860, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:1381-1424, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:736-750, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:3111-3124, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:8333-8365, installer/runtime-env.json:39
- **Why paid:** Correctness backstop. The guest may rewrite a display list in place at the same address and size, and the cache must not replay stale commands. Guest-write tracking is flush-based (NATIVE_EXECUTION F4: stores do not publish, dcbf/dcbst and gather bursts do), so nobody has relied on it alone. NATIVE_EXECUTION:472-474 says skipping the compare 'would rely on guest-write tracking being complete'.
- **Frequency** (derived; Code path in fifo_parser.cpp:2154-2194 runs on every depth-0 CALL_DL. Replay volume comes from the gx_backend.cpp:752 comment.): Once per top-level CALL_DL replay, comparing the whole list each time, thousands of replays per frame (gx_backend.cpp:752-756 comment: 'a busy SMG frame replays tens of thousands of draws out of cached display lists'). Byte volume per frame is not measured.
- **Design:** Validate by flush tracking. (1) Turn on the existing per-chunk display-list invalidation from the drained dirty ranges (invalidate_dirty_display_list_ranges, page-indexed via display_list_cache_page_map_). (2) Give CachedDisplayList a validated_epoch. A surviving entry (no published dcbf/gather-burst write overlapped it since capture) skips the std::equal. (3) Keep a backstop: memcmp a sampled entry each frame (round robin, e.g. 1/64 of hits), and on mismatch log [gx-dl-stale] and invalidate. (4) Uncached-mirror writers (0xC0000000 alias) never pass through dcbf. Before enabling by default, run GALAXY_GX_DL_FLUSH_VALIDATE=audit, which does both checks and counts disagreements on the gameplay, boot and Home-menu routes. The unordered_map variant probe can become a direct-mapped slot keyed by (addr,size) that holds the index of the last hit.
- **Risk** (medium): If a writer bypasses publication (CPU stores through the uncached mirror, a DMA engine that does not call notify_write, cache-eviction-only publication that the game relies on), the renderer replays stale commands: wrong materials or geometry until the next sampled backstop catches it. With live render memory (shipped GALAXY_RENDER_LIVE_MEMORY=1), a list rewritten after its chunk's dirty set was drained is seen one chunk late. The game must double-buffer anyway, because the hardware GP reads at execution time.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.2-0.6 ms/frame. Measured: validation (memcmp, lookups) ~13 % of the render thread (CPU_GAP_REPORT), and memcmp alone 9 % (NATIVE_EXECUTION:472, AMD iGPU), shared between display-list and packet-run compares. Assume the display-list share is 40-70 % of the 9 %, i.e. 3.6-6.3 % of the thread (hypothesis). est_ms = share x render_ms_6500U: 0.036 x 6 ms = 0.22 ms low, 0.063 x 10 ms = 0.63 ms high, with ~90 % removed.

### 36. T-BCTRL-DISPATCH: bctrl direct dispatch: 8 dependent loads and 8 branches through the services table, over a 10.6 MB pointer table

- **Status:** done, per-site cache (--bctrl-site-cache); global snapshot and smaller table open. Area: Translator + header: indirect calls. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--dispatch-globals (translator); module export galaxy_module_dispatch_snapshot, runtime GALAXY_DIRECT_DISPATCH_COMPACT=0 default`. Already listed: False F19 shipped the table (NATIVE_EXECUTION); PERFORMANCE_PLAN P10.4 lists only the withheld entries
- **What:** fast::call_guest_indirect is force-inlined at every bctrl/bclrl site. Compiled, the hot path is about 29 instructions: test services for null, load direct_dispatch, subtract direct_dispatch_base, compare direct_dispatch_count, test alignment, shift, load the entry, test it for null, load the pending_event_mask pointer and its value, load the direct_dispatch_suspended pointer and its value, store context->pc, then the indirect call. Each site also emits two static cache variables and an out-of-line boundary call. The runtime table has one 8-byte pointer per 4-byte guest word from the first to the last function record (about 5.3 MB of text, so about 10.6 MB), mostly null.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1857-1905, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7743-7769, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15866-15919
- **Why paid:** The table can be withdrawn while an external interrupt dispatch is live or a device event is pending (those calls must cross the audited service boundary), so the generated code re-reads every gate after each opaque call. The table is direct-mapped over the whole text range for an O(1) lookup.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; instruction count from compiling the synthetic bctrl site; bctrl share of calls not measured): Once per bctrl/bclrl. Exact dynamic share of calls unknown (SMG is virtual-call heavy: nerve execute, LiveActor movement); assumed 25-40 % of calls.
- **Design:** (1) Module-global dispatch snapshot, as with checkpoint-globals: `g_direct_dispatch {table, base, count}` filled through an optional export at load, plus one combined gate word (pending event mask OR suspended), so the fast path becomes sub, ror 2, cmp, jae, load entry, test, load gate (RIP-relative), test, call: about 12 instructions. The rotate folds the alignment test into the range compare, and the services null test goes because services is never null in a live module. (2) Make the table 4-byte RVA entries relative to the module base (5.3 MB), or index only address-taken function starts, so the hot targets' lines stay in L2/L3 on a 4 MB L3. (3) Add a profile-guided inline cache for monomorphic sites: compare against the most frequent target from the exact profile and call it directly (devirtualized, inlinable).
- **Risk** (low): A stale snapshot after the runtime withdraws the table would let an intercepted target run without its host transaction. The refresh must happen at every withdraw/suspend site, inside a call as with the checkpoint gate. Monomorphic caches need a fallback compare and miss the boundary only if the target list is wrong.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.06-0.32 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G (G = 5.56-10.56 ms). Saves ~16 of ~29 dispatch instructions per bctrl. With calls at 8 % of guest instructions and bctrl assumed at 25-40 % of calls, that is 0.08 x 0.3 x 16 = 0.4 x86 instructions per guest instruction out of ~8 (5 % of instructions). Loads hit L1 and branches predict, so f = 1-3 %: 0.01 x 5.56 = 0.06; 0.03 x 10.56 = 0.32. The table cache-miss part is unmeasured.

### 37. T-FCVT: Each single-precision result is a cvtsd2ss + cvtss2sd round trip (about 10 cycles of latency) because guest floats are held as binary64 bits

- **Status:** done (--single-float-arithmetic). Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--float-binary32-locals (with --float-typed-locals)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT per-instruction table: fmuls/fadds 'two conversions'
- **What:** single_result_bits computes in double, then narrows and widens again (vcvtsd2ss + vcvtss2sd; on Skylake about 5 cycles of latency each). Paired results do the same with cvtpd2ps + cvtps2pd. Every single-precision value is kept as a widened binary64 in the register file, so a dependent chain such as fmadds -> fmadds -> fadds pays about 10 extra cycles per link. stfs then narrows again (cvtsd2ss).
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:800-803, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:824-828, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1027-1030, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:374-381
- **Why paid:** The FPR is architecturally binary64, and the fast-float model computes single forms in binary64 and rounds once. The helpers have no type information, so every single result must be a widened double in context->fpr_bits.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT mix (measured categories), split hypothesised; latencies from Skylake instruction tables): Every single arithmetic result: about 90 % of float arithmetic (3.6 %) plus the paired arithmetic (about 2.5 %), about 5.7 % of hot guest instructions (hypothesis on the split).
- **Design:** Under --float-typed-locals plus the provenance pass of T-FMUL25: a register whose lanes are proven binary32 is held as a `float` local (scalar) or `__m128` (paired, 2 lanes used). fadds/fsubs/fmuls/fdivs and ps_add/sub/mul/div compute directly in binary32. For +, -, *, / this is bit-identical to compute-in-double-then-round, because 53 >= 2*24+2 makes the double rounding innocuous. lfs/psq_l then skip cvtss2sd and stfs/psq_st skip cvtsd2ss. Widen only at sync points or when a double op consumes the value.
- **Risk** (low): add/sub/mul/div are bit-identical. fmadds/ps_madd computed with fmaf (one rounding) differ from today's fma-in-double-then-round only in double-rounding corner cases (at most 1 ulp), within the declared fast-float accuracy class. FTZ/DAZ behaviour is unchanged. A provenance bug feeding a true binary64 into a float local loses precision, which the differential harnesses detect.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.056-0.32 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). 2 x86 instructions x 5.7 % = 0.11 x86 per guest instruction (about 1.4 % of instructions). Each removes about 10 cycles from latency-bound float chains; f = 1-3 %. Low = 0.625*0.01*8.9 = 0.056; high = 0.625*0.03*16.9 = 0.32 ms. Realisable only with T-FTYPED, since values stored to the context must be widened.

### 38. R-HEAPALLOC: Remaining per-frame / per-chunk heap allocations on the render thread

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: allocations. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_FRAME_ARENA (0 = today's allocations)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT item 6 'Pool the per-frame allocations'
- **What:** Sources found in code: (a) the three frame binding unordered_maps: node allocation per emplace and free per clear, every chunk. (b) Decoded-run miss vectors (R-DECODECHURN). (c) The display-list miss recording: dl_recording.bytes.assign plus commands, xf_values, packet and index vectors per first-sight or changed list. (d) find_cached_display_list copies the candidate vector when there are more than 8 variants. (e) invalidate_display_list_cache_range builds a candidates vector per range. (f) poll_completed_frame_effects builds a 'ready' vector per completion. (g) PendingFrameEffects, FrameChunk.pe_events and preclassified_pe_events vectors are not pooled (fifo, dirty_ranges, memory_ranges and snapshots are). (h) TextureCache LRU std::list nodes plus decoded_lru_pos_ map nodes per new texture.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:9928-9937, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:10615-10712, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5143-5229, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:2227-2240, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:856-861, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:1381-1424, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7792-7808, runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:1429-1450, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7349-7378
- **Why paid:** Standard containers chosen for convenience. Chunk buffers were already pooled (fifo_pool_ etc.), the rest were not.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT: 'Heap allocation and free on the render thread: 3.4 % of its running samples'): Measured total: heap allocation and free are 3.4 % of render-thread running samples. Per-source rates are not measured.
- **Design:** (1) Flat open-addressed maps with an epoch tag for the frame binding caches (clear = epoch++). (2) A per-game-frame std::pmr::monotonic_buffer_resource (reset at the frame boundary) for miss-path scratch: reads, merged, candidates, ready. (3) Make the 'ready' vector and candidates vectors members with retained capacity. (4) Pool pe_events vectors alongside fifo_pool_. (5) Replace the texture LRU list plus position map with a frame-stamp array indexed by SRV slot.
- **Risk** (low): Lifetime bugs only. Arena memory reused while a pointer is still held gives use-after-reset, which ASan-style tests catch. Behaviour is otherwise unchanged.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.1-0.27 ms/frame. Measured 3.4 % (CPU_GAP_REPORT) x 6..10 ms = 0.2-0.34 ms. Removing 50-80 % gives 0.1-0.27 ms. Some overlap with R-BINDTABLES and R-DECODECHURN; do not add these three together without discounting.

### 39. T-FCMP: fcmpu/fcmpo + bc: about 24 x86 with integer-domain NaN tests, read-modify-write of FPSCR.FPCC and CR, then a branch through context->cr

- **Status:** done, CR-only compare (--fast-float-forms); fusion into the branch open. Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--fuse-float-compare`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** compare_f64 moves both operands to GPRs and runs two movabs/and/cmp NaN tests, then two vucomisd and setcc/cmov. It read-modify-writes context->fpscr (FPCC, bits 12-15), which nothing reads under fast float except mffs, and read-modify-writes context->cr. The following `if (cr_bit(context, k))` branch then reloads cr through store forwarding. Probe: 24 hot instructions plus the branch, against 2 (ucomisd + jcc) for a direct compile. cr0_fusion handles only compare_signed/compare_unsigned and update_cr0, not compare_f64. ps_cmpo/ps_cmpu do the same plus a HID2 test.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5752-5762, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4952-4967, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12676-12750, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/cr0_fusion.rs:243-286, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5450-5476
- **Why paid:** The exact-model compare sets FPSCR.FPCC and the CR field architecturally, and the branch lowering reads the CR from the context. The CR0 fusion pass was written for integer compares only.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Not in the measured mix; cost per site derived from the probe disassembly (today_fcmp)): Every float compare. Hypothesis 0.5-1.5 % of hot guest instructions; CodeWarrior also emits `cror 2,0,2` / `cror 2,1,2` between fcmpo and beq for <= and >=.
- **Design:** Extend cr0_fusion (translator) to `galaxy::compare_f64(context, F, A, B, o);` followed by a `cr_bit(context, 4F+k)` branch for k in {0,1,2}, on any field, since the compare's only consumer is that branch. Emit `if (std::bit_cast<double>(A) < std::bit_cast<double>(B))` for LT (or >, ==). A NaN makes all three false, exactly as the CR bits do. Also fuse the three-line `compare_f64; cror 4F+2,4F+0,4F+2; branch on 4F+2` into `<=` (and >=). Decline bit 3 (FU) and multi-consumer sites. In fast-float modules, drop the FPCC write from the unfused compare_f64 too (a kFastFloat branch in the header).
- **Risk** (low): IEEE <, >, == on doubles reproduce LT/GT/EQ, including NaN (all false) and +/-0 (equal). Changes: FPSCR.FPCC is no longer updated (only mffs could observe it, and fast float already leaves the FPSCR status stale), and the CR field write is dropped under the same dead-CR adjacency rule cr0_fusion already relies on. fcmpo's VXVC on NaN is not recorded (fast float already records no status). A wrong fusion flips a branch, which the differential harnesses and routes catch.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.045-0.32 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). About 20 x86 saved x 0.5-1.5 % = 0.1-0.3 x86 per guest instruction (1.25-3.75 % of instructions), plus the CR store-to-load latency before the branch; f = 0.8-3 %. Low = 0.625*0.008*8.9 = 0.045; high = 0.625*0.03*16.9 = 0.32 ms.

### 40. T-FLOAT-STACK: stfs and psq_st/psq_l on the stack keep the pipe test and run-time GQR decode

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: memory access. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--stack-float-stores (with --assume-gqr0-float)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md item 1 ('r1/r11-relative accesses skip the gather-pipe test'). Integer r1 is done in generation 25 and r11 _savegpr is on this branch; the float and psq forms are not done.
- **What:** stfs/stfsu with base r1 go through store_fpr_single{_hw}, which calls fast::store with the pipe test, whatever the base register. psq_st/psq_l are lowered without regard to the base and pay for:
- loading context->gqr[i] and decoding the type;
- for stores, the pipe test and four argument spills for the inline quantised/cold path.
CodeWarrior's prologue/epilogue FPR saves ('psq_st fN,d(r1),0,0' / 'psq_l') and TVec3f stack locals are exactly this form, using GQR0.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4303-4345, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4195-4213, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:374-381, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:414-440, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:497-540
- **Why paid:** The r1 exemption was implemented only for integer stores and stfd (generation 25, flat_store_helper), and for _savegpr through r11 on this branch. The GQR value is read at run time because the translator never proves GQR0.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; instruction mix and stack share measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md; float stack fraction assumed): Every float or paired stack access. Float stores are 3.2% and paired/other float 3.7% of hot instructions, and 60% of hot stores are stack-relative. The float-specific stack share is assumed.
- **Design:** - With base r1 (or r11 proven as in --stack-r11-stores), lower stfs to store_fpr_single_hw_stack (cvtsd2ss plus store_stack) and psq_st/psq_l to psq_store_stack/psq_load_stack.
- With --assume-gqr0-float, psq with GQR0 becomes two cvtsd2ss plus one 8-byte movbe (or two loads plus cvtss2sd), with no GQR load and no type switch. The translator verifies that no mtspr 912 writes anything but a proven 0; the SDK saves and restores only GQR1-7.
- **Risk** (low): The stack part writes the same bytes as today. The GQR0 part is medium-low: if anything set GQR0 non-zero, quantised data would be misread and floats corrupted. This is guarded by the static scan plus a debug assert in write_spr for GQR0.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.08-0.28 ms/frame. Measured in scratchpad/micro:
- stfs to r1 is 12 hot instructions plus 5 forced push/pop pairs (247 B); the lean form is 6 instructions with no pushes (41 B).
- psq_st to r1 with GQR0 is about 30 hot instructions including 4 argument spills (401 B); the lean form is about 13 (65 B).
Calculation: stfs to stack at 1.0 to 1.9% of guest instructions x 6 = 0.06 to 0.11; psq stack at 0.5 to 1.5% x about 11 (average of load and store) = 0.055 to 0.165. Together 0.11 to 0.28 x86 per guest instruction, i.e. 1.4 to 3.5% of game code, giving 0.11 to 0.28 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms. The low bound is widened to 0.08.

### 41. T-FPR-RESIDENCY: Float registers stay in context: every float op loads/stores context->fpr_bits/ps1_bits

- **Status:** done with 2.1 (--experimental-guest-resident-float now pays). Area: Translator: register residency (float). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--abi-trusting-float-residency`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'Later: Float registers as typed locals'; PERFORMANCE_PLAN P10.1; TRANSLATOR_CPU_TASK item 4
- **What:** Release modules set guest_resident_integer only. guest_resident_float is off, so fmuls/fadds read two context FPRs and write fpr+ps1, lfs writes fpr+ps1 to context, and stfs reads context. When float residency is on, it loads every used FPR/PS1 entry at entry, stores and reloads every one at each call, and stores all written ones at every return. In the synthetic float caller that was 6 stores + 6 reloads per call (f1, f2, f31 x ps0/ps1) and 6 exit stores, where only f1 matters by ABI.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-cli/src/generate.rs:56-84, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:129-155, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:460-556, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:665-686, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:262-266
- **Why paid:** Float residency was measured slower: 87.9 vs 83.2 ns under the exact model, and 21.1 vs 20.7 % under fast float. The pass loads and stores every used float register at entry, exit and calls, which offsets the removed per-operation traffic.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT instruction mix; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md 'FPR/PS1 residency under fast float'): Every float instruction (float loads 6.3 % + float arithmetic 3.6 % + float stores 3.2 % + paired/other 3.7 % = 16.8 % of hot guest instructions), about 2-4 context memory operations each.
- **Design:** Turn float residency on only together with ABI-trusting float sync. Entry: load only read-before-write FPRs (f1-f8 arguments, f14-f31 that the prologue saves). Calls: store dirty f1-f8 and the ps1 of arguments only for callees on a paired-argument list; reload only f1/f2 if used; never reload f14-f31. Exit: store f1/f2 (+ps1) only. Use the same opt-out list as the integer side. Optionally type the locals as double (fpr) and float pairs to avoid bit casts.
- **Risk** (medium): ps1 halves of argument registers: a callee that reads ps1 of f1-f8 without writing it (hand-written paired asm taking vectors in registers) would read stale data. It needs a census of ps_* reads of argument registers before a write at function entry. Win64 makes xmm6-15 callee-saved, so live float locals add prologue saves.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.08-0.63 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G. 16.8 % float instructions x ~2.5 context operations is ~0.42 memory operations per guest instruction (~5 % of x86 instructions). Discounting the remaining sync and the measured +0.4 points of today's naive version, f = 1.5-6 %: 0.015 x 5.56 = 0.08; 0.06 x 10.56 = 0.63.

### 42. G-EFBCOPY-GPU: Render-pass breaks, pipeline drains and depth resolves per copy and per post-process pass

- **Status:** open. Area: EFB-copy path (GPU). Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_EFB_COPY_LAZY_TRANSITIONS (default 0)`. Already listed: False P7 (PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:495-538) lists scale cap and identity aliasing, not per-copy drains
- **What:** Gen9 renders directly to memory (no tiling), but every RT change and RT-to-SRV transition makes the driver emit a render-cache flush plus a CS stall (PIPE_CONTROL), draining the 3D pipeline. Per frame there are about 5.5 copy passes (each switches RT away from and back to the EFB), 5 post-process RT switches (edges, weights, blend, scaled_, backbuffer) and clears. Depth-source copies (DOF) and the pointer-depth field move EFB depth from DEPTH_WRITE to PIXEL_SHADER_RESOURCE, which on Intel forces a HiZ depth resolve of the 640x528 x scale surface, and the transition back costs another HiZ step.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7684-7743, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7623-7648, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:8315-8350, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:589-611, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:861-912, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:12705-12710
- **Why paid:** GX semantics: copies read the EFB mid-frame and later draws sample them. D3D12 needs state transitions, and Intel implements them with flushes and resolves.
- **Frequency** (derived; Copy rate measured (PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:503-509); post-process pass count from post_process.cpp:589-611 and 877-912; pointer depth every frame in mouse mode.): About 10-12 RT switches and 2-3 depth-to-SRV transitions per frame.
- **Design:** (1) The lazy EFB transition from G-EFBCOPY-CPU also removes GPU drains for consecutive copies. (2) For the pointer-depth field use a sub-window (G-PTRDEPTH), which shrinks one depth resolve. (3) Fuse the SMAA blend with EASU or the resample when the preset keeps SMAA, saving one RT switch and a full read-write. (4) The EFB copy scale cap and identity aliasing are already listed (P7).
- **Risk** (medium): Lazy transitions must be flushed before any GX draw or clear touches the EFB. A missed one is a D3D12 validation error or corruption on Intel (no implicit promotion for RTs).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.15-0.55 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame: 10-12 pipeline drains x 10-30 us (Gen9 3D pipe drain plus RT cache flush) = 0.1-0.36 ms, plus 2-3 HiZ resolves of 338k px x 0.02-0.06 ms = 0.04-0.18 ms. Total 0.15-0.55 ms at 1x, more at 2x.

### 43. T-SAVEGPR-PIPE: _savegpr stores through r11 paid the gather-pipe test

- **Status:** done on branch (item 1). Area: Translator: memory access (stack stores). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--stack-r11-stores`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md item 1; docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS.md 1.4
- **What:** The inline _savegpr_N expansion stored rN..r31 through fast::store. That added a masked compare and branch per register, plus about 200 B of cold code per register store.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5788-5815, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:6835-6907
- **Why paid:** r11 was not recognised as a frame pointer.
- **Frequency** (measured; measured, docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS.md 1.4 (11,719 sites; 75 to 26 instructions for 7 stores)): 11,719 static sites; paid once per execution of a function that saves registers with _savegpr.
- **Design:** --stack-r11-stores: when 'addi r11,r1,d' directly precedes the bl and the bl is neither a branch target nor an entry, use store_stack.
- **Risk** (low): Same bytes as before; a stack frame can never be the gather pipe.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.3 ms/frame. Probe savegpr_frame retires 114 x86 instructions without the flag and 42 with it, for 14 guest instructions (this session's asm-probe callgrind run on Linux). If 5-15% of function executions (4.2% of guest instructions) use _savegpr and each saves about 50 instructions: 0.1 to 0.3 x86 per guest instruction, i.e. 1.3 to 3.9% of game code, giving 0.1 to 0.3 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms. The low bound is widened to 0.05.

### 44. G-RGBA8-TEX: Every GX texture is expanded to RGBA8, including 4-bit CMPR/I4 and 8-bit I8/IA4

- **Status:** open. Area: HD 520 texture bandwidth. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_TEXTURE_NATIVE_FORMATS (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** TextureCache creates every decoded texture as DXGI_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_UNORM (with generated mips). CMPR (4 bpp, a DXT1 relative), I4/I8 and IA4/IA8 grow 2-8x in memory, upload traffic and per-sample bandwidth. On UMA that is DRAM bandwidth shared with the CPU, and texture-cache footprint on a 4 MB shared LLC.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:2519-2531, runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:2536-2556, runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:2568-2580
- **Why paid:** Decoder simplicity: one texel format for all TEV sampling, with TLUT and intensity conventions handled on the CPU.
- **Frequency** (derived; texture_cache.cpp:2525 (single format).): Per texture sample (every frame) and per texture upload.
- **Design:** Exact formats first: I8 to R8_UNORM and IA8 to R8G8_UNORM, using D3D12 Shader4ComponentMapping to replicate I into RGB/A (the SRV desc at :2570-2574), which is bit-exact. Then CMPR to BC1 as a block transcode. This is exact only where GX's 2/3-1/3 interpolation matches BC1's, which needs a per-texture verification or limiting it to opaque blocks; keep RGBA8 otherwise. Gate as GALAXY_TEXTURE_NATIVE_FORMATS.
- **Risk** (medium): BC1 decoders differ from GX CMPR rounding (visible banding on gradients). Only the I8/IA8 swizzle path is bit-exact. EFB-copy aliases must keep RGBA8.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-0.6 ms/frame. GPU ms per frame: texture fetch is about 20-30 % of the 1.5-4 ms TEV fill at 1x, so 0.3-1.2 ms. Assume 30-50 % of sampled texels come from CMPR/I-formats, where bandwidth drops 4-8x, giving 0.1-0.6 ms saved. Not measured.

### 45. T-ABI-STORE: Every translated call stores the function-wide 'written' set first, including volatile and unchanged registers

- **Status:** done, step 2 (--abi-call-sync); dirty tracking left to clang. Area: Translator: calls, returns, register residency. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--abi-trusting-registers (step 1 alone: --residency-dirty-stores)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT plan item 3 ('A call stores only the argument registers it sets'); the dirty-since-last-sync refinement is new
- **What:** Before each Sync::Full call, store_sequence writes every local the function writes anywhere: volatile non-argument registers (r0, r11, r12, CTR, XER), registers already stored at the previous call and unchanged since (r1, r30, r31 at the second call), and LR. The synthetic caller stored 8 registers at the bctrl and 9 (with the pc store) at the following bl. The second call needed only r3 and the LR constant.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:607-618, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:741-746, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:935-941
- **Why paid:** Stores are function-wide, not per program point: there is no dirty-since-last-sync tracking, and the pass assumes an unknown callee may read any register through context.
- **Frequency** (derived; compiled synthetic body; CPU_GAP_REPORT NameObjCategoryList::execute census): Once per translated or indirect call. Measured: 7-9 context stores per call (derived from clang output); 11 at a real bctrl.
- **Design:** Two steps in the same flag. (1) Dirty tracking: keep a per-straight-line-region set of locals written since the last sync point, reset to 'all written' at every label (labels are the only joins in the emitted text), and store only dirty locals at a sync. Safe for any callee. (2) ABI filter for translated callees not on the opt-out list: store only r1 (if dirty), r3-r10 that are dirty, LR, and dirty r14-r31. Keeping non-volatile stores preserves guest stack-frame fidelity, because the callee's _savegpr stores read context. Never store r0, r11, r12, CTR or XER. An optional third step, --abi-stale-save-area, also drops the non-volatile stores and accepts stale register save slots in callee frames.
- **Risk** (medium): Step 1 is low risk: it only removes stores whose value context already holds, but a label miscount in the textual pass would drop a needed store. Step 2: a callee reading a volatile non-argument register at entry (the 14 census functions: exception vectors, OSSaveContext, the ptmf thunk) reads stale data, so it must be opted out. Step 3: setjmp, debugger backtraces and OSDumpContext would read stale save slots.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.14-0.53 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G, with G = 5.56 / 8.06 / 10.56 ms as in T-ABI-RELOAD. In the compiled synthetic body about 7 of ~150 hot instructions are removable stores (5 %). Stores are cheap but contend for the single store port on Skylake, so f = 2.5-5 %: 0.025 x 5.56 = 0.14; 0.05 x 10.56 = 0.53.

### 46. T-SWITCH-TABLE: Jump-table switches (bctr) execute as a re-entrant indirect call into the same function

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: control flow. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--inline-switch-tables`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** A non-link bctr (CodeWarrior's jump-table switch: lwzx from a .data table, mtctr, bctr) lowers to emit_indirect_guest_call followed by 'return'. Each dispatch pays for:
- a full residency sync (store all written registers, reload all used ones);
- the call_guest_indirect gate (22 x86);
- the owner's prologue: pushes, entry loads, flat_base;
- the pc-dispatch switch and a resident_entry stub that reloads every used register.
The rest of the function then runs in a nested native frame, so a switch inside a loop adds one native frame per iteration.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5988-6045, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7745-7769, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1941-1953, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1215-1257, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3549-3572, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:826-840, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:990-993, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1881-1906
- **Why paid:** Every bctr target is treated as unknown. The data-section code pointers (the jump tables) are made interior entries so the call can land, but nothing turns the in-function case into a goto.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; assumed; the mechanism is derived from code): Once per executed switch dispatch. Assumed 0.05 to 0.3% of hot guest instructions; switch tables cannot be counted without main.dol.
- **Design:** For a non-link bctr in a function that owns data-section interior code pointers, emit:
'switch (branch_target) { case 0x..: goto label_..; ...; default: <today's indirect call>; return; }'
Those labels already exist because the addresses are entries. Optionally restrict the cases to the table decoded from the lis/addi/rlwinm/lwzx/mtctr pattern. The residency pass sees plain gotos and needs no sync.
- **Risk** (low): Event delivery that the call boundary performed while pending_event_mask was non-zero no longer happens at that bctr; this changes only cadence. A target outside the case list keeps today's path. Native stack depth falls, which removes a potential fiber-stack overflow source.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.3 ms/frame. About 100 x86 instructions removed per dispatch (derived from the micro and probe disassembly): 22 for the gate, about 10 stores, about 15 reloads, about 16 push/pop, about 15 entry loads, the dispatch, about 15 stub reloads and about 10 return stores. Calculation: (0.05 to 0.3%) x 100 = 0.05 to 0.3 x86 per guest instruction, i.e. 0.6 to 3.75% of game code, giving 0.05 to 0.3 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms. The low bound is widened to 0.03.

### 47. R-DECODECHURN: Decoded-vertex cache misses that recur every frame still pay snapshot capture and entry replacement

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: decoded-vertex cache miss path. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_DECODED_CACHE_ADAPTIVE_ADMISSION (0 = always admit)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** On a miss with the cache enabled, the decoder records guest_array_reads per attribute. capture_decoded_packet_run_dependencies then allocates reads, merged, captured.bytes and captured.ranges vectors, memcpys every array byte into the snapshot, and builds and emplaces a new entry (vertices vector moved in, guest_array_reads rebuilt, page mask) before prune. Packet runs whose arrays change every frame (double-buffered CPU-skinned or morph buffers alternate array_base, or contents change) miss every frame and pay all of this for an entry that never hits.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5143-5229, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:11380-11500, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5110-5141
- **Why paid:** Admission is unconditional. A miss always tries to cache.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Code path; no published miss rate): Per miss of a packet run whose inputs change every frame. Counts exist (frame_decoded_vertex_cache_misses_, stale_rejects) but are not published.
- **Design:** Adaptive admission. Keep a small direct-mapped 'churn' table keyed by (cache_token, packet_run_index) with a consecutive-stale/miss counter. After 2 consecutive misses whose snapshot would have changed, decode with record_indexed_read_ranges=false into decoded_packet_vertex_scratch_ (the path already used when the cache is off) and skip snapshot capture and emplace for 60 frames, then retry.
- **Risk** (low): Only changes whether an entry is cached, never what is drawn. The worst case is a lost hit for a run that became static (until the retry window).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.3 ms/frame. Per churned run: 4 allocations plus an array memcpy (KBs) plus map emplace and prune, ~2-10 us on the 6500U, x 10-30 churning runs per frame = 0.02-0.3 ms. The allocations are part of the measured 3.4 % heap share.

### 48. R-DLLOOKUP: Display-list cache lookup and re-parse on changed bytes (double-buffered lists)

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: FIFO parse / display-list cache. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_DL_DIRECT_TABLE (0 = today's map lookup and always-record misses)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** When the last-index fast path misses, find_cached_display_list probes an unordered_map, runs remove_if over the variant vector, and memcmps each candidate. A list whose bytes differ (a dynamically built list at a reused address, e.g. alternating per-frame buffers) is invalidated and fully re-parsed with recording, which costs DisplayListRecording allocations, a byte copy, and prepared/packet-run building, every time it changes.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:778-860, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:909-919, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:2196-2280
- **Why paid:** The cache is keyed by (address, size), and the content check is the only discriminator. Rebuilt lists cannot hit.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; NATIVE_EXECUTION:617 (miss parse/store counters exist; Observatory first-sight store 0.7 ms)): Per CALL_DL whose last-index slot differs (interleaved lists) and per changed list. Miss counters exist (call_dl_cache_misses, fifo-call-dl-miss-parse-us) but steady-state values are not published.
- **Design:** (1) Replace the unordered_map plus vector with a direct-mapped table (e.g. 4,096 slots, hash(addr,size)) holding up to 4 variant indices inline, with no remove_if on lookup. (2) When R-DLMEMCMP flush tracking is on, a list with no published write needs no compare. For lists that change every frame, add a per-address 'volatile' counter: after k consecutive content misses, execute it uncached (run_window without recording) to stop paying recording and allocation.
- **Risk** (low): An uncached execution of a volatile list is the original parse path, so it is correct by construction. A lookup-table bug can return a wrong variant, which is guarded by the existing address, size and CP-dependency checks.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.3 ms/frame. Part of the measured 'FIFO parse and display-list replay ~13.5 %' (0.81-1.35 ms) and 'lookups' in the 13 % validation share. Assume 3-25 % of the lookup and miss-parse work is removable: 0.03-0.3 ms.

### 49. R-PKTMEMCMP: Decoded packet-run cache re-compares vertex-array snapshots against guest memory every chunk

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: decoded-vertex cache validation. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_VERTEX_FLUSH_VALIDATE (0 = today's per-generation compare)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT render-thread item 6 ('display-list and packet validation'); NATIVE_EXECUTION:472-474
- **What:** invalidate_dirty_decoded_packet_run_ranges bumps decoded_packet_run_generation_ unconditionally once per render chunk, including empty batches. The first replay of every used entry in each chunk then runs decoded_packet_run_dependencies_match, which memcmps every merged snapshot range (indexed position/normal/color/uv arrays) against guest memory, after the eager overlap scan has already erased every entry a published write touched.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5231-5271, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5414-5433, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:11268-11318, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:752-786
- **Why paid:** Backstop 'against a coalesced or missed notification' (gx_backend.cpp:765-768). The overlap scan is trusted only for eviction, never for validity.
- **Frequency** (derived; Generation bump per chunk at gx_backend.cpp:5422 together with the per-chunk call at gx_backend.cpp:8363. Chunk count from NATIVE_EXECUTION:287.): Once per cached packet-run entry used per render chunk. About 5 chunks per frame (measured, NATIVE_EXECUTION:287 'about 5 chunks of 38 KB per frame'). Each compare covers that entry's whole array snapshot.
- **Design:** In invalidate_dirty_decoded_packet_run_ranges (non-lazy, sorted path, which ships on), stamp survivors validated_generation = new generation as the scan proves them non-overlapping, so the hit path takes the existing 'entry_generation == current_generation' fast return. Keep the memcmp only for entries created or touched under GALAXY_GX_LAZY_VERTEX_INVALIDATION, plus a round-robin sampled backstop (1 entry per chunk) that logs [gx-vtx-stale]. Vertex arrays must be DCFlushRange'd before GX reads them on hardware too, so flush publication is the hardware contract.
- **Risk** (medium): An unpublished writer to a vertex array (uncached mirror, unnotified DMA) gives stale vertices (wrong skinning or morph) until the backstop samples that entry. Bounded by the sampled compare and visible as a counter.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.15-0.5 ms/frame. The remaining 30-60 % of the measured 9 % memcmp share (NATIVE_EXECUTION:472) is 2.7-5.4 % of the render thread. est_ms = 0.027 x 6 ms = 0.16 ms low, 0.054 x 10 ms = 0.54 ms high, rounded, with ~90 % removed.

### 50. R-PE-PREPARSE: PE pre-classification: a second FIFO parse on the simulation thread for every submission

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: PE pre-parse. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_PE_EVENTS_FROM_HOST_CALLS (default 0); step 1 uses the existing GALAXY_PE_PRECLASSIFY=0`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 10 (GX stage A); docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md section 3 stage A
- **What:** Every submitted FIFO chunk (about 5 streamed chunks plus the frame-boundary chunk per frame) is parsed a second time on the simulation thread by FramePeEventClassifier, a FifoParser in classify-only mode. The only purpose is to decide whether the chunk is 'event-free', so its completion receipt can be issued as already done. The classify-only parse still walks every top-level command. For every CALL_DL it resolves the list in guest RAM, byte-compares the whole display list against the cached copy (std::equal), checks dependencies, and replays its CP writes and last BP write into the classifier's own GxState.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5886-5911, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:2932-2993, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:721-734, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5963-5975, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:2145-2221, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:1431-1475, runtime/src/gx/frame_completion.cpp:44-59
- **Why paid:** History. With GPU-fence PE delivery, an event-bearing receipt completed only at the GPU fence, so proving a chunk event-free let waiters skip that wait. Since P1.5 A (GALAXY_PE_EVENTS_AT_CONSUMPTION=1, the default) and independent VI completion (installer: GALAXY_INDEPENDENT_VI_PE_COMPLETION=1), event-bearing receipts already resolve when the render thread consumes the chunk. The guest emulates PE through BP 0x45/0x47/0x48 writes in the FIFO, so the only way to know which chunk carries an event is to parse it.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md (PE pre-parse 2.5 %); docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:167 and :287 (61-126 KB/frame, about 5 chunks of 38 KB)): Once per non-empty submission: about 5 streamed chunks of ~38 KB plus 1-2 boundary chunks per frame, 61-126 KB of FIFO per frame. Measured as 2.5 % of simulation-thread running samples.
- **Design:** Step 1 (no code): run the laptop A/B with the existing GALAXY_PE_PRECLASSIFY=0. Under PE-at-consumption and independent VI, the only consumers that would now wait for render consumption are sync captures and the non-independent VI path. Step 2 (GX stage A, SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN section 3): GXSetDrawDone 804B9F7C, GXSetDrawSync 804B9EC8 and GXDrawDone 804BA014 become host calls. GXSetDrawSync is already admitted through native_call_admission.h. The host calls append {fifo_offset, kind, token} to a per-capture PE mark list on GuestAddressSpace, and take_gx_fifo hands the marks over with the bytes. render_frame issues issue_event_free() when the mark list is empty, so no parse runs on the simulation thread. The render parser's existing preclassified-vs-actual comparison (gx_backend.cpp:8695-8720) becomes the guard. A display list whose cached classify_summary==2 (contains a PE write) forces the old classifier for that chunk.
- **Risk** (medium): If a PE BP write reaches the FIFO by a path other than the three SDK functions (a display list recorded around GXSetDrawSync, raw gather-pipe writes), an event-free receipt would complete before the render thread delivers that event, and a draw-done waiter could consume a receipt early. The existing render-side mismatch check turns this into a hard error rather than silent misbehaviour. With step 1 alone, any path that waits on a streamed chunk's receipt now waits for render consumption, which must be checked in the cadence and timeline recordings.
- **Estimate** (measured): 0.22-0.42 ms/frame. est_ms = share_of_thread x sim_thread_ms_6500U = 0.025 x 8.9..16.9 ms (midpoint 0.025 x 12.9 = 0.32 ms). Share is measured (CPU_GAP_REPORT, 2.5 %); the 6500U thread time is the CPU_GAP_REPORT projection (hypothesis). Both steps delete roughly all of it. R-CLASSIFY-TAIL-COPY is a subset of this share and must not be added on top.

### 51. H-GX-STAGEA: GXDrawDone/GXSetDrawSync as translated SDK code: PE emulation and the simulation-thread FIFO pre-parse

- **Status:** open. Area: GX API as host code (stage A). Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--host-gx-draw-sync / GALAXY_GX_STAGE_A=1`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 10 'GX stage A'; docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md section 3 stage A
- **What:** GXDrawDone 0x804BA014, GXSetDrawSync 0x804B9EC8 and GXSetDrawDone run translated and wait on emulated PE registers and interrupts. To classify draw-done and token events, the simulation thread runs a second FifoParser pass (classify-only since Native19) over every FIFO chunk.
- **Where:** docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md:210-232, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:113, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:500
- **Why paid:** The PE token and finish interrupts are emulated from the FIFO contents, so the simulation thread has to know where in the FIFO they are before the render thread consumes it.
- **Frequency** (measured; 'PE pre-parse of the FIFO 2.5 %' (measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md)): Every FIFO chunk, about 5 per frame at 38 KB (Native11 streamed FIFO). Measured at 2.5 % of the simulation thread.
- **Design:** Translator --host-gx-draw-sync adds GXDrawDone, GXSetDrawSync, GXSetDrawDone and GXSetDrawSyncCallback/DoneCallback to requires_host_guest_call. Runtime GALAXY_GX_STAGE_A=1: GXSetDrawSync(token) records the token as a FIFO marker. The render thread's own parse fires the sync callback through deferred delivery. GXDrawDone flushes and blocks the fiber on the submission's consumption event (PE-at-consumption semantics already ship). The PE classifier is skipped when stage A is active.
- **Risk** (medium): Token ordering against the game's own polling of PE registers: any direct PE register read left in game code would see stale values. The pointer-depth boundary at 0x804B9EC8 (mouse capture) must move to the new host GXSetDrawSync. Concrete failure: a frame that never receives its draw-done stalls the main thread. A fallback to the PE path per frame is needed while validating.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.22-0.42 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.025 x T_sim = 0.025 x 8.9-16.9 ms = 0.22-0.42 ms (midpoint 0.32 ms). The GALAXY_PE_PRECLASSIFY=0 A/B (sim -0.75, render +1.9 points) shows that skipping the pre-parse alone moves cost to the render thread unless tokens are found by a parse that already runs there.

### 52. T-FMUL25: 25-bit multiplier rounding runs on every single multiply, including operands that are already binary32

- **Status:** done differently (--single-float-arithmetic computes in binary32). Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--single-provenance`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT per-instruction table: fmuls/fadds ~13 'multiplier rounding' (cost listed; the elision design is not)
- **What:** fmuls, fmadds family, ps_mul/ps_muls*/ps_madd* always apply round_multiplier_25: (bits + 0x08000000) & ~0x0FFFFFFF. Scalar: vmovq xmm->gpr, add, and, vmovq gpr->xmm, which is 4 instructions and about 6-7 cycles of latency on the multiplier path (probe disassembly). Paired: vpaddq + vpand with two constant loads. For a widened binary32 value the low 29 fraction bits are already zero, so the operation is the identity. It only changes binary64 multipliers (results of double ops, lfd). Because the operand is reloaded from the context after aliasing stores, clang cannot hoist the rounding out of loops: the probe loop recomputes it for f1 on each element.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:622-626, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:814-854, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1032-1037, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1088-1114, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4558-4564, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4619-4625
- **Why paid:** The Gekko rounds frC of single multiplies to 25 significant bits. The fast-float model keeps this to stay in the Dolphin accuracy class. The translator emits it unconditionally because it tracks no value provenance.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT mix (measured categories); the multiply share and provenance fraction are hypotheses): Every single-precision multiply: about 40-60 % of float arithmetic (3.6 %) plus about 40-60 % of paired arithmetic (within 3.7 %), about 2.4-4 % of hot guest instructions. Hypothesis: 70-95 % of those have a binary32-proven multiplier.
- **Design:** Translator-only binary32 provenance pass per function, forward and per lane (ps0 and ps1 tracked separately). A lane is binary32 after lfs/lfsx/lfsu, psq_l (type 0, and quantized: int16 times 2^-s is exact in binary32), any single or paired arithmetic, frsp, fres, ps_res/rsqrte, and fmr/fneg/fabs/ps_merge of binary32 lanes. It is cleared by lfd, double ops, fctiwz, mffs, calls (or only f0-f13 under ABI trust), interior entries and unknown merges (meet = AND). Emit scalar_binary/scalar_ternary/paired_* with a new template bool RoundMultiplier=false where proven. Header: one extra template parameter on the fast helpers and their _r forms.
- **Risk** (low): With correct provenance the results are bit-identical, since rounding a binary32 value to 25 bits is the identity. A provenance bug (an unrounded binary64 multiplier treated as binary32) changes the product by at most about 2^-25 relative: a last-bit difference, caught by diff_sdkmath/diff_tier2. Unit-testable on Linux by emitting both forms and comparing them over random binary32 inputs.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.056-0.26 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). 4 x86 instructions x about 3 % of guest instructions x 0.85 elidable = 0.1 x86 per guest instruction, about 1.3 % of game-code instructions, plus about 6 cycles of latency removed from multiply chains; f = 1-2.5 %. Low = 0.625*0.01*8.9 = 0.056; high = 0.625*0.025*16.9 = 0.26 ms.

### 53. T-LEAF-INLINE: Tiny leaves cannot be inlined across shards

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: code layout, shard emission. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--inline-tiny-leaves`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Later' (inlining small leaf functions across shards); docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_TASK.md item 4
- **What:** Shards hold 64 address-ordered functions with extern definitions, and clang modules have no LTO (GALAXY_MODULE_ENABLE_LTCG applies only to MSVC). A call to a tiny leaf therefore pays call/ret, the pc store, the callee's prologue and epilogue, its entry loads and return stores, plus the caller's full sync. Example: Vec::operator= is 28 B of PowerPC and 1,792 B of x86.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:10888-10990, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:11342-11345, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7106-7124
- **Why paid:** Function-per-symbol sharding by address, with no cross-translation-unit optimisation in the clang build.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; sizes measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md; call share assumed): Once per call to a tiny leaf. Assumed 10-25% of calls.
- **Design:** - Emit leaves of at most 8-12 PowerPC instructions that are checkpoint-free, have no interior entries and are not address-taken as GALAXY_ALWAYS_INLINE bodies in a generated leaves.h (inside the PCH), operating on the caller's resident locals.
- Keep the out-of-line fn_X for indirect and runtime calls.
- Alternative: a CMake option for clang ThinLTO (-flto=thin with lld-link) if install-time memory and time allow.
- **Risk** (medium): Code growth and longer compile times on a 2-core installer. Semantic risk is low because the body is the same. Profile attribution moves into callers.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-0.5 ms/frame. 0.5 to 1.75% of guest instructions are calls to tiny leaves, each saving 30-40 x86 instructions: 0.15 to 0.7 x86 per guest instruction, i.e. 1.9 to 8.7% of game code. This overlaps T-PRESERVE-NONE and T-LEAF-CONTRACT, so the range is taken as 0.1 to 0.5 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms.

### 54. G-WORKINGSET: No working-set floor or locking: hot code and guest RAM can be trimmed and soft-faulted mid-frame on an 8 GB laptop

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: page residency. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_WORKING_SET_FLOOR_MB (0 = today)`. Already listed: False PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:540 (P8) lists the working-set size, not trimming or faults
- **What:** The 209 MB game module is pre-read with PrefetchVirtualMemory on a background thread, which fills the standby list. The first touch of each page in the simulation thread is still a soft fault (~0.5-1 us). Guest RAM is a pagefile-backed section. The process has no SetProcessWorkingSetSizeEx minimum and no VirtualLock (grep finds none). The working set reached 5.3 GB on a 16 GB laptop (P8). An i7-6500U typically has 8 GB, so Windows' working-set manager can trim hot code, guest RAM and the dispatch table, and they come back by soft or hard faults inside a frame.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:101-128, runtime/src/flat_guest_memory.cpp:71-87, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15897
- **Why paid:** Residency was handled only by prefetching (to stop disk faults at first use). Trimming under memory pressure was never addressed.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:540-546 (working set 5.3 GB, 8 GB target cannot hold it)): Unknown; it depends on memory pressure. Every soft fault costs ~0.5-1 us, a hard fault milliseconds.
- **Design:** GALAXY_WORKING_SET_FLOOR_MB, default 0 = today. At startup call SetProcessWorkingSetSizeEx with a soft minimum of guest RAM (88 MB) + dispatch table + the hot-code estimate (~32 MB after T-LINK-ORDER) + 64 MB. VirtualLock the host view of MEM1/MEM2 (locking one view keeps the shared physical pages resident) and the dispatch table. Add PageFaultCount deltas (GetProcessMemoryInfo) to the 10 s sampler and per-frame telemetry, so a laptop recording shows whether faults occur.
- **Risk** (low): A higher minimum working set takes memory from other apps on an 8 GB machine. VirtualLock fails beyond the minimum working set, so the floor must be set first and failure logged, not fatal. No privilege is needed: the 'Increase a process working set' right is granted to users by default.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0-0.3 ms/frame. 0 when there is no memory pressure. At 50-300 soft faults per second on hot pages x 0.5-1 us = 0.025-0.3 ms/s on average, plus rare hard-fault spikes of milliseconds that cost frames rather than average ms. The upper bound of 0.3 ms/frame assumes trimming bursts (~18,000 faults/s, about 300 per frame) after a memory-pressure event. The telemetry is needed to turn this into a measurement.

### 55. R-CLOCK: Runtime time-base reads: shared CAS clamp, per-read bookkeeping and repeated reads per host transition
