- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: clock / timeline. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_SIM_LOCAL_CLOCK (default 0 = shared CAS and per-read suspension sampling as today)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 14 'Clock reads 3.3 %'; PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P4 step 1 (one time-base read per body), P9 (GetThreadTimes cache); NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native15/16 'Clock reads' row. The thread-local clamp and moving suspension detection off the read path are new.
- **What:** Every runtime clock read goes through runtime_time_base_ticks: a relaxed lock-xadd census counter, update_native_audio_output_gate (static-guard test + compare), RuntimeTimeline::now_ticks (indirect call through source_.read to rdtsc, 128-bit multiply, acquire load of discarded_host_ticks_, then a compare_exchange on the process-shared last_ticks_ that almost always executes because time advances), a saturated() check, a GetThreadTimes refresh once per elapsed millisecond, and the host-suspension candidate bookkeeping. The same host transition reads the clock several times: checkpoint-body entry (timeline->now_ticks for the gap record), synchronize_decrementer_deadline (second read when DEC is armed and due), arbitrate_external_before_decrementer (VI inspect), cap_realtime_vi_wait_for_ai_dma, idle wait, quiet checkpoint, AI dispatch. The broker thread does 3 now_ticks per published event on the same last_ticks_ line, so the CAS line bounces between cores.
- **Where:** runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:488-534, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:37-39, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5465-5532, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5192-5206, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:184-201, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:22589-22629, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27794-27811, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5992-6029, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1968-1999, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:32432-32443
- **Why paid:** The timeline promises a cross-thread monotonic value (clamp in now_ticks) because broker, render and simulation threads all compare against the same deadlines; host-suspension recovery needs a CPU-time sample paired with wall time on every read; each runtime phase was written to sample fresh time independently (P4 step 1 only partly done: the body's entry sample is reused only for the 'DEC not yet due' test).
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md (56,000 clock reads/s, 9,600 by the game); GetThreadTimes 1 ms cache from native_runtime.cpp:5482-5493): 46,400 runtime reads/s (56,000 total minus 9,600 by the game) = ~770 per frame; GetThreadTimes at most 1,000/s
- **Design:** (1) Give the simulation thread a thread-local monotonic clamp: sim reads do rdtsc+mul+max(local_last) with no locked instruction, and publish the value with a relaxed store to a sim-owned slot; commit_guest_observation and cross-thread consumers take max(own, published). Keep the shared CAS only on the broker/render side. (2) Inline the TSC read (no source_.read indirect call when the source is TSC). (3) Move host-suspension candidate detection out of the per-read path: sample GetThreadTimes only at VI waits / idle waits (where a suspension can be distinguished) or on the broker. (4) Add a per-host-transition TickSample on RuntimeState (taken once at branch_checkpoint / call_guest_impl entry, invalidated when control returns to generated code or after a phase that can run long: VI dispatch, render submission, waits) and pass it to synchronize_decrementer_deadline, the VI inspect in arbitration, cap_realtime_vi_wait_for_ai_dma and AI dispatch.
- **Risk** (medium): A thread-local clamp lets the sim thread's value trail a broker-published deadline by the cross-core TSC skew (invariant TSC: tens of ns); inspect() may then see AwaitingPublication/NotDue for an already published edge and loop once more, never earlier delivery. commit_guest_observation throws on backward movement, so it must use the max form. A stale per-transition sample delays a deadline decision by the length of host work inside the transition (bounded by refreshing after VI/render/wait phases). Moving suspension detection can miss a suspension that ends inside guest code; keep the broker-side detection as backstop and validate with the laptop sleep/resume route.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.17-0.41 ms/frame. est_ms = share_of_sim_thread * T_sim_6500U, T = 8.9-16.9 ms (midpoint 12.9). Runtime share of the measured 3.3 % clock-read row = 3.3 % * 46,400/56,000 = 2.73 % -> 0.24-0.46 ms/frame (0.35 midpoint). Desktop cost per read = 3.3 % * 2.25 ms * 60 / 56,000 = 80 ns against 7 ns for a raw rdtsc (measured in the comment at native_runtime.cpp:5433-5436), so ~90 % of each read is overhead; assume 70-90 % removable -> 0.17-0.41 ms.

### 56. T-FCMP-FUSE: Float compares (fcmpu/fcmpo, cror) fully materialise FPSCR[FPCC] and the CR field

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: CR/XER handling, conditional branches. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--fuse-float-compares`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** fcmpu/fcmpo lower to compare_f64:
- two 64-bit NaN-range screens (movabs/and/cmp);
- two ucomisd plus a select;
- a read-modify-write of FPSCR FPCC;
- a CR-field read-modify-write.
The following bc then re-reads context->cr. The common 'fcmpo; cror 2,0,2; beq' (<=) pattern adds two cr_bit reads and a set_cr_bit. No fusion exists for float compares: fusible_compare_branches only matches Cmp/Cmpi/Cmpl/Cmpli.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5752-5762, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1640-1650, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5450-5476, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12700-12749
- **Why paid:** Exact PowerPC compare semantics (FPCC, the CR field, VXVC for fcmpo on NaN) are kept even in the fast-float module, although fast float already drops FPSCR status for arithmetic.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; instruction mix measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md; fcmp share assumed): Once per executed fcmp. The share is not separated in the measured mix (it sits inside float arithmetic 3.6% and system/CR logic 4.8%); assumed 0.4 to 1.0% of hot guest instructions.
- **Design:** Behind --fuse-float-compares, under --fast-float:
- Lower 'fcmp crF' followed by bc on F (optionally through one cror/crnor/creqv of bits of F) to a single C++ double comparison on context->fpr_bits: LT is a<b, GT is a>b, EQ is a==b, UN/SO is isunordered(a,b).
- Negated forms test !(...), which is true for NaN exactly as the CR bit is 0.
- Keep the CR write (and the fcmpo VXVC slow path) only where the field is live (same liveness as T-CR-LAZY).
- Drop the FPCC write, consistent with the fast-float rule that FPSCR status is not maintained.
- **Risk** (low): An error in NaN ordering would flip branches only for NaN inputs. A guest mffs that read FPCC after an fcmp would see a stale value; fast float already accepts FPSCR status loss. Validate with diff_sdkmath, diff_tier2 and frame dumps.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.24 ms/frame. Measured in scratchpad/micro: fcmpu+blt today is 27 hot x86 instructions (53 static, 207 B); fused it is 3 instructions (34 B). The cror form is 60 static. Calculation: 0.4 to 1.0% x 24 saved = 0.10 to 0.24 x86 per guest instruction, which is 1.2 to 3.0% of game code. est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms gives 0.10 to 0.24 ms; the low bound is widened to 0.05.

### 57. R-PSCONST: PS constants rebuilt and uploaded per material change; a new VA every time breaks batching

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: per-draw GX->D3D state (constants). Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_PS_CONSTANT_MEMO (0 = always rebuild)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT item 6 'Rebuild constants ... only when their inputs change' (generic)
- **What:** Whenever tev_dirty, tev_c_dirty or tex_dirty is set (including a texture-binding change alone), fill_ps_constants rebuilds all 768 bytes from the BP file (16 TEV stages, konst colors, fog, alpha, tex dims) and uploads them to a fresh constant-ring slot. Two draws with identical material constants then carry different ps_constants VAs, so draw_calls_batch_compatible rejects merging and the renderer issues another SetGraphicsRootConstantBufferView.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:10489-10506, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:2698-2858, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:10747-10776, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7438-7442
- **Why paid:** An immutable upload per change is simplest and always correct.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Code path; material change counts not measured): Per material change (dirty TEV/konst/texture). Alternating materials re-upload identical blocks.
- **Design:** Content-addressed reuse within the current ring segment. Keep a 64-slot direct-mapped memo keyed by (current_ps_hash_, a hash of the konst/TEV color/fog/alpha register words and the tex-dims words) that returns the VA of a block already uploaded in this segment. On a hit, skip both the fill and the upload, so equal materials share a VA and batch. Clear the memo when the segment advances (R-CHUNKFRAME makes it last a whole game frame).
- **Risk** (low): A memo key that omits an input of fill_ps_constants returns stale constants (wrong colors or fog). Derive the key from exactly the registers fill_ps_constants reads and add a debug mode that compares the 768 bytes on every hit.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.25 ms/frame. ~0.2-0.5 us per fill and upload on the 6500U x 150-500 redundant rebuilds per frame = 0.03-0.25 ms, plus fewer root CBV calls (driver share, measured 11.5 % on NVIDIA). Inside the 16 % state share (0.96-1.6 ms).

### 58. R-FASTMEM-FAULTS: Residual fastmem faults: exception dispatch and NtContinue per dynamic device access, costlier under KVA shadow on Skylake

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: OS / exception path. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--device-access-pcs <file>`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 8 (DI/IPC register emulation as host calls); the profile-guided interim flag is new
- **What:** Device accesses whose address the translator cannot prove constant (DI/IPC pointers held in structures) take a host page fault. Each one goes through the kernel, KiUserExceptionDispatcher, the vectored handler (decode, then GuestAddressSpace::fault_access), and NtContinue: two kernel transitions per access. The i7-6500U is Meltdown-affected, so Windows runs KVA shadow there, while the 9950X3D baseline does not. Each transition therefore costs relatively more than the PassMark ratio predicts. The same applies to every syscall in this ledger.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3093-3112, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:32815-32835
- **Why paid:** Fastmem modules map RAM flat and let rare device accesses fault. The static device-site analysis covers constant addresses only; dynamic ones still fault.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 8 row (about 65,000 handled faults per run; device reads/writes about 0.5 %)): About 65,000 handled faults per run, about 520/s if the run is 124 s
- **Design:** A translator flag --device-access-pcs <file> takes the guest PCs of the hot fault sites the runtime already prints ([fastmem] hot sites resolved to owning guest functions) and lowers those loads and stores to the checked helper. The translate.rs comment says a misclassified site is slower, never wrong. That is a one-generation fix ahead of plan item 8 (DVD/IOS/NAND/SC as host calls), which removes the accesses entirely.
- **Risk** (low): A PC that also touches RAM pays the checked path, a small slowdown, not a correctness problem. The profile-derived list must be regenerated if guest code layout changes (it is keyed by guest PC, which is stable for RMGE01).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.09-0.19 ms/frame. Device handling 0.5 % of the simulation thread measured: est_ms = 0.005 * 8.9 = 0.045 to 0.005 * 16.9 = 0.085 ms. The kernel fault path is not in that share: 520/s * 5-12 us under KVA shadow (hypothesis) = 2.6-6.2 ms/s = 0.043-0.10 ms/frame. Sum 0.09-0.19 ms/frame. The faults/s figure assumes a 124 s run.

### 59. G-EFBCOPY-CPU: Per-EFB-copy CPU: root-signature swap, full GX rebind, shared RTV rewrite, barriers and broken draw batching

- **Status:** open. Area: EFB-copy path (CPU). Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_EFB_COPY_LEAN_BINDING (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Every on_efb_copy does the following. It flushes the pending draw batch. A texture copy looks up and LRU-budgets efb_copy_dests_. record_conversion_blit issues about 17 D3D12 calls: a dest barrier, an EFB RT to SRV barrier, OMSetRenderTargets, RSSetViewports, RSSetScissorRects, SetGraphicsRootSignature(conv), SetPipelineState, 16 root constants, a descriptor table, topology, Draw, a barrier back, SetGraphicsRootSignature(GX) and bind_efb (3 more), then the dest barrier back. allocate_conversion_rtv re-creates the shared RTV whenever the destination differs from the previous copy's. invalidate_gx_bindings then forces the next draw to re-issue PSO, 2 CBVs, 2 tables, the palette SRV, topology, VB, IB, viewport and scissor (about 11 calls). Texture copies also mark alias bindings dirty, so the next draw rebuilds its texture descriptor table.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:12543-12760, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7650-7750, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7752-7827, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7829-8153, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5550-5565, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7301-7342
- **Why paid:** The conversion blit has its own root signature (s_conv_root_sig) and RTV slot, so it was written to restore the whole GX state afterwards. GX copies need EFB format conversion, filtering and gamma, so a draw is used rather than a plain copy.
- **Frequency** (measured; PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:503-509 ([efb-copy-work]: 50,820 shader copies in 255 s = 200/s; 76,134 in 9 min = 141/s; 2,694 identity copies in 255 s). XFB 1 per frame. The pointer-depth rate is derived from native_runtime.cpp:24292-24353.): About 5.5 conversion passes per frame: 141-200 shader copies/s (2.35-3.3 per frame), 60 XFB copies/s, 10.6 identity copies/s (0.18 per frame) and 60 pointer-depth conversions/s (G-PTRDEPTH).
- **Design:** (1) Fold the conversion root parameters into the GX root signature: a 16-DWORD root-constant slot plus reuse of an existing SRV table param. The copy then neither switches nor invalidates root signatures, and only PSO, RT, viewport and scissor are restored. Skip invalidate_gx_bindings and re-arm only bound_pipeline_, the viewport/scissor flags and the topology. (2) Pass both entry barriers in one ResourceBarrier(2) call and defer the EFB return barrier to the next GX draw or clear (lazy), so back-to-back copies (the bloom chains) share one EFB to SRV transition. (3) Give each EfbCopyDest its own RTV (an RTV heap of about 64 entries) so allocate_conversion_rtv stops rewriting the shared slot. Gate as GALAXY_EFB_COPY_LEAN_BINDING.
- **Risk** (medium): Missed state restoration (wrong RT or root constants bound after a copy) shows as black or garbled frames, and EFB/XFB dumps and the replay path (replay_recording_) must stay consistent. A lazy barrier left pending at end_frame or before present must be flushed, or the debug layer flags a state mismatch.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.15-0.4 ms/frame. Render-thread ms per frame = passes x calls x per-call cost: 5.5 x about 28 calls x 1-2 us (Intel UMD on the 6500U, hypothesis) = 0.15-0.31 ms, plus about 6 barriers x 5.5 x 2-3 us of UMD barrier tracking = 0.07-0.1 ms. As a share check: 0.15-0.4 / 8 ms = 2-5 % of the 6500U render thread. Lean binding removes an estimated 50-70 % of it.

### 60. G-UMA-UPLOAD: No UMA-aware resource path: staging copies for every texture, and WC upload heaps for all rings

- **Status:** open. Area: UMA upload path. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_UMA_UPLOAD (default 0)`. Already listed: False PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:532-535 mentions UPLOAD-heap vertex rings on iGPUs (already the case), not textures or custom heaps
- **What:** D3D12_FEATURE_ARCHITECTURE (UMA / CacheCoherentUMA) is never queried. adapter_is_unified_memory() exists but only sizes and logs rings. Every decoded texture is written to an UPLOAD staging buffer, a DEFAULT texture is created or pooled, and a CopyTextureRegion per mip plus two barriers are recorded. On the HD 520 that is a CPU write, a GPU read, and a GPU write to the same DRAM. The vertex, constant and matrix rings are WC UPLOAD memory. On Gen9's shared LLC, a CacheCoherentUMA custom heap (CPU_PAGE_PROPERTY_WRITE_BACK, MEMORY_POOL_L0) would let CPU writes stay in the 4 MB LLC for the GPU to read.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:2505-2566, runtime/src/gx/texture_cache.cpp:1660-1670, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:3075-3115, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:4657-4684, runtime/src/gx/post_process.cpp:454-514
- **Why paid:** The discrete-GPU upload pattern (staging plus copy) is the portable D3D12 default. UMA was only considered for memory reservation.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; No per-frame texture-upload count was found in the docs.): Per texture decode (streaming bursts, rate not measured), and per byte of ring data each frame.
- **Design:** At device creation query D3D12_FEATURE_DATA_ARCHITECTURE1. If UMA && CacheCoherentUMA: (1) create texture-cache textures on a CUSTOM heap (WRITE_BACK, L0) and have decode workers call WriteToSubresource per mip directly, dropping the staging allocation, the CopyTextureRegion and the COPY_DEST to SRV barrier. (2) Optionally move the constant and matrix rings (small, re-read by the GPU) to WRITE_BACK custom heaps. Keep the vertex ring WC, since it is streaming. Gate as GALAXY_UMA_UPLOAD.
- **Risk** (medium): WriteToSubresource on UNKNOWN-layout textures makes the driver swizzle on the CPU (decode workers), which may cost more CPU than the GPU copy saves, so measure first. Custom heaps are unsupported on some drivers, which needs a fallback. Gen9 reports CacheCoherentUMA, but this must be verified on the user's driver.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.5 ms/frame. Render thread plus GPU, per frame averaged: texture uploads in streaming scenes of about 0.2-2 MB per frame, as copy GPU time at 10-20 GB/s = 0.01-0.2 ms, plus per-texture recording (barriers, copies, SRV) at 10-30 us x 1-10 textures per frame = 0.01-0.3 ms. Spikes are higher at galaxy load.

### 61. H-PTMF: __ptmf_scall native uses checked loads and always crosses the runtime cached-call boundary

- **Status:** open. Area: host natives (native_pure_helper). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-ptmf`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** native_ptmf_scall_805173E0 replaces the CodeWarrior pointer-to-member call thunk, used for every `(obj->*pmf)()` that is not inlined (MR functors, member-function tables). It loads the PTMF triple and the vtable slot with guest_load_u32: region admit plus a trace_fileloader_stack gate per load. It then calls galaxy::call_guest_cached, which is services->call_guest_cached. That runtime path checks pending_transfer, runs the input-deadline service check, normalizes the address, takes the intercept decision (cached_call_has_required_entry_hook plus about 25 conditions in needs_call_guest_intercept) and does external-dispatch accounting. The direct dispatch table that bctrl uses is never consulted. At the caller, the helper is a Full residency sync point (no lean contract) and keeps a call-return checkpoint.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:15842-15879, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14127-14150, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30795-30870, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7830-7831, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:690-700, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:238-253
- **Why paid:** The helper was written before F19's direct dispatch table and the cooperative scheduler, when every indirect call had to reach the runtime for interrupt ownership. The runtime also lists 0x805173E0 in its cached-call set, for the trace_ptmf_targets diagnostic only (native_runtime.cpp:15993).
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; code: native_pure_helper maps 0x805173E0 and every `bl __ptmf_scall` lowers to this helper): Per non-inlined member-function-pointer call. Rate unknown; my assumption is 200-3,000 per frame.
- **Design:** Header-only change under translator flag --lean-ptmf (defines GALAXY_LEAN_PTMF in functions.h). Loads use fast::load through flat_base under GALAXY_FLAT_VIEW_ONLY_ACTIVE, and the tail becomes fast::call_guest_indirect(services, target, &cache..., 0x80517404u): the direct table entry when one is published and no event is pending, otherwise the same audited boundary as today. Optionally, when the target is a translated leaf, the translator could resolve constant PTMFs statically, but that is out of scope here.
- **Risk** (low): Same contract as bctrl through the direct table: a withheld or null entry, a pending event or an active trace still takes call_guest_cached. The one behavioural difference is that input-report servicing at this boundary no longer happens on warm direct hits. That is the same as for bctrl today, which relies on the next checkpoint.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.25 ms/frame. Assumes 200-3,000 calls per frame x a saving of 0.1-0.3 us each on the 6500U. The saving is the runtime cached path versus a table load and two atomic loads, scaled 2.8x from the desktop by the PassMark ratio 4,739/1,686: 0.02-0.9 ms, capped at 0.25 ms because the whole runtime-call bucket is 0.16-0.30 ms (1.8 %, measured).

### 62. R-DIRTY-DRAIN: drain_guest_memory_writes: an unconditional locked exchange on ~704 summary words per submission, then per-32-byte-bit range building

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: guest write drain / dirty tracking. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_DIRTY_DRAIN_SPARSE (default 0)`. Already listed: True docs/PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P9 table (drain_guest_memory_writes); docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md (guest write drain 1.0 %)
- **What:** Each render_frame submission drains the renderer's dirty bitmap: 32-byte pages over 320 MiB, with one summary word per 64 bitmap words (128 KB). The loop runs dirty_word_summary_[group].exchange(0) unconditionally on every MEM1 and MEM2 group: 192 + 512 = 704 locked XCHGs per drain, even when the group is clean. For each set word it walks bit by bit, building ranges one 32-byte page at a time, then sorts and coalesces. Every one of the 5-8 submissions per frame repeats this.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:4851-4952, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:4954-4995, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5610-5612, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/gx_backend.h:1180-1185, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:5307-5321
- **Why paid:** Flush-based coherency (F4): dcbf/dcbst, DMA and gather bursts publish lines, and the render thread needs the ranges, in FIFO order, to invalidate textures and display lists. The 32-byte page matches the cache line, so texture invalidation stays exact.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md (guest write drain 1.0 %); docs/PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P9 (drain_guest_memory_writes 780 samples)): Once per non-empty or empty submission: ~5 streamed chunks + 1-2 boundary chunks per frame. Measured as 1.0 % of simulation-thread samples.
- **Design:** (1) Load the summary word first and exchange only when it is non-zero. This is safe for the same reason as the existing zero-word skip at 4918-4922: a producer that sets the bit afterwards is caught by the next drain. (2) Add a third level, one bit per summary word (11 words for 704 groups), so a drain touches only dirty groups. (3) Build ranges per run inside a word with countr_zero/countr_one, and emit a full 2 KB range for an all-ones word, instead of one iteration per bit. (4) Together with R-FIFO-RING-MIRROR, the 2-4k FIFO-ring bits per frame disappear.
- **Risk** (low): Memory-order argument identical to the existing code. A bug in the run builder would drop a dirty range and cause stale textures, so it needs a unit test against the old bitwise drain on random bitmaps; the C++ backend tests run on Windows only.
- **Estimate** (measured): 0.09-0.17 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.010 x 8.9..16.9 ms (midpoint 0.13 ms), measured share (CPU_GAP_REPORT). Derived check: 704 XCHG x ~22 cycles / 3.0 GHz = 5.2 us per drain x 6-8 drains = 31-41 us/frame for the summary scan alone. The design should remove 60-80 % of the cost.

### 63. G-CHUNK-SUBMIT: Each streamed FIFO chunk is a full command-list submission: allocator reset, heaps, root signature, timestamps, ExecuteCommandLists and Signal

- **Status:** open. Area: Intel driver / kernel submission. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_COALESCE_CHUNK_SUBMITS (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** render_frame_on_thread performs begin_frame and end_frame for every chunk. Per chunk that means allocator Reset, list Reset, SetDescriptorHeaps, SetGraphicsRootSignature, invalidate_gx_bindings, 2 EndQuery and a ResolveQueryData, Close, ExecuteCommandLists, Signal, a nonblocking fence poll, the readback Map/Unmap, and a possible slot-fence wait. On Intel, each ExecuteCommandLists goes through the UMD batch-buffer build and the KMD/dxgkrnl submission path, and every chunk starts with a full rebind of GX state.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:8250-8263, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:9012-9045, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5728-5806, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:6286-6327
- **Why paid:** Mid-frame streaming lets the render thread start while the simulation is still producing the frame. pe_events_at_consumption delivers PE events at submission (render_config.h:215-222).
- **Frequency** (derived; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:128: 16,778 streamed submissions in a 124 s profile = 135/s = 2.25 per frame at 60 fps (assumes one chunk per successful streaming attempt).): About 3.25 submissions per game frame (2.25 streamed + 1).
- **Design:** Coalesce: keep the command list open across streamed chunks and close and submit only at (a) the display copy or present, (b) a PE token or finish whose event needs consumption semantics, or (c) about every 4 ms of recorded work, so the GPU still starts early. Deliver at-consumption PE events after recording plus texture_cache_.finish_pending_decodes(), which already completes every guest read, instead of after ExecuteCommandLists. Gate as GALAXY_COALESCE_CHUNK_SUBMITS.
- **Risk** (medium): Later GPU start reduces CPU/GPU overlap on a GPU-bound frame, which works against G-PRESENT-SERIAL, so the 4 ms cap is needed. PE event delivery moves relative to submission, and the GXDrawDone ordering tests must still pass.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.12-0.4 ms/frame. Render-thread ms per frame = submissions x cost: 3.25 x (ExecuteCommandLists plus Signal 25-80 us on the 6500U under Intel KMD, plus begin/end bookkeeping and rebind 10-40 us) = 0.11-0.39 ms. Coalescing to 1.5 submissions per frame saves about half.

### 64. R-CKPT-BODY: Checkpoint bodies run as periodic polls of sources that do not publish into the event mask

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: checkpoint body / scheduler. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_EVENT_DRIVEN_CHECKPOINTS (default 0 = count-based polls as today)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Checkpoint bodies 2.6 %' and plan item 5; PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P4. Folding device levels into the mask and the early exit before FullScope are not written down there.
- **What:** branch_checkpoint enters a FullScope (gate unpublish/republish), toggles the adaptive call-return flag, runs record_* diagnostics, then branch_checkpoint_body: a clock read and gap record, ~20 latch/level loads, poll_native_dsp twice (preflight and again inside publish_next_inline_checkpoint_slow), synchronize_decrementer_deadline, the fast-return decision over VI/input/DEC/IPC/DSP/PE/PE-scan/AI-probe/host-pump, and publish_next_inline_checkpoint_slow, which re-tests the same sources to compute the next watermark. Most bodies find nothing to do: they are scheduled by count-based polls (realtime_vi_poll_checkpoints = 2048 counts, host pump every 4096 counts whose message pump is now a no-op, AI fallback probe every 4*2048 counts while audio plays) or by level-triggered sources (PE finish/token, PE scan pending, DSP/ARAM/AI/IPC interrupt lines) that are not part of pending_event_mask and therefore can only be seen by polling.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:28805-29138, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27784-28803, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:28310-28385, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27278-27415, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5262-5268, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5334-5340, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:17366-17390, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:18151-18176, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5537-5540, runtime/src/checkpoint_gate.cpp:167-212
- **Why paid:** Before the deadline broker and the cooperative scheduler, checkpoints were the only clock; the count-based polls remain as 'broker-health' fallbacks and as the only way to observe device levels raised by the render thread (PE), the IOS/DSP emulation and AI DMA without a latch bit. Plan item 5 (checkpoint-free generation) was meant to remove this and has not started.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md rates (9,400 checkpoint bodies/s, package run with Lightweight recording on)): 9,400 bodies/s = ~157 per frame
- **Design:** Make the body event-driven, keeping the counter test as the only inline cost: (1) every level producer publishes a latch bit when it raises its level (PE finish/token and PE-scan-pending from the render/sim GX path, DSP/ARAM/AI/IPC interrupt lines from native_host, quit request); (2) with all sources in the mask, raise the periodic watermark from 2048 to a broker-health value (e.g. 1<<20 counts) and drop the host-pump and AI-fallback count schedules (the broker publishes AI deadlines; coop_idle already tests AI by time); (3) add a first-line exit in branch_checkpoint: if mask == 0 and the watermark expired, bump next_inline_checkpoint_slow and return before FullScope, adaptive-flag toggling and diagnostics; (4) drop the second poll_native_dsp in publish (host JAudio mixer: the DSP is off).
- **Risk** (medium): A level producer that forgets to publish its bit would now be noticed only at the broker-health interval, delaying an interrupt (PE token -> frame pacing, IPC reply -> DVD reads stall, AI -> audio underrun). Mitigate by keeping the old 2048 poll under the env flag and asserting in a Detailed recording that no body ever finds a level whose bit was not set. AI-DMA lateness and VI lateness (frame-cadence diagnostics) are the acceptance metrics.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.12-0.37 ms/frame. Measured share 2.6 % -> est_ms = 0.026 * 8.9-16.9 = 0.23-0.44 ms/frame. Desktop cost per body = 2.6 % * 135 ms/s / 9,400 = 373 ns. The fraction of bodies scheduled by polls rather than real events is not measured (the cadence diagnostics' limiting_cause_mask would give it); assume 50-85 % removable -> 0.12-0.37 ms.

### 65. H-MTMSR-QUIET: OSEnableInterrupts/OSRestoreInterrupts: mfence plus a runtime callback plus a clock read on every interrupt re-enable

- **Status:** open. Area: OS interrupt natives (mtmsr lowering). Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--inline-interrupt-enable / GALAXY_PUBLISH_DEADLINE_TSC=1`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Every `mtmsr` lowers to `context->msr = rS; std::atomic_thread_fence(seq_cst); galaxy::architectural_interrupt_checkpoint(...)`. When EE is set, which is every OSEnableInterrupts and every OSRestoreInterrupts(enabled), the helper calls services->branch_checkpoint. The runtime's branch_checkpoint first builds a RuntimeBoundarySelfScope, a ScopedCallbackProfile, the function-async and input-trace observers, a ScopedBranchCheckpointIdentity and home-button RSO recording. Only then does it reach the 'quiet' test at 0x804A8104 or 0x804A8128. That test calls runtime_time_base_ticks (a full clock read: census atomic, timeline CAS, CPU-time cache, suspension-candidate logic), inspects the VI deadline consumer and polls seven device-pending flags. Residency also treats the call as a Full sync point.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5258-5266, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14904-14935, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:28805-28860, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:28983-29032, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5465-5560
- **Why paid:** mtmsr enabling EE is an interrupt-recognition point. A latched VI, DEC, AI, HID or PE event must be delivered when the game re-enables interrupts, and the inline gate cannot test absolute deadlines because it has no clock, so the decision is made in the runtime. The quiet path (Native21) removed the full body but kept the transition and the clock read.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native21 'Quiet interrupt re-enable' census (2.56 M per 90 s at 0x804A8128); quiet_architecture_checkpoint_returns counter at native_runtime.cpp:29029): Before the quiet path, 0x804A8128 alone made 2.56 M full callbacks in 90 s (about 28,400 per second, measured, Native21). Rough rate now: 250-500 per frame (assumed 15,000-30,000 per second); the quiet-return count is printed but not in the docs.
- **Design:** Translator flag --inline-interrupt-enable, for the three SDK bodies OSDisableInterrupts 0x804A80E8, OSEnableInterrupts 0x804A80FC and OSRestoreInterrupts 0x804A8110 (or every mtmsr). Emit msr = rS and no fence (one simulation thread; the event mask is read with acquire). If EE goes from 0 to 1, run an inline test against module globals published like --checkpoint-globals: *pending_event_mask != 0, or __rdtsc() >= g_module_next_deadline_tsc, or *counter >= *next_slow. Only a hit calls branch_checkpoint. Runtime GALAXY_PUBLISH_DEADLINE_TSC=1 converts min(VI, DEC, AI-DMA, HID deadline) to a raw TSC value. It refreshes that value wherever the broker arms or replaces a deadline and wherever the DEC is rewritten, and sets it to 0 (always due) while any condition the quiet test rejects is active: an external dispatch, servicing input, a pending VI boundary, an active sync capture.
- **Risk** (medium): If a deadline publication is missed, an event is delivered late: at the next scheduler point or checkpoint instead of at the interrupt re-enable. VI or AI-DMA lateness would then show as audio lateness or VI cadence jitter. Mitigation: publish 0 whenever unsure, and compare [idle-loop-sleep] and AI-DMA lateness counters against today. Removing the seq_cst fence is safe only because the simulation thread is the sole writer of the context and the event latch uses its own atomics.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.08-0.4 ms/frame. Clock read cost derived from measurements: 3.3 % x 12.9 ms = 0.43 ms per frame over 56,000/60 = 933 reads per frame, so 0.46 us per read on the 6500U. Per quiet return: 0.46 us plus 0.15-0.5 us of callback entry, scopes, VI inspect and the mfence (about 33 cycles), so 0.6-1.0 us. Times 250-500 per frame gives 0.15-0.5 ms; I take 0.08-0.4 ms to allow lower current rates. This overlaps the measured 'clock reads 3.3 %' and 'checkpoint bodies 2.6 %' rows and must not be added to them.

### 66. T-HOST-PROLOGUE: Host callee-saved register pushes and pops in every translated function

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: function entry/exit (host ABI). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--preserve-none-calls`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'Function entry and exit ~40: 8 host register pushes and pops' (cost only)
- **What:** Each translated function is an external C++ function with the default host calling convention. The compiled synthetic body pushed 6 registers plus an alignment slot and popped them (SysV). The gap report counts 8 pushes and pops on Win64, where rdi, rsi and xmm6-15 are also callee-saved. Values live across calls are mostly just context, memory, services and flat_guest_base, because residency syncs at every call.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:10206-10221, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3518-3531
- **Why paid:** NativeGameFunction is a plain function pointer type shared with the runtime and the dispatch table, so every body uses the platform ABI. Functions are non-static across 64-function shards, so nothing is inlined across shards.
- **Frequency** (derived; compiled synthetic body; CPU_GAP_REPORT entry/exit row): Once per translated call (entry and exit): 14-16 push/pop instructions plus 3 argument moves.
- **Design:** Compile translated-to-translated calls with a cheaper convention. Use clang's `__attribute__((preserve_none))` on the generated fn_* (and a thin default-ABI thunk per function record for the runtime and the dispatch table), so a callee saves nothing and the caller spills only what is actually live (context, memory, services, flat base). Alternatively make shards static with a cross-shard header of `[[gnu::always_inline]]` candidates. Measure first by compiling one shard both ways and counting .text and instructions.
- **Risk** (medium): Toolchain dependency: preserve_none needs clang 19+, and Windows x64 support must be verified for clang-cl. The runtime's calls through NativeGameFunction must go through the thunks, or the stack and registers are corrupted. Unwind info for SEH (fastmem fault handler) must remain valid.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.06-0.42 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G. 14-16 push/pop per call against ~50-75 call-machinery instructions per call is about 10 % of call instructions, but push/pop run on the stack engine near-free, so f = 1-4 %: 0.01 x 5.56 = 0.06; 0.04 x 10.56 = 0.42.

### 67. T-FLATVIEW-STMW-DCBZ: Checked stmw (rS <= 24), dcbz and null-view tests in lean bodies

- **Status:** done on branch (item 1). Area: Translator: memory access. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--flat-view-only`. Already listed: True docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_TASK.md 1.3; docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS.md 1.3
- **What:** Three sites never used flat_base:
- stmw with rS <= 24 called guest_store_multiple_gprs: trace gates, overlap test, resolve, and a full residency sync point;
- dcbz called guest_zero: resolve plus a byte loop;
- inlined lean bodies null-tested the view and fell back to resolve_guest.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3991-4030, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4454-4470, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:256-275
- **Why paid:** These sites predate generation 25's flat_base assumption.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; per-sequence costs measured, docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS.md 1.3; dynamic frequency not measured): Once per stmw (rS <= 24), dcbz, or inlined TVec3/string/GD body.
- **Design:** --flat-view-only: per-register stores through the view, fast::zero_line, and assumed_flat_view in lean bodies.
- **Risk** (low): stmw loses diagnostic trace gates. An access outside RAM faults into the vectored handler, exactly as a translated store does.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.2 ms/frame. stmw r22 drops from 75 to 33 instructions, dcbz from 28 to 17, TVec3 += from 51 to 20 (measured, TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS 1.3); probe stmw_low drops from 122 to 50 dynamic instructions (this session). The share of hot instructions is assumed at 0.3-1.5%, about 0.4-2.5% of game code, giving 0.03 to 0.2 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms.

### 68. T-SDA-CONST: r2/r13 (SDA bases) are treated as ordinary registers: loaded at entry and reloaded after every call

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: register residency. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--constant-sda-bases`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** r13 (_SDA_BASE_) and r2 (_SDA2_BASE_) are constant for the whole program after __init_registers, and discover_device_pointer_globals already relies on that. Residency still declares gr13/gr2 locals loaded from context at entry, reloads them after every Sync::Full call in functions that use SDA globals, and computes `gr13 + disp` addresses at run time instead of absolute constants.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:2995-3030, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:665-686, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:620-628
- **Why paid:** Nothing in the translator specializes r2/r13. Correctness only needs that no executed code changes them, which nobody has proven statically.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; code reading; SDA access frequency not counted): Per call in functions that read r13/r2 (one reload each), plus one entry load and one add per SDA access.
- **Design:** Census pass in the translator: every instruction that writes r2 or r13 in main.dol. Expected: __init_registers, plus OSLoadContext-style restores of the same value. If the only writers are those, emit `constexpr std::uint32_t kSda = 0x...` and replace gpr[13]/gpr[2] reads with the constant in game functions (not in the writers, exception vectors or RSO sidecars). Add a one-time runtime assert after __init_registers that context r2/r13 equal the constants.
- **Risk** (low): If a debugger stub, exception path or RSO module sets a different r13, SDA accesses go to the wrong address. The census plus the boot assert catches it before any game code runs. The Home Button RSO uses main's SDA, so keep it on context anyway.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.21 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G. 1-3 instructions per call or SDA access in r13-using functions, assumed 0.3-2 % of game-code time: 0.003 x 5.56 = 0.02; 0.02 x 10.56 = 0.21.

### 69. R-RENDERWAIT: Render thread polls on a 1 ms timed condvar wait instead of waiting on the fence event

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: waiting / synchronization. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_EVENT_WAIT (0 = today's 1 ms condvar polling)`. Already listed: False The ~10 % wait share is measured in CPU_GAP_REPORT; no design is listed
- **What:** While idle_present is set or a pointer-depth or async peek field is pending, render_thread_main parks with queue_cv_.wait_for(1 ms) and loops through poll_completed_frame_effects, poll_completed_pointer_depth_capture and poll_async_peek_fields (each taking queue_mutex_ and building a 'ready' vector). That is a timed wakeup up to 1,000 times per second plus mutex/condvar traffic contending with the simulation thread's queue_space_cv_ waits. On 2 cores / 4 threads the spinning half of std::condition_variable/SRW waits steals the HT sibling that the simulation thread or driver needs.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7160-7200, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7792-7808, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:6368-6410
- **Why paid:** GPU completion (peek fields, pointer depth) is only observed by polling a fence value at the top of the worker loop.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT render-thread table: waiting/condvar spin ~10 %): Up to 1 kHz while any peek or pointer depth is pending (8,688 depth peeks per run on the title route, NATIVE_EXECUTION:337-339); measured 'waiting, condition-variable spin ~10 %' of render running samples.
- **Design:** Wait on Win32 objects. The worker blocks in WaitForMultipleObjects on {queue event (auto-reset, set by render_frame after the push), fence event (SetEventOnCompletion for the oldest pending peek/pointer-depth/frame-effects fence)}, with a timeout only for idle_present pacing. Replace the 1 ms predicate loop and keep std::condition_variable only for the producer-side queue_space wait, or move it to WaitOnAddress on the queue count to avoid the SRW spin.
- **Risk** (medium): A missed SetEventOnCompletion registration (e.g. a new pending fence added after the wait began) delays peek and pointer-depth delivery until the next queue push, causing pointer depth lag or late PE events. Re-register on every new pending item, and cap the timeout at ~4 ms as a guard.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.4 ms/frame. Measured 10 % of render running samples = 0.6-1.0 ms (0.10 x 6..10 ms), but most of it is legitimate waiting. Assume 10-40 % is spin and timed-wake overhead that event waits remove: 0.06-0.4 ms, plus HT-sibling relief for the simulation thread (not counted).

### 70. G-PTRDEPTH: A full 640x528 pointer-depth field is converted and read back every scene in keyboard/mouse mode

- **Status:** open. Area: EFB-copy path / pointer depth. Thread: other. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_POINTER_DEPTH_WINDOW (default 0 = full field, today's behaviour)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** With GALAXY_NATIVE_MOUSE_POINTER_FRAME=1 in KeyboardMouse mode, each scene boundary (GXSetDrawSync at 0x804B9EC8 from LR 0x80385ADC) attaches a pointer-depth capture. The render thread records, inside the frame's command list, an EFB depth to SRV transition (HiZ resolve), a full-screen conversion draw into a 640x528 RGBA8 target (1.35 MB), a copy of that to a READBACK ring (another 1.35 MB), and a GX state restore through invalidate_gx_bindings and bind_efb. The game reads one Z value under the pointer.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:24290-24353, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:8252-8289, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:8297-8371, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:8389-8404, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:6584-6605, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7469-7494, installer/runtime-env.json:175-178
- **Why paid:** Star-pointer picking needs depth under the cursor. The asynchronous in-frame field replaced a FIFO split that cost 2.4 ms of simulation per frame on the 185H (native_runtime.cpp:24342-24344). A full field lets any later pointer position be answered.
- **Frequency** (derived; native_runtime.cpp:24292-24353 runs per scene when the mouse mode and pointer frame are active.): Once per scene, about 60/s in gameplay with mouse input (frequency not measured).
- **Design:** Capture a 128x128 window centred on the latest host pointer position (scaled to EFB), with a full-field fallback when the pointer moved more than 48 px since the capture. Conversion and readback shrink by about 20x. Alternatively, skip the capture when no depth query consumed the previous field (frame.ready unused) and the pointer is outside the window or idle for more than 0.5 s. Gate as GALAXY_POINTER_DEPTH_WINDOW (0 = full field).
- **Risk** (medium): A fast pointer sweep could query outside the captured window. The fallback must then answer from the guest query path or the previous full field, never a wrong Z, or picking errors appear (star bits not collected, pointer hover wrong).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-0.35 ms/frame. GPU: a conversion of 338k px at about 10 ops plus a 1.35 MB write is 0.03-0.08 ms; the readback copy of 1.35 MB at 15-25 GB/s read plus write is 0.1-0.18 ms; the HiZ resolve plus re-entry is 0.02-0.06 ms. Render-thread CPU: about 22 calls plus about 11 rebinds at 1-2 us = 0.03-0.07 ms. Total 0.1-0.35 ms per frame.

### 71. H-JUT-RANGE: The whole 0x8041xxxx range (413 JKR/JSupport/JUTility functions) is forced through the host boundary

- **Status:** open. Area: host-call list / direct dispatch withheld entries. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--direct-jut-range / GALAXY_DIRECT_DISPATCH_JUT_RANGE=1`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** requires_host_guest_call returns true for every target with (target >> 16) == 0x8041. In metadata/rmge01/functions.csv that range holds 413 functions: the tail of JKR archive code (0x8041071C RARC mount), JUTVideo (pre/post retrace 0x80419308/0x80419554), JUTResFont and J2D-style drawing (inline GXPosition3f32 copies at 0x8041B214 and 0x8041F300), and the JSupport lists. Every direct `bl` into the range goes through call_guest_resolved and the 162 KB call_guest_impl_body, and the runtime withholds every callable entry in the range from the direct dispatch table. Only one functional handler exists in the range (0x8041071C); the range rule has no other consumer than a log line.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7875-7877, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15825-15827, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:16016-16018, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:24811-24819, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15869-15919, metadata/rmge01/functions.csv
- **Why paid:** History. Under the exception-transfer scheduler, JUTVideo's retrace callbacks ran inside the emulated VI dispatch, and the range was routed to the host so that 'any JUT-layer call made from inside the VI retrace dispatch' could be logged. Today the only effect is a std::cout line under trace_runtime_scaffolding() (native_runtime.cpp:24813-24818). needs_call_guest_intercept intercepts the range only while dispatching_vi_retrace, while the translator routes it statically at every call.
- **Frequency** (derived; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md F19 (326,540 published / 3,331 withheld); metadata/rmge01/functions.csv count of 0x8041 functions = 413; explicit list count from native_runtime.cpp:15828-15860 = 111): Withheld entries: about 3,200 of the 3,331 recorded at F19. Derived: 3,331 withheld minus about 111 explicit addresses is about 3,220, which matches 413 functions x 7.8 callable entries per function (326,540 published entries / 41,941 functions). Calls per frame into the range: unknown; my assumption is 100-2,000 (JUTResFont glyph draws, JSUPtrList link and unlink, JUTVideo).
- **Design:** Translator flag --direct-jut-range: drop the range rule and keep 0x8041071C as an explicit entry, since it is a hard-fail assertion and only called at archive mount. Runtime GALAXY_DIRECT_DISPATCH_JUT_RANGE=1 (default 0, today's behaviour): direct_dispatch_target_requires_host stops withholding the range, and needs_call_guest_intercept only intercepts it while trace_runtime_scaffolding() is on. The '[direct-dispatch] withheld=' count at boot should fall by about 3,200.
- **Risk** (low): With the flag, nothing in the runtime changes behaviour for these addresses except the VI-callback trace line. If a future runtime hook is added inside 0x8041xxxx, it has to be listed explicitly, or it would be silently bypassed by direct calls. Under the cooperative scheduler, the VI callbacks run as interrupts-as-calls on the current fiber, so no transfer protocol depends on the boundary. Check: the Home menu and gameplay routes keep the same flag-return counts.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.2 ms/frame. Assumes 100-1,000 calls per frame at 0.25-0.6 us of saved boundary each on the 6500U: about 0.025-0.6 ms, capped by the measured 'calls through the runtime' bucket of 0.16-0.30 ms (1.8 % x 8.9-16.9 ms). I take 0.02-0.2 ms, shared with H-TRACEONLY inside that bucket. Indirect calls into the range additionally return to the direct table: about 22 memory ops plus a runtime boundary each, which this range does not count.

### 72. R-WGPIPE-BATCH-SLOWPATH: Proven WGPIPE store runs go through an indirect write_device call and the full host write_wgpipe_bytes path

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: gather pipe. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--wgpipe-inline-batches`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** For runs of 2-16 consecutive stores proven to target the pipe, the translator builds a std::array from context->gpr[] and calls guest_write_wgpipe_bytes. That makes an indirect call through memory->write_device. GuestAddressSpace::write_device decodes the address and calls write_wgpipe_bytes, which tests four diagnostic flags, recomputes wgpipe_feeds_live_fifo() (two validity tests plus cpu_fifo_is_live) up to three times, goes through a switch on the size, may drain, and calls maybe_stream_gx_fifo. The total is roughly 80-120 instructions plus call overhead, more than the in-place scalar append it replaced (~20 per store). Hand-written GX natives (GXLoadPosMtxImm and others) also enter this path through write_gx_fifo_bytes.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3298-3358, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:5775-5806, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:13520-13538, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:12086-12155, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:11524-11531, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:11993-11998
- **Why paid:** The batch emitter predates the 4 KB in-place gather buffer (F21 batch, ABI 26). It was written when every pipe store was a device call, and the 'fail closed' contract made write_device the single owner.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Static site count from the native_api.h:5735-5745 comment; dynamic count unknown): Per executed batch: 117 static sites (native_api.h:5739 comment) plus the GX immediate-load natives. Executions per frame are unmeasured.
- **Design:** Emit wgpipe_put_bytes<N>(memory, bytes) inline. It does one bound check against the published gather buffer (or the cursor/limit from R-WGPIPE-STORE-INLINE), a fixed-size memcpy and a count update, then the flush callback when full. It falls back to write_device only when the buffer is unpublished or lacks room. Build the bytes from the resident register locals rather than context->gpr[], so no register-file sync is forced before the run. Make write_gx_fifo_bytes in the host use the same in-place append.
- **Risk** (low): The byte order of the run must be preserved, and the run must not be split across a flush with diagnostics on. The existing diff_gx harness (bench/diff_gx.cpp) covers gather-pipe bytes.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.2 ms/frame. About 60-100 instructions saved per batch (~8-13 ns at 2.5 IPC, 3 GHz) x an assumed 1k-15k batch executions per frame = 0.01-0.2 ms. A [wgpipe-batch] counter is needed first.

### 73. R-DIAG: Diagnostic records and trace gates on every checkpoint, runtime call and clock read

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime + generated-code headers: diagnostics. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_HOT_DIAGNOSTICS (runtime, default 1 = records on as today); --no-trace-gates (translator)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 14 (F18 'compile-time removal of remaining runtime trace gates'); PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P4 step 3
- **What:** Release runs still execute, per checkpoint callback: RuntimeBoundarySelfScope, ScopedCallbackProfile, full_checkpoint counters, trace_checkpoint_hotspots/time gates, observe_function_async_causality_marker, observe_native_input_trace_checkpoint, record_home_button_rso_checkpoint, cadence ScopedPhaseTimer, record_vi_service_checkpoint, record_inline_checkpoint_callback, ~14 mark_inline_checkpoint_stage calls in body and publish, route-marker probes, run_timed_checkpoint_phase gate per phase, record_checkpoint_gap; per runtime call: record_call (pc_history ring, trace_guest_call), record_vi_post_rfi_handoff x2, frame_activity_recording_enabled, cached_call_diagnostics_enabled; per clock read: census lock-xadd. Module-side helpers (call_guest_resolved, call_guest_direct_resolved, call_guest_cached) test function-local 'static const bool enabled = []{getenv_s...}()' gates (24 in native_api.h) whose thread-safe-static guard is checked on every call.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:28805-28980, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:29071-29092, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:3565-3713, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:2994-3003, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:4266-4310, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:28182-28266, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:2029-2041, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:13339-13408, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:13843-13880, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14119-14143
- **Why paid:** Diagnostics were added to find cadence and RFI bugs and kept compiled in so a user's recording can turn them on without a separate build; Native17 converted 68 runtime gates to constant-initialized atomics, but the records themselves and the module-side gates remain.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Diagnostic records, gates 0.9 %'): per checkpoint callback (9,400 bodies/s plus quiet callbacks), per runtime-routed call, per clock read
- **Design:** Runtime: one hot-path diagnostics word on RuntimeState (set at start from the Detailed-recording flag and the trace env vars); every record_* and mark_* call and the scope objects test it first ([[unlikely]]) so the release path is one predictable branch per callback, and move record_checkpoint_gap/record_vi_service_checkpoint behind it (P4 step 3). Translator: a define in functions.h (GALAXY_MODULE_NO_TRACE_GATES) that compiles the module-side trace helpers to constant false; diagnostic builds keep the old module.
- **Risk** (low): Recordings made with the gates compiled out lose those traces; the failure-only rings (VI service, post-RFI handoff) are what a cadence failure report prints, so they must stay on whenever the Lightweight recording is on. No guest-visible behaviour changes.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.06-0.15 ms/frame. Measured share 0.9 % -> est_ms = 0.009 * 8.9-16.9 = 0.08-0.15 ms/frame; assume 70-100 % removable -> 0.06-0.15 ms. The measured runs had Lightweight recording on, so normal play may be at the low end.

### 74. R-IDLE-WAIT: Idle-loop wait: Win32 wait and timer syscalls and a cross-thread SetEvent per idle period

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: timing / OS. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_BROKER_COALESCE_US (default 0)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md runtime items (idle wait 2.2 %); PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11 P5
- **What:** idle_loop_wait_for_next_deadline does up to 6 device-pending tests and 2 steady_clock::now() calls. It may call SetWaitableTimer (skipped when keep-armed matches the target) and CancelWaitableTimer, then a seq_cst idle_waiting store and WaitForMultipleObjects(event, timer). The broker wakes it with SetEvent (PendingEventLatch::wake_idle_waiters). Each idle period costs at least 2 kernel transitions and 2 context switches across cores.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:18742-18905, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:860-879
- **Why paid:** The guest idle loop (SelectThread with nothing runnable) must not spin. Device edges come from the broker thread, so a kernel-object wake is required. WaitOnAddress was rejected because its timeouts are quantized to 16-33 ms (runtime_timeline.h:371-374).
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md rates (450 idle waits/s); idle wait 2.2 % of simulation-thread running samples): 450 idle waits/s (about 7.5 per frame)
- **Design:** Reduce the wake count rather than the per-wake cost. (a) Broker coalescing window: when the next deadline is within a per-kind tolerance of another (input report 1 ms, AI DMA 0.5 ms; VI and DEC 0), fire once at the later one and publish both in one wake. (b) Remove the two steady_clock reads per wait: reuse now_ticks and the timeline tick at return. (c) The biggest source is the guest's 500 Hz BTU alarm (DEC); plan item 9 (WPAD/KPAD host calls) removes it.
- **Risk** (medium): Coalescing delays an input report by up to 1 ms (a latency change of 0-1 ms) and an AI DMA service by up to 0.5 ms, inside its buffer slack. Exact periodic identity checks in publish_periodic must still see in-order sequences.
- **Estimate** (measured): 0.04-0.37 ms/frame. Measured 2.2 % of the simulation thread. est_ms = share * thread_ms_6500U: 0.022 * 8.9 = 0.20 ms, 0.022 * 12.9 = 0.28 ms, 0.022 * 16.9 = 0.37 ms. The profiler inflates syscall rows: a suspended thread is sampled at syscall return, and GetThreadTimes showed 3 us in the profile against 144 ns measured (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:311-314). The derived floor is 450 * 3-6 us = 1.35-2.7 ms/s = 0.02-0.045 ms/frame, so the low bound is 0.04.

### 75. R-CKPT-MASKED: A pending but undeliverable event turns every checkpoint into a host body

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: checkpoint policy. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_MASKED_EVENT_BACKOFF (default 0)`. Already listed: True docs/PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P4 step 2
- **What:** While any bit of pending_event_mask is set, the inline gate escalates every counted checkpoint (backward branch and adaptive call return) to the host (event_pending path in branch_checkpoint_taken_slow, no repeat-PC throttle), and publish_next_inline_checkpoint_slow schedules the next body at current+1 for VI, input, DSP, IOS, DEC, AI, IPC, DSP-interface and PE causes. If the event cannot be delivered (EE clear inside OSDisableInterrupts sections, vi_edge_delivery_active, PE masked) the body runs at every checkpoint until the guest restores EE.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14703-14753, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14760-14800, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27321-27403, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:28619-28645
- **Why paid:** The mask is a single 'something is pending' word; the generated test cannot tell a deliverable event from one blocked by MSR[EE] or by an active VI transaction, so it asks the host each time.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; code: native_api.h:14708-14716 and native_runtime.cpp:27321-27403): every counted checkpoint inside an EE-masked window while an event is latched; rate not measured
- **Design:** In the body, when the only pending sources are EE-gated and guest_external_interrupts_enabled(context) is false, publish next_slow = current + 64 (P4 step 2) and set a runtime-owned 'masked' word that the inline test reads instead of the raw mask (module checkpoint gate: pending_event_mask pointer swapped to a deliverable-mask word). The EE-setting mtmsr (architectural_interrupt_checkpoint, always a host call) clears 'masked' and delivers. Non-gated sources (input report, runtime failure, transfer) keep current+1.
- **Risk** (low): Delivery happens at the EE-enable mtmsr, which is exactly where hardware would take the interrupt; input reports (not EE-gated) keep their current+1 schedule. The failure mode is a guest that spins with EE clear waiting on a level only the host can change (masked DSP mail wait); that path already has its own branch (native_runtime.cpp:28410-28522) and must stay at current+1.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0-0.2 ms/frame. Unknown share; bounded above by the body row: at most a third of the measured 2.6 % body share in EE-masked windows -> 0.0087 * 8.9-16.9 = 0.08-0.15 ms, plus inline escalation cost; range 0-0.2 ms/frame until a Detailed recording splits bodies by event_was_pending and MSR[EE].

### 76. R-CKPT-GLOBALS: Inline checkpoint tests re-read the services table; --checkpoint-globals has no runtime refresh sites yet

- **Status:** translator side done (--checkpoint-globals); runtime refresh sites open. Area: Runtime: checkpoint gate / generated inline tests. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_MODULE_CHECKPOINT_GATE (default 0 = export ignored, snapshot stays empty)`. Already listed: True docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_TASK.md 'Runtime changes' item 2 (refresh sites still to do); docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md gap item 1
- **What:** Every non-leaf call return (adaptive mode is on whenever AI DMA plays, i.e. all of gameplay) and every 32nd backward branch runs call_return_checkpoint_due / branch_checkpoint_due_budgeted, which reload branch_checkpoint, pending_event_mask, runtime_flags, checkpoint_counter and checkpoint_next_slow from the services table after each opaque call. The translator now emits the snapshot form under --checkpoint-globals, but runtime/src contains no call to refresh_module_checkpoint_gate and no lookup of the galaxy_module_checkpoint_gate export, so a flagged module sees an empty snapshot and every site takes the NotCounted cold path (call_return_checkpoint_general): correct but slower than without the flag.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14966-15015, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14860-14877, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:15090-15216, runtime/src/checkpoint_gate.cpp:324-340, runtime/src/checkpoint_gate.cpp:273-286, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:29058-29070, docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_TASK.md:148-166, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:6534
- **Why paid:** GateController republishes the two gate pointers at every host callback scope and fiber switch, and branch_checkpoint toggles kNativeServiceFlagAdaptiveCallReturnCheckpoints at run time, so generated code could not cache them; adaptive call returns exist so a long chain of direct calls cannot starve AI/VI edges.
- **Frequency** (derived; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md generation 21 'call-return sites 304,463 to 283,457'; kBranchCheckpointBudget = 32 at native_api.h:6534): once per translated non-leaf call return (283,457 static sites) and once per 32 backward branches; dynamic rate not measured
- **Design:** Implement the four runtime refresh sites listed in TRANSLATOR_CPU_TASK.md: load the optional export after init_fn, call refresh_module_checkpoint_gate from an observer at the end of publish_full_unchecked/publish_inline_unchecked (covers bind/activate/scopes/exchange_chain), after the adaptive-flag toggle (only when the bit changes), and with nullptr before unload. Optionally raise kBranchCheckpointBudget from 32 to 128 (P4 step 4) once the body is event-driven (R-CKPT-BODY).
- **Risk** (low): A missed refresh site leaves a stale counter pointer published inside a FullScope (cadence change, not wrong delivery); the 576-scenario test and the fail-safe empty snapshot cover the decision logic. Do not ship a --checkpoint-globals module to a runtime without the refresh sites: it is slower than the base module.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.03-0.17 ms/frame. Inline call-return test measured at 1-2 % of the simulation thread (docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native15/16 'The inline call-return check costs about 1 to 2 %'). The snapshot cuts a site from 26 to 13 instructions (adaptive) and 14 to 9 (non-adaptive) (docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS.md 1.2), i.e. 35-50 % of the test. est_ms = (0.35-0.5) * (0.01-0.02) * 8.9-16.9 = 0.03-0.17 ms/frame.

### 77. H-J3D-LEAF: J3D packet and FIFO leaf writers are translated (J3D is 16 % of guest)

- **Status:** open. Area: host natives (J3D packet code). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-j3d-draw`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** J3D is 16 % of guest samples. Matrix math is already lean (tier 2: Mtx33 copies, ArrayConcat, CalcInverseTranspose, keyframes). The per-shape and per-packet draw path is translated: J3DShapePacket and J3DMatPacket draw, J3DShapeDraw::draw (which calls GXCallDisplayList), J3DShapeMtx::loadMtxIndx_* and the J3DFifoLoad*Indx writers, which push 5-byte XF index-load commands through fast::store to the gather pipe. Each is a small leaf or near-leaf paying function entry and exit (about 40 instructions) and register synchronisation at its call site. ShapePacketUserData::loadTexMtx is 324 B of PowerPC that becomes 25,024 B of x86 (77x).
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7535-7637, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:67-72, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:465-469
- **Why paid:** Only the floating-point matrix and keyframe helpers were replaced (tiers 2 and 3). J3D's GX-writing code is game middleware and was left translated by policy (SDK plan section 7: 'JKR, JUT, JAS ... leave translated').
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native16 guest split (J3D 16 %); docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md code-size table (loadTexMtx 77x)): Per shape, material and packet per frame; counts not measured. J3D share 16 % of guest samples (measured, approximate last-call-target attribution).
- **Design:** Translator flag --lean-j3d-draw. Take the 10-20 hottest J3D leaf writers from rva_profile.py on the generation 25 map (J3DFifoLoadPosMtxIndx, J3DFifoLoadNrmMtxIndx3x3, J3DFifoLoadIndx, J3DShapeMtx::loadMtxIndx_PNGP/PCPU/NCPU, J3DShapeDraw::draw) and give them lean bodies that append the exact pipe bytes through one wgpipe_append per command, with lean contracts. Validate with a diff_gx-style harness over the gather-pipe bytes. Not possible here: the tree has no J3D symbol addresses; the Petari map is needed.
- **Risk** (medium): Byte-exact FIFO output is required: the concrete failure is wrong matrix indices on skinned models. Some writers read J3DSys global state (mtx-cache flags) that must be read the same way. A diff harness on random packet state catches both.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-0.3 ms/frame. J3D share of the simulation thread = 16 % x 0.625 = 10 %. est_ms = 0.10 x 12.9 = 1.29 ms (0.89-1.69 ms over T_sim 8.9-16.9 ms). I assume packet and FIFO leaf writers are 10-25 % of J3D time and that lean bodies remove most of their call overhead: 0.1-0.3 ms. This is the largest uncertain item in this area; an rva_profile breakdown of the J3D rows would settle it.

### 78. T-STORE-OUTLINE: Every non-stack store inlines the gather-pipe append and device path (about 200 B of cold code per site)

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: memory access, code size. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--outline-store-slow-paths (with --static-ram-stores)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** fast::store<T> puts the following behind its pipe test, inline at every site:
- the wgpipe address check and three host-pointer null tests;
- the in-place 32-byte gather append and the flush callback;
- a checked_store_slow call with spilled arguments.
The cold path's register demands also force callee-saved pushes and spills in small functions. Stores whose base is provably RAM still pay the pipe test: r13 (SDA), a lis/addi RAM constant, or an r1-derived register. flat_store_helper exempts only r1.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:119-140, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:175-226, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3086-3093, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3949-3989, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4060-4120, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3112-3296
- **Why paid:** Generation 21 moved the checked path out of line but deliberately kept the gather append inline so GX stores stay cheap (NATIVE_EXECUTION 'Checked store path'). RAM proof exists only for r1, from generation 25.
- **Frequency** (derived; measured instruction mix and 60% stack share, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md): Every non-stack integer store site: about 40% of hot stores, i.e. 8.9% x 0.4 = about 3.6% of hot guest instructions. Paid as code size at every such site in the module.
- **Design:** Two parts:
- --outline-store-slow-paths: the hot path is the masked compare plus movbe; anything else calls one GALAXY_NOINLINE store_device_slow<T> that does the pipe append and the checked store. The inline append is kept only where discover_wgpipe_store_runs' constant propagation proves the pipe address.
- --static-ram-stores: use store_stack for r13-based stores and for bases that the device scan's constant propagation proves to be is_flat_ram_address or r1+const.
- **Risk** (low): Behaviour is unchanged. A pipe store through an unknown base pays a call (performance only). A base wrongly proven to be RAM that actually reaches 0xCC008000 faults into the fastmem device handler, which is still correct but slow.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.18 ms/frame. Code size, measured in this session:
- the asm-probe int_store function is 1,515 B for 6 stores (about 230 B per site);
- micro stw_today is 229 B, stw_outline 97 B, with the same 5-instruction hot path;
- micro stfs shows 5 push/pop pairs forced only by the cold path.
Store slow paths come to about 22 B per guest instruction against about 160 B per guest instruction of module code (40x), roughly 14%.
Hot path: 3.6% x 15-30% provable x 3 instructions = 0.016 to 0.032 x86 per guest instruction, 0.2 to 0.4% of game code, about 0.02 to 0.03 ms (est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms).
The cache and TLB gain from a 7-10% smaller module is a hypothesis, 0 to 0.15 ms.

### 79. T-BCTRL-GATE: The bctrl direct-dispatch gate chases seven runtime-owned loads per virtual call

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: control flow (indirect calls). Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--module-dispatch-table (runtime: GALAXY_MODULE_DISPATCH_TABLE, default 0 = today's services path)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md ('bctrl ~68: dispatch-table checks'); docs/PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P10.4 (withheld entries)
- **What:** Before calling, every bctrl/bclrl does:
- a services null test;
- loads of direct_dispatch, its base and count, plus bounds and alignment tests;
- the table-entry load and a null test;
- two pointer-chased atomic loads (pending_event_mask, direct_dispatch_suspended);
- the pc store.
That is 22 x86 instructions, 7 loads and 7 branches. services is not restrict, so these fields are re-read after guest stores (-fno-strict-aliasing).
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1863-1906, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7745-7769, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5988-6045, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:6047-6075, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15873-15916 (grep), runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:33484-33487 (grep)
- **Why paid:** The table, its bounds and the suspension flag are owned by the runtime and published only through the services table (F19).
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; instruction mix measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md; bctrl share assumed): Once per virtual call. Calls are 8% of hot guest instructions; the bctrl share is assumed at 25-40% of calls.
- **Design:** Module-owned direct-mapped table:
- The translator emits constexpr kDirectTable[(addr - kTextBase) >> 2] for every callable translated address; the text base and span are compile-time constants.
- The table is exported so the runtime nulls withheld targets at init (2,149 withheld, P10.4).
- One module-global gate word (pending_event_mask OR suspended) is kept current by the runtime at the same refresh points as checkpoint-globals.
- Fast path: subtract and compare immediates, table load, gate load, test, call (about 8 x86 instructions, 2-3 loads).
- The runtime switch GALAXY_MODULE_DISPATCH_TABLE=0 (default) keeps today's services path.
- **Risk** (medium): The runtime must null every withheld or host-transaction target and must raise the gate before an external dispatch starts. A miss calls an intercepted function natively, skipping the host transaction (a behaviour bug, e.g. a missed scene or movie marker).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.08-0.3 ms/frame. Measured in scratchpad/micro bctrl_today: the fast path is 22 instructions; the target is about 9. Calculation: 2 to 3.2% of guest instructions x 13 = 0.26 to 0.42 x86 per guest instruction. The loads hit L1, so count them at 50-75%: 1.6 to 3.9% of game code, giving 0.13 to 0.32 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms. The low bound is widened to 0.08.

### 80. T-PSQ: psq_l/psq_st dispatch on GQR at run time and spill cold-path state on the hot path; each site inlines about 280-370 bytes

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--static-gqr`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** psq_load_impl/psq_store_impl load context->gqr[i], extract the type (shift/and) and branch, even though the translator knows the GQR index as a literal. The quantized-lane lambdas capture by reference ([&]), which makes clang materialise base/memory/services/guest_pc/type on the stack on the hot path. The probe one_psqst shows 5 stack stores (mov [rsp+..]) among 26 hot instructions. psq_st addressed from r1 also runs the gather-pipe test. Static size per site: psq_st 367 bytes, psq_l 281 bytes, against about 40 for a type-0 known path (probe, nm -S).
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:414-475, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:497-576, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4144-4221
- **Why paid:** GQRs are guest SPRs that the SDK can change at run time (OSInitFastCast sets GQR2-5, and the context switch saves and restores GQR1-7). The helper is written generically for any type, with the quantized paths inlined.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT mix (measured categories); the psq and GQR0 shares are hypotheses; per-site cost from probe disassembly): Every psq_l/psq_st. Hypothesis 1-2.5 % of hot guest instructions (inside the measured float load 6.3 % and store 3.2 % categories: prologue/epilogue psq_st/psq_l of paired nonvolatiles, inlined vector math). Hypothesis 60-90 % use GQR0.
- **Design:** (1) Header: move the quantized and gather-pipe lane code into a GALAXY_NOINLINE cold function that takes its arguments by value, removing the hot-path spills and about 250 bytes per site. (2) Translator --static-gqr: scan main.dol and the RELs for `mtspr 912..919`. If GQR0 is never written with a nonzero value (SDK convention: GQR0 = 0, not saved or restored by OSLoadContext), emit `fast::psq_load_f32`/`psq_store_f32` for I=0: no gqr load, no type dispatch, and store_stack when rA = r1. Optionally specialise the OSInitFastCast GQRs if they are proven constant after init.
- **Risk** (low): (1) has no behaviour change. (2) If some code writes GQR0 at run time, I=0 sites dequantize wrongly (garbage vertex or matrix data). Mitigation: the translator refuses the flag when any mtspr to GQR0 is found that is not provably 0, and a debug build asserts context->gqr[0] == 0 at the specialised sites.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.022-0.16 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). Saved per site: about 4 instructions of GQR dispatch + about 5 spills (+4 gather-test instructions at stack sites). With 1-2.5 % psq x 0.75 GQR0, that is about 0.06-0.15 x86 per guest instruction (0.75-1.9 % of instructions), plus i-cache relief from about 250 bytes per site; f = 0.4-1.5 %. Low = 0.625*0.004*8.9 = 0.022; high = 0.625*0.015*16.9 = 0.16 ms.

### 81. H-TRACEONLY: Trace-only and bring-up targets are hard-wired host calls (JKR alloc/free, OSGetTick, getters)

- **Status:** open. Area: host natives / host-call list. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--direct-trace-only-targets / GALAXY_WITHHOLD_TRACE_ONLY_TARGETS=0`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** The translator's requires_host_guest_call list contains about 15 targets whose runtime handling is a trace or a bring-up assertion. They are: 0x8038BC80 and 0x803F81AC (scene-change trace); 0x803981A0 (an 8-byte FileLoader getter); 0x8039B9E0; 0x80409F48 (JKR do_alloc); 0x8040A2B4 (JKRExpHeap::allocFromHead); 0x8040ABF4, 0x8040ACE0 and 0x8040CA60 (JKR free paths); 0x80418440; 0x804A8A0C; 0x804AC380 (OSGetTick); 0x804B1E38 and 0x804B1E7C (VISet*RetraceCallback); 0x803CE128 (a vtable assertion on a GX display-list object). Every direct `bl` to these targets is emitted as galaxy::call_guest_resolved and pays the full path: the inline wrapper with 3 trace gates, the services call, call_guest_impl, then call_guest_impl_body (162 KB of source with 73 address compares and 38 trace gates), and only then the translated body. The call site is also a Full residency sync point and keeps its call-return checkpoint. On the runtime side, direct_dispatch_target_requires_host withholds the same addresses from the bctrl table, so indirect calls to them take the runtime cached-call path.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7871-7931, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7281-7298, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:13347-13416, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:16101-16130, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15821-15864, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:24850-24940, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:25466-25487, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:26956-26987, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27045-27075
- **Why paid:** These are boot-time bring-up diagnostics: heap free-list corruption, the svc1 singleton, the OSGetTick first/last bookkeeping and scene-change logging.
