The runtime already admits the problem: needs_call_guest_intercept returns false for them unless trace_runtime_scaffolding(), trace_scene_changes() or trace_ticks is set, and its comment reads 'The JKR allocator/free entries and OSGetTick are invoked through virtual/indirect calls many times per frame, so keeping them on the cached native path matters'. The translator list was never pruned to match, so direct calls still always cross the host boundary.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; runtime comment at native_runtime.cpp:16101-16104; 'calls through the runtime 1.8 %' (measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md)): Unknown per target. JKR alloc/free and OSGetTick are 'many times per frame' by the runtime's own comment; my assumption is 100-1,000 host transitions per frame across the set. All runtime-routed calls together are measured at 1.8 % of the simulation thread.
- **Design:** Translator flag --direct-trace-only-targets: remove the trace-only addresses from requires_host_guest_call. Each becomes an ordinary linked call, so checkpoint-free-leaf elision applies (OSGetTick, the 0x803981A0 getter and 0x803F81AC are leaves). Keep 0x8039EF50 (OOM, cold), 0x8041071C, KPADRead, the OS and scheduler entries, and DVDOpen. Runtime GALAXY_WITHHOLD_TRACE_ONLY_TARGETS (default 1, today's behaviour; 0 means withhold only while the matching trace flag is on) applies the same rule in direct_dispatch_target_requires_host. It also covers the trace-only 'cached call service' rows: OSSendMessage, OSReceiveMessage and OSJamMessage 0x804A88E4/89AC/8A88 for trace_dsp_host, and the JPA resource traces 0x80441400/0x804414DC/0x804414FC/0x80448024. A module built without the flag keeps every trace.
- **Risk** (low): Traces only. With the flag, the alloc/free/scene/OSGetTick logs and tick counters go silent for direct calls; rebuild without the flag when debugging. The one functional loss is the 0x803CE128 vtable assertion (a bring-up guard against a corrupted display-list object): with the flag, a recurrence of that corruption would crash in translated code instead of producing a clear RuntimeFailure.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.15 ms/frame. The bucket bounds the saving. 'Calls through the runtime' is 1.8 % of the simulation thread, and est_ms = 0.018 x T_sim gives 0.16-0.30 ms on the i7-6500U (T_sim 8.9-16.9 ms, midpoint 0.23 ms). The trace-only targets are an unknown fraction of that bucket; I assume 20-50 %. Cross-check: 100-500 calls per frame at 0.3-0.6 us each on the 6500U (path length above, about 2.8x the desktop by the PassMark ratio) also gives 0.03-0.3 ms.

### 82. G-CODE-ITLB: 7.7 MB of hot translated code on 4 KB image pages vs a 128-entry iTLB and shared STLB

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: code TLB reach. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_LARGE_PAGE_CODE (default 0)`. Already listed: False CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:144-147 (link order, TLB as a hypothesis) is the prerequisite, not this design
- **What:** The functions behind 80 % of game-code samples are 7.7 MB of x86, about 1,900 4 KB pages, spread over a 209 MB DLL image. Skylake's L1 iTLB holds 128 4 KB entries (split or shared between SMT threads) and the STLB holds 1,536 entries shared with data. With a flat profile, almost every call and return to another function crosses a page, and code page walks compete with the data walks in G-LARGE-PAGES. Windows maps user-mode DLL images only with 4 KB pages; there is no large-page image option outside kernel drivers.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:101-128, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:11642-11692
- **Why paid:** Code size (~40x the PowerPC) and the DLL image model. Link order (T-LINK-ORDER) can make the hot set contiguous but cannot reduce it below 1,900 pages.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:22,62-66 (7.7 MB / 592 functions; profile flat)): Every instruction fetch outside the iTLB reach (512 KB). With a flat profile (no function above 1.2 %), a large fraction of calls and returns land on a page not in the iTLB.
- **Design:** After T-LINK-ORDER puts the hot set in one contiguous range, add an optional manual mapper (GALAXY_LARGE_PAGE_CODE=1, requires the same privilege as G-LARGE-PAGES). It loads RMGE01_game.dll into a MEM_LARGE_PAGES allocation, applies base relocations, resolves imports, runs TLS callbacks, and registers .pdata with RtlAddFunctionTable. Only the hot contiguous range needs large pages, about 4 x 2 MB. The cheaper alternative with no privilege is to shrink the code (other ledger items).
- **Risk** (high): A manual DLL loader must reproduce relocations, imports, TLS, CRT init, SEH/C++ unwind registration and debugger/profiler symbol mapping, or exceptions and crash reports break. Large pages cannot carry per-4 KB protections, so the code would be RWX or must be RX for the whole range after copy (W^X weakened during load). The profiler's module-row attribution (the map file resolution in CPU_GAP_REPORT) must learn the new base.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-0.6 ms/frame. Formula: est_ms = share_of_thread x 12.9 ms (sim). I assume ITLB walk plus STLB-miss stall share on Skylake is 1-5 % of the game-code part (0.625 x 12.9 = 8.06 ms) after link order: 0.08-0.40 ms. I raised the high bound to 0.6 because code walks also evict data PTEs. Needs ITLB_MISSES.WALK_ACTIVE and ITLB_MISSES.STLB_HIT on any Skylake.

### 83. R-QUIET-MTMSR: Every interrupt re-enable (OSRestoreInterrupts/OSEnableInterrupts) is a host transition, and every mtmsr carries a seq_cst fence

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: architectural checkpoints / translator mtmsr lowering. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--inline-quiet-mtmsr (translator); GALAXY_MODULE_QUIET_SNAPSHOT (runtime, default 0 = snapshot invalid, module always calls the host)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Translated mtmsr stores MSR, emits std::atomic_thread_fence(seq_cst) (mfence or locked op), then architectural_interrupt_checkpoint; when EE is set it increments the counter and calls services->branch_checkpoint (coop_branch_checkpoint -> branch_checkpoint). The quiet path at 0x804A8128/0x804A8104 then builds RuntimeBoundarySelfScope, ScopedCallbackProfile, census counters, two trace observers, ScopedBranchCheckpointIdentity, the cadence ScopedPhaseTimer, ~25 state tests, runtime_time_base_ticks, VI inspect, HID cadence stats and 8 device-level loads before returning without work. OSDisableInterrupts (EE cleared) pays the fence only.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5064-5072, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14896-14928, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30014-30039, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:28805-29032, runtime/include/galaxy/checkpoint_gate.h:33
- **Why paid:** mtmsr that sets EE is an architectural interrupt-recognition point: a DEC or external interrupt latched while EE was clear must be taken before the next instruction. The quiet path (Native21) skips the body but still decides in the host because the deadlines and device levels live in RuntimeState.
- **Frequency** (derived; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native21 'Quiet interrupt re-enable': 0x804A8128 alone 2.56 M full callbacks in 90 s (28,400/s) before the change, checkpoint bodies 3.48 M to 1.25 M per run after it, i.e. ~2.2 M quiet returns per ~124 s route; disables assumed equal to restores): ~18,000 quiet EE-enable callbacks/s and ~50,000 mtmsr fences/s (disable + enable)
- **Design:** Publish a module-visible quiet snapshot (optional export, same pattern as ModuleCheckpointGateV1): quiet_until_tsc = min(VI, DEC, AI-DMA, HID absolute deadlines) converted to raw TSC, and a levels word that the device producers set (shared with R-CKPT-BODY step 1). The translator lowers EE-setting mtmsr at the two OS sites to: if ((msr & EE) && mask == 0 && levels == 0 && counter < next_slow && __rdtsc() < quiet_until_tsc) continue; else host call. Drop the seq_cst fence for mtmsr that clears EE (no recognition possible) and replace it with a compiler barrier; keep it on EE-set where the mask must be re-read after the MSR store.
- **Risk** (medium): The snapshot must be refreshed whenever a deadline is re-armed (DEC write, VI periodic advance, AI DMA start, HID arm) or the inline path can return quietly past a due deadline, delaying the interrupt until the next checkpoint (bounded by the 2048-count watermark). The deadlines are re-armed on the simulation thread inside host calls, except the broker's periodic VI/HID publication, which sets a mask bit and therefore forces the host path anyway. Removing the fence for EE-clear is safe only if no other thread reads context->msr expecting ordering with later stores; the render/broker threads do not read PpcContext in the code read.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.04-0.3 ms/frame. Per quiet callback on the desktop assumed 40-170 ns excluding its clock read (counted in R-CLOCK): 18,000/s * 40-170 ns = 0.72-3.1 ms/s = 0.5-2.3 % of the desktop simulation thread (135 ms/s). est_ms = 0.005-0.023 * 8.9-16.9 -> 0.04-0.39 ms; capped at 0.3 because part of this sits in the measured 'checkpoint bodies' row. Fence: 50,000/s * 15-35 ns on Skylake = 0.75-1.75 ms/s = 0.013-0.03 ms/frame, included.

### 84. G-ALIAS-VIEWS: Host consumers read guest RAM through a separate host view, so the same physical pages have two sets of PTEs and TLB entries

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: guest-RAM alias views. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_HOST_ALIAS_VIEW (host = today, guest = read through the 0x80/0x90 alias)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Translated code addresses guest RAM at kGuestBase+0x80000000 and +0x90000000. The runtime's GuestAddressSpace regions (host_base), and therefore the render thread's FIFO, display-list and vertex-array reads, texture decode, DVD/AI DMA, and the packet and display-list memcmp validation, use the 'ordinary section view' returned by MapViewOfFile (mem1_host_view/mem2_host_view), a different virtual range backed by the same physical pages. A physical page touched by both sides needs two PTEs (twice the PTE cache lines in L3) and two TLB entries. On SMT siblings, which share the STLB, it needs two STLB entries. The 0x00/0xC0/0x10/0xD0 aliases are mapped but cost nothing until touched, because PTEs are created on first touch.
- **Where:** runtime/src/flat_guest_memory.cpp:174-184, runtime/src/flat_guest_memory.cpp:112-132, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:10909-10922, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:10955-10960, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:10987-11004
- **Why paid:** The host view existed before the flat 4 GB view (WiiCompiled adaptation), and the vector-backed fallback needs a span. The consistency check at native_host.cpp:10993-10998 pins region.host_base to the host view.
- **Frequency** (derived; Code path (regions host_base = mem1_view_/mem2_view_); FIFO size from NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:167): Every host-side access to guest RAM. That covers per frame the 61-126 KB FIFO plus the display lists, vertex arrays and textures it references, read by the render thread, and DMA writes on worker threads. The duplication costs extra TLB/STLB entries only when both views of a page are live on one core (same thread, or SMT siblings); otherwise it costs only extra PTE lines in the shared L3.
- **Design:** Point mem1_view_/mem2_view_ at kGuestBase+0x80000000 and kGuestBase+0x90000000, the aliases translated code uses, instead of the MapViewOfFile host view. Change mapped_host_pointer and the native_host.cpp:10993 check accordingly, and keep the host view only for tests (or unmap it). Behind GALAXY_HOST_ALIAS_VIEW=guest, default host. With G-LARGE-PAGES this is mostly moot, but it is the zero-privilege variant.
- **Risk** (low): Any code that turns a host pointer back into a physical offset by subtracting mem1_host_view() would break. grep shows mem1_host_view/mem2_host_view used only in native_host.cpp:10912-10914 and tests, but region-relative pointer arithmetic in gx/ must be audited. With GALAXY_FLAT_GUEST_MEMORY=0 (vector backing) nothing changes.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.15 ms/frame. Each fully touched view costs 22,528 PTEs x 8 B = 176 KB of PTEs; the duplicate share of the hot set is perhaps 20-60 KB of PTE lines in a 4 MB L3 (0.5-1.5 %). It also matters when render and simulation are SMT siblings (see G-SMT-PLACEMENT). Formula: est_ms = share x thread_ms with an assumed 0.05-0.5 % of sim (12.9 ms) plus 0.05-1 % of render (8 ms): 0.006-0.065 + 0.004-0.08, about 0.01-0.15 ms.

### 85. G-SMT-IDLE-SPIN: Idle-loop pause spin occupies an SMT slot and package power on a 2-core 15 W part

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: SMT-shared core resources. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_IDLE_SPIN_US (default 20)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:51 lists idle wait 2.2 %; the SMT and package-power angle is new
- **What:** Inside the AI-DMA margin, coop_idle_spin pause-spins for up to 20 us per idle pass, checking the latch mask, PE flags and the timeline clock. On a 2-core/4-thread Skylake the spinning logical CPU shares its core with whatever runs on the sibling (often the render thread or the driver). PAUSE (~140 cycles on Skylake) limits but does not remove that sharing, and the spin consumes the shared 15 W budget that the HD 520 also needs. The latch lock_slot spin (G-LATCH-LAYOUT) has the same Skylake PAUSE scaling.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:29923-29948, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:29950-29966, runtime/include/galaxy/runtime_timeline.h:371-385
- **Why paid:** The armed waitable timer is less punctual than a spin for the AI DMA boundary (comment at native_runtime.cpp:29923-29926), and audio underruns are worse than CPU burn on a desktop.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:51,57): Up to 450 idle waits/s; idle wait is 2.2 % of simulation samples.
- **Design:** GALAXY_IDLE_SPIN_US, default 20 = today. On hosts where GetLogicalProcessorInformationEx reports 2 or fewer physical cores with SMT, use 0-5 us and rely on the high-resolution waitable timer plus wake event already in place. Log [idle-spin] budget and late-AI count to verify that no audio deadline is missed.
- **Risk** (medium): Late AI DMA servicing causes audio crackle or underrun if the timer fires late (WaitOnAddress timeouts are quantized to 16-33 ms; the high-resolution timer is better but not exact). The late-deadline count must be checked on the laptop.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.03-0.28 ms/frame. Formula: est_ms = share x thread_ms = 0.022 x 12.9 = 0.28 ms/frame upper bound (idle wait share, measured, projected). Only the spin part is removable and its benefit goes to the sibling thread and package power, so the low bound is about 10 % of it (0.03 ms).

### 86. T-CALLRET-CKPT: Inline call-return checkpoint after every non-leaf call

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: calls. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--checkpoint-free (translator); GALAXY_COOP_POLLED_DELIVERY=1 (runtime, default off)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT plan item 4 / PERFORMANCE_README item 5; TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS 1.2
- **What:** After each call to a non-leaf or indirect target the body tests call_return_checkpoint_due(_g) inline. With --checkpoint-globals the compiled fast path is still about 12 instructions: load the gate pointer, increment and store the call-return counter, compare against the next_slow watermark, load the event mask pointer, test it. A counter read-modify-write per call also chains through memory across calls. Every call_return_* continuation stub repeats the test, and a function-wide shared cold block holds the full stores, the policy call, the reloads and a switch.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7515-7532, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5849-5858, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:6268-6274, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:902-934, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:995-1013
- **Why paid:** Events (VI, AI DMA, DSP, DEC) are delivered at checkpoints, flag transfers are detected there, and the adaptive call-return counting keeps the checkpoint cadence calibrated.
- **Frequency** (measured; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md (call-return sites 304,463 to 283,457); CPU_GAP_REPORT rates): Once per call to a non-leaf target. 283,457 static call-return sites (NATIVE_EXECUTION Leaf calls row); 9,400 checkpoint bodies/s run the slow path.
- **Design:** Partly done by --checkpoint-globals, whose runtime refresh sites are still to be written. Remaining: --checkpoint-free (plan item 5) keeps only a one-load transfer test after calls (`if (g_transfer_pending) [[unlikely]] return;`), as the NATIVE_EXECUTION experiment showed is required, and drops the counter RMW. Leave the adaptive counter only where the runtime still needs count-based cadence.
- **Risk** (medium): Without counted call returns, event delivery latency depends on the cooperative scheduler's poll points. A loop that waits on memory and calls a leaf could stop servicing AI DMA or VI (the boot hang seen when branch checkpoints were dropped). Keep the transfer test or a boot fails with an RFI during a pending transfer (measured).
- **Estimate** (measured): 0.05-0.25 ms/frame. est_ms = share_of_sim_thread x sim_ms_6500U. Measured: the inline call-return check costs 1-2 % of the simulation thread before globals (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md 'Call-return checkpoints reduced to that transfer test'). --checkpoint-globals roughly halves the fast path (26 to 13 instructions, TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS 1.2), leaving about 0.5-1.5 %: 0.005 x 8.9 = 0.05; 0.015 x 16.9 = 0.25.

### 87. T-LEAF-CONTRACT: Calls to translated checkpoint-free leaves are full register sync points

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: calls, memory traffic. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--leaf-call-contracts`. Already listed: False docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md item 3 covers ABI-trusting registers (an approximate contract), not exact per-leaf contracts
- **What:** In the residency pass, a call to a translated leaf is Sync::Full: every written local is stored before the call and every used local reloaded after it. The leaf's real read and write sets are statically known, but only hand-written lean host bodies carry call contracts.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:632-663, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:935-950, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7517-7534, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1338-1367, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/guest_ppc_effect.rs:503, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/guest_ppc_effect.rs:594, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/guest_abi_effect.rs:264
- **Why paid:** The contract machinery was built for lean bodies (generation 19). Translated callees are treated as unknown.
- **Frequency** (derived; measured, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md gen21 'Leaf calls' (304,463 to 283,457); dynamic share assumed): Once per call to a checkpoint-free leaf: 21,006 of 304,463 static call sites (6.9%). The dynamic share is assumed to be 15-30% of calls.
- **Design:** Compute each leaf's GuestAbiContract (read_before_write and possible_writes for GPRs, LR, CTR, CR fields, XER, FPR/PS1) with the existing decode_guest_ppc_function/analyze_guest_abi. Emit the call with a Sync::Lean-style contract: store only written-and-read-by-leaf locals, reload only used-and-written-by-leaf locals. Verify with the lean-contract register-poison harness pattern (diff_tvec3 style).
- **Risk** (medium): A contract that misses an implicitly read or written register (CR0 from record forms, XER CA, LR via mflr) leaves a stale register after the call and gives wrong logic. The analysis fails closed: an unsupported instruction means full sync. A poisoned-register differential test catches misses.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.25 ms/frame. Calls are 5-7% of guest instructions (8% static including inlined _savegpr); 15-30% go to leaves; 6-12 context memory operations are saved per site. Calculation: 0.045 to 0.25 x86 per guest instruction, i.e. 0.56 to 3.1% of game code, giving 0.05 to 0.25 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms.

### 88. R-VTX144: Fixed 144-byte canonical vertex written for every decoded vertex

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: CPU vertex decode / upload. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_COMPACT_VERTEX (0 = 144-byte canonical vertex)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'Not started: ... the compact per-layout vertex format (F12; the decoder writes 144 bytes per vertex)'
- **What:** GxVertexOut always carries position, normal, tangent, binormal, 2 colors, 8 uv pairs and 4 matrix-index words (144 B). A typical SMG vertex needs about 24-40 B. The decoder writes and zeroes the whole struct (decoded.vertices.resize value-initializes 144 B per vertex on the cached-run path), memcpys it into WC upload memory or the resident arena, and the HD 520 fetches 144 B per vertex from shared memory.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/gx/vertex_loader.h:38-56, runtime/src/gx/vertex_loader.cpp:1560, runtime/src/gx/vertex_loader.cpp:960-1018, runtime/src/gx/vertex_loader.cpp:1595-1648
- **Why paid:** One input layout for every PSO keeps pipeline permutations down (vertex_loader.h:38-40).
- **Frequency** (derived; vertex_loader.h:55 static_assert; every decode path writes GxVertexOut): Per decoded vertex (cache misses and uncached draws), and the GPU fetch on every draw.
- **Design:** Compact per-layout format (F12). Emit only present attributes, packed in a fixed order, and key the PSO input layout by a small 'vertex class' id (e.g. 6-10 classes covering the SMG layouts), so color stays RGBA8 and absent uv/tangent/binormal are not stored. VS inputs that are absent take shader defaults (0,0). Combine with R-VTXSPEC so each specialized decoder writes its compact class directly.
- **Risk** (medium): Adds an input-layout dimension to the PSO key, so more pipelines and first-use compiles (the uber path covers misses). A missing attribute default differing from today's zeros changes shading on that class.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.25 ms/frame. CPU side only: assume write and zero bandwidth is 10-30 % of the measured 8 % decode share (0.48-0.8 ms) and that 3-4x fewer bytes removes most of it, giving 0.05-0.25 ms. GPU and memory-bandwidth savings on the shared 15 W package come on top and are not counted.

### 89. T-CA-DEAD: XER[CA] is computed and stored even when nothing reads it

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: CR/XER handling. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--dead-carry-elimination`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** srawi, sraw, addic(.), subfic, addc, subfc and the adde/addze/subfe/subfze family always compute the carry and do a read-modify-write of context->xer through set_xer_ca. This happens even when no adde-family instruction, mfxer or mcrxr reads CA before the next CA writer, call or return.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5167-5212, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5245-5289, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5350-5410, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5530-5608, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12468-12475
- **Why paid:** The lowering has no XER liveness, so CA is treated as always live.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; not in the measured mix; assumed): Once per executed CA writer. Assumed 0.5 to 1.5% of hot guest instructions (srawi for signed shifts and divisions by 2^n, subfic, addic idioms), of which 50 to 80% have a dead CA.
- **Design:** Per-function liveness of CA (the untracked ppc_dataflow.rs work in progress already models 'ca'):
- Readers: adde, addze, addme, subfe, subfze, subfme, mfxer, mcrxr.
- CA is dead at bl/bctrl/blr exits (XER is volatile in the EABI).
- CA is live at sc, rfi, unknown bctr and interior entries.
Where CA is dead, drop the set_xer_ca statement and its carry expression.
- **Risk** (low): A missed reader would feed a wrong carry into an adde chain, giving wrong 64-bit arithmetic (OSTime, 64-bit counters). Unknown paths fail closed by keeping CA. Unit-test carry chains and run diff_tier2.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.12 ms/frame. Measured in scratchpad/micro: srawi with CA is 14 hot x86 instructions; with CA dead it is 4, so 10 are saved. Calculation: (0.5 to 1.5%) x (0.5 to 0.8) x 10 = 0.025 to 0.12 x86 per guest instruction, i.e. 0.3 to 1.5% of game code. est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms gives 0.02 to 0.12 ms.

### 90. R-INDEXCOPY: Precomputed indices re-copied into the index ring on every resident packet-run replay

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: D3D12 draw submission / index upload. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_RESIDENT_INDICES (0 = ring copy)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** A decoded-cache hit draws vertices from the resident arena, but upload_cached_packet_run_indices still allocates index-ring space and memcpys the cached triangle-list indices every replay. When the run joins a pending batch, it also rebases every index (rebase_cached_indices) instead of using a base-vertex offset. A new index view each time also defeats the IASetIndexBuffer elision.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/vertex_loader.cpp:1650-1770, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:11318-11345, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:7550-7575
- **Why paid:** Indices live in the per-frame ring, so they are re-emitted each frame. Batching across runs needs a common base vertex.
- **Frequency** (derived; Code: index_ring.allocate plus memcpy at vertex_loader.cpp:1717-1754 runs whenever total_indices != 0 and the run is not pure triangles): Per replayed non-triangle (strip/fan/quad) packet run on a decoded-cache hit, every chunk.
- **Design:** Store the run's indices in the ResidentVertexArena next to its vertices at admission (immutable, retired with the block) and draw with DrawIndexedInstanced(index_count, 1, resident_first_index, BaseVertexLocation=0, 0). For batching, use separate DrawIndexedInstanced calls with BaseVertexLocation set instead of rebasing indices on the CPU.
- **Risk** (low): Resident index lifetime must match the vertex block (same retirement rule). Mis-sized index blocks give corrupted triangles on that run.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.12 ms/frame. 100-300 K indices per frame x 2 B = 0.2-0.6 MB/frame of WC copy plus per-run allocation and view changes, ~0.02-0.12 ms on the 6500U.

### 91. H-NATIVE-SYNC: Non-lean native helpers and inline wrappers: full register sync, call-return checkpoint and checked loads at every call

- **Status:** open. Area: native_pure_helper / audited inline wrappers. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-native-helpers`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** About 20 hand-written native_* helpers are reached through native_pure_helper: native_read_next_char_utf16, native_add_12, native_noop, native_indexed_word_load, native_jpa_list_remove/append, native_jpa_actor_flags_mask, native_jpa_random_i16, native_jpa_actor_check x2, native_gx_set_chan_color (actually GXSetTevColor 0x804BD28C), native_gx_set_array (actually GXSetClipMode 0x804BE59C) and native_stride6_offset8. None has a lean_call_contract, so residency classifies each call as Sync::Full: it stores every written local and reloads every used one. The helpers are excluded from checkpoint_free_leaves, so call_return_checkpoint_needed keeps a call-return checkpoint at each site even though their guest bodies are leaves. Their bodies use checked guest_load_u32/guest_store_u32, each with a region admit and a trace_fileloader_stack gate, instead of the flat view. The audited inline wrappers (SceneObj accessors 0x80344AA8, 0x8039D440, 0x8039D44C, and the delegate thunks) also emit checked galaxy::guest_load_u32 rather than flat loads.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7791-7845, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7745-7760, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:690-700, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7354-7495, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:210-253, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:4985-5003, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:15690-15725, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:7704-7712, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:11388-11413
- **Why paid:** These helpers predate residency (F5), lean contracts (generation 19) and flat-view-only lean bodies (generation 23). Only the lean_* set was migrated to contracts and flat access.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; code paths listed; no per-site census exists): Per call. The JPA helpers run per particle or emitter, the SceneObj accessors per actor or system query, and GXSetTevColor per material set-up. Rate unknown; my assumption is 1,000-5,000 calls per frame across the set.
- **Design:** Translator flag --lean-native-helpers. (1) Add lean_call_contract entries for the integer helpers, covering only the ABI-visible registers: for example native_jpa_random_i16 reads r3 and writes r3, and native_indexed_word_load reads r3,r4 and writes r3. Drop their writes to dead volatile registers (r0, r4, r5, CR) as the lean bodies already do. (2) Switch their memory accesses to the lean_guest_span / fast flat-view accessors under GALAXY_FLAT_VIEW_ONLY_ACTIVE. (3) Emit the inline-wrapper loads as fast::load. Extend lean_call_contracts_cover_the_bodies and the poisoned-register harness to the new entries.
- **Risk** (low): A contract that misses a register a caller later reads would corrupt that local. The existing contract test plus a diff harness (in the style of diff_tvec3, against the translated bodies, with volatile registers poisoned) catches this. Dropping writes to r0, r4, r5 and CR0 is safe only if no caller reads them after the call, which the EABI guarantees and the CPU_GAP_REPORT census found violated by only 4 debugger functions.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.12 ms/frame. Assumes 1,000-5,000 calls per frame x 20-60 x86 instructions saved (Full sync of about 10-40 context memory operations, a 9-13 instruction call-return checkpoint per TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS 1.2, about 5 extra instructions per checked load). At about 1 instruction per cycle at 3 GHz with cache misses on the 6500U, that is 7-20 ns per call: 0.007-0.1 ms, rounded up for i-cache effects.

### 92. R-IDLE: Idle passes: full checkpoint callback, two kernel waits/timer arms and an AI-margin spin per pass

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: idle loop / coop scheduler. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_IDLE_DIRECT_WAIT (default 0 = body-driven idle pass and 20 us margin spin as today)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Idle wait 2.2 %'; PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P5 step 5 (spin the final 300 us). The direct wait from coop_idle and the single-timer AI margin are new.
- **What:** With no runnable guest thread, Scheduler::select_thread calls coop_idle per pass: save/load of the whole register file (parked copy), coop_idle_ai_due (clock read), a full branch_checkpoint (prologue, FullScope, body) whose 0x804AB360 branch calls idle_loop_wait_for_next_deadline: runtime_time_base_ticks, VI inspect, cap_realtime_vi_wait_for_ai_dma (second clock read), double-based duration conversions, 6 device-level loads, SetWaitableTimer when the target moved, set_idle_waiting (seq_cst) x2, WaitForMultipleObjects on event+timer, two timeline records and two steady_clock::now. Inside the 250 us AI-DMA margin the wait is skipped and coop_idle_spin polls for up to 20 us per pass, then the whole pass repeats.
- **Where:** runtime/src/coop_scheduler.cpp:193-270, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:29909-29966, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:18742-18905, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5992-6040, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27983-28058, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5974-5982
- **Why paid:** F24 replaced spinning with an event/timer wait bounded by the next VI and AI deadline; the AI margin spin exists because the event wake was measured less punctual than a spin for AI-DMA service (Native17: margin 0 raised AI lateness 96 to 230 us). The idle pass reuses the generic checkpoint body so every device is serviced in the same order.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md (450 idle waits/s); docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native21 (idle passes 5.3 M to 61 k per run)): 450 idle waits/s; idle passes ~500/s (61 k per run)
- **Design:** (1) In coop_idle, when the latch mask is empty and no level is up, call the wait directly (one clock sample shared with the AI test and the VI inspect) and run the body only after the wait returns, instead of running the body, waiting inside it, and running another pass. (2) Replace the 20 us spin-and-repeat inside the AI margin with one armed high-resolution timer at ai_due - 50 us followed by a single bounded spin to ai_due (or rdtsc-based spin only for the last 50 us), so the margin costs one wake instead of ~12 passes. (3) Keep the parked register copy only when the body actually delivers an interrupt (coop already restores registers in the dispatcher).
- **Risk** (medium): AI-DMA service lateness is the acceptance metric (today ~96-103 us mean); a later wake shows up as audio crackle on the laptop. On the i7-6500U the spin also takes the core from its hyper-thread sibling (likely the render thread) and package power from the HD 520, so shortening it helps beyond the simulation thread. Windows timer resolution is raised with timeBeginPeriod(1) (native_runtime.cpp:32112); without it the high-resolution timer flag still covers Windows 10 1803+.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.22 ms/frame. Measured share 2.2 % -> est_ms = 0.022 * 8.9-16.9 = 0.20-0.37 ms/frame. Desktop cost per wait = 2.2 % * 135 ms/s / 450 = 6.6 us, mostly kernel transitions and the repeated body. Assume 25-60 % removable -> 0.05-0.22 ms. Kernel transitions are dearer on the 6500U (Skylake has no Meltdown hardware fix, so Windows runs KVA shadowing), which is not in the scaling factor.

### 93. H-GXIMM: GXLoadPosMtxImm stays on the exact helper; GXLoadNrmMtxImm, GXSetCurrentMtx and GXCallDisplayList are translated

- **Status:** open. Area: GX immediate-mode command writers. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-gx-imm`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** 0x804BE1D8 GXLoadPosMtxImm maps to native_gx_load_pos_mtx_imm_804BE1D8, which has no lean contract. Its body checks require_paired_single_mode and calls resolve_guest. It widens and narrows 12 lanes through the integer helpers (widen_f32_bits/narrow_paired_single_ftz) and writes FPR0-5 and PS1 0-5 back to the context, although those registers are volatile and dead. It ends with an indirect memory->write_device callback for 53 bytes. Each call site is a Full sync point with a call-return checkpoint. GXLoadNrmMtxImm 0x804BE228, GXSetCurrentMtx 0x804BE280, GXLoadTexMtxIndx 0x804BE354 and GXCallDisplayList 0x804BDEA8 run as translated SDK code. Their old native was retired in the restartability list, and they write the gather pipe through fast::store with a per-store pipe test.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:11415-11530, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:9878-9890, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7587, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7645-7741, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:15768-15840, docs/sdk/RMGE01_SDK_FUNCTIONS.txt
- **Why paid:** The PosMtxImm helper predates fast float and the lean contracts. NATIVE_EXECUTION Native22 notes 'The exact paired-single GXLoadPosMtxImm helper keeps the full boundary'. Native20 added lean TexMtxImm and LightObjImm through gx_lean::load_xf_paired, but PosMtxImm and NrmMtxImm were not migrated.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native16 guest split (GX 2.5 %), Native20 (these routines plus the psq slow path 330 -> 149 samples)): Per immediate-mode draw outside J3D display lists (game draw code, 2D layouts, particles). Rate unknown; my assumption is 300-3,000 calls per frame. GX as a whole was 2.5 % of guest samples (measured, approximate attribution, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native16).
- **Design:** Translator flag --lean-gx-imm. Add lean_gx_load_pos_mtx_imm = gx_lean::load_xf_paired<12>(memory, r3, ((r4 << 2) & 0xFFFC) | 0x000B0000), with contract (&[3,4], &[], &[], &[]). Add lean_gx_load_nrm_mtx_imm, taking 9 words from the 3x4 matrix to XF 0x400 + 3*id, with count 9. Add lean_gx_set_current_mtx, which sets __GXData matIdxA and writes the CP/XF matrix-index commands, as __GXSetMatrixIndex does. Add lean_gx_call_display_list: when __GXData dirty or bpSentNot is set, call the translated __GXSetDirtyState/__GXSendFlushPrim through a cold path, then append 0x40, addr, size to the pipe. Validate with bench/diff_gx (gather-pipe bytes and RAM identical over 20,000 trials).
- **Risk** (low): The psq-copy store image must flush denormals exactly as load_xf_paired already does for TexMtxImm (validated in Native20). GXCallDisplayList's dirty-state branch must stay translated, or command order changes: the concrete failure would be wrong TEV or vertex state on the first draw after a state change, which diff_gx and frame dumps catch.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.1 ms/frame. GX share of the simulation thread = 2.5 % of guest x 62.5 % guest = 1.56 %. est_ms = 0.0156 x 12.9 = 0.20 ms (0.14-0.26 ms over T_sim 8.9-16.9 ms). I assume these four entry points are 20-50 % of GX time: 0.03-0.1 ms.

### 94. H-OSTIME: OSGetTime makes 3 runtime clock reads per call; OSGetTick is a host call plus a clock read

- **Status:** open. Area: OS time natives (OSGetTime/OSGetTick, mftb). Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--lean-os-time / GALAXY_MODULE_CLOCK=1`. Already listed: True docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md section 2.1 (OSGetTick from the host TSC, mftb inline); docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 14 (clock reads)
- **What:** OSGetTime (0x804AC368, 0x18 B) is the mftbu/mftb/mftbu retry loop. Each mftb or mftbu lowers to read_spr, then read_time_base, then services->time_base_ticks, then runtime_time_base_ticks. That is three full runtime clock reads per call, each with a census atomic, a timeline rdtsc CAS, the CPU-time cache test and the host-suspension-candidate logic. OSGetTick (0x804AC380, 8 B: mftb; blr) is on the host-call list (see H-TRACEONLY), so it also pays call_guest_resolved and the 162 KB chain, where its only work is tick_call_count/first/last bookkeeping.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:13000-13020, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12818-12826, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5465-5560, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:26956-26987, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7920
- **Why paid:** The time base is emulated per read with suspension detection: a host pause must not look like elapsed guest time. Each guest TB half is a separate SPR read, as on hardware.
- **Frequency** (measured; '56,000 clock reads/s (9,600 by the game)' (measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md)): 9,600 guest clock reads per second, 160 per frame (measured). OSGetTime accounts for up to 3 of them per call.
- **Design:** Translator flag --lean-os-time. lean_os_get_time does one read_time_base and returns r3 = high and r4 = low (contract: gpr writes 3,4). lean_os_get_tick returns r3 = low (contract: gpr write 3), and 0x804AC380 comes off requires_host_guest_call. Second step under the same flag: a module clock. The runtime publishes {tsc_scale, tsc_shift, tb_base} in a module global (GALAXY_MODULE_CLOCK=1, refreshed on suspension recovery and rebase), and mftb, mftbu, OSGetTime and OSGetTick read rdtsc inline with no services call. Runtime reads keep suspension detection.
- **Risk** (low): Lean OSGetTime/OSGetTick on their own carry no risk: same values, fewer reads. The inline module clock is medium risk. Guest reads stop feeding host-suspension-candidate detection, though runtime reads at 46,000 per second still do. The published scale must be updated atomically with any timeline rebase, otherwise the guest sees time jump backwards; the timeline clamp avoids that today.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.03-0.1 ms/frame. Per-read cost derived from the measured row: 0.033 x 12.9 ms / 933 reads per frame = 0.46 us per read on the 6500U. Lean OSGetTime removes 2 of every 3 OSGetTime reads; if OSGetTime is most of the 160 guest reads per frame, that is about 107 reads, or 0.05 ms (0.034 ms at T_sim 8.9). The inline clock removes all 160: 0.074 ms, or 0.10 ms at T_sim 16.9. OSGetTick's host transition adds 0.3-0.6 us per call (unknown count).

### 95. H-JPA: JPA particle callback lists run as translated bctrl loops; JPA natives are retired or on the exact float model

- **Status:** open. Area: host natives (JPA). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-jpa`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** JPA particle work is 4.7 % of guest samples. Per emitter and per particle, JPACalcChildFuncList and the emitter and draw callback lists call small calc functions through bctrl, each paying the virtual-call register synchronisation (about 68 instructions plus the callee). The hand-written loops native_jpa_calc_child_func_list_80443904, native_jpa_emitter_callback_8044592C, native_jpa_draw_particle_803A3B6C and native_actor_list_apply_80448710 are on the restartability-retired list. The per-particle callbacks native_jpa_alpha_callback_804465A0 and native_jpa_scale_callback_804465CC are FPU helpers on the exact float model (load_fpr_single, ppc_commit_scalar_result), so native_substitution_uses_fpu keeps the translated bodies.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:15768-15840, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1242-1245, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:11942-12062, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:16086-16190
- **Why paid:** The natives were retired because a hand-written loop could not resume at an interior call-return continuation after an exception-path transfer, and because exception 7 could fire. Eager FPU removed the second reason and the cooperative scheduler the first (4 flag transfers per run, all in OSInit). The FPU callbacks were never rewritten for fast float.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native16 guest split (JPA particles 4.7 %)): Per particle per frame for the calc callbacks, per emitter for the lists; counts not measured. JPA share 4.7 % of guest samples (measured, approximate attribution).
- **Design:** Translator flag --lean-jpa. (1) Rewrite the hot per-particle calc callbacks (alpha, scale, colour and rotate animation) as fast-float lean bodies with contracts, validated by a diff harness (diff_jpa) against the translated ones. (2) Add a lean JPACalcChildFuncList loop that compares each function pointer against the lean set and calls the lean body inline, otherwise fast::call_guest_indirect. It checks the cooperative scheduler's pending_event_mask every 32 iterations and falls back to the translated loop when an event is pending.
- **Risk** (medium): Particle visuals: a slightly different float order changes alpha and scale curves. That is acceptable under fast float, but must be compared on frame dumps of particle-heavy scenes (star bits, Launch Star). Event latency inside long particle loops is bounded only if the loop keeps its periodic check.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.07-0.19 ms/frame. JPA share of the simulation thread = 4.7 % x 0.625 = 2.94 %. est_ms = 0.0294 x 12.9 = 0.38 ms (0.26-0.50 ms over T_sim 8.9-16.9 ms). I assume lean callbacks plus the host loop remove 25-40 % of that (per-callback bctrl sync, function entry and exit): 0.07-0.19 ms.

### 96. T-FPS1DUP: Every lfs and scalar single result also stores ps1, even when ps1 is overwritten before anything reads it

- **Status:** done for resident floats (dead ps1 stores eliminated under --abi-exit-sync). Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--elide-dead-ps1`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** load_fpr_single_hw writes context->ps1_bits[index] unconditionally in fast-float modules. write_single writes ps1 for every single arithmetic result (duplicates_single folds to true under --assume-paired-singles). That is one extra 8-byte store (vmovsd [rdi+0x1xx], 9 bytes) per result. In scalar code most of these ps1 values are dead: f0-f13 are reused within a few instructions and scalar code never reads ps1.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:359-372, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:805-812, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:59-67
- **Why paid:** On the Gekko with HID2.PSE set, scalar single results and lfs write ps1 = ps0, and a later ps_merge1x/psq_st/ps_sum can observe them. --assume-paired-singles removed the HID2 test but kept the store.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT mix (measured categories); the dead fraction is a hypothesis): lfs (most of float loads 6.3 %) + single arithmetic (about 3.3 %), about 8.3 % of hot guest instructions write ps1. Hypothesis: 60-90 % of these are dead.
- **Design:** Translator-only ps1 liveness per function, backward and per register. A ps1 write is dead if the same register is written again (by an instruction that writes ps1) before any instruction that reads ps1 (paired ops, ps_merge*, ps_sum, two-element psq_st, fmr-like ps_mr), any call, sc, mtmsr, return, interior entry or block exit to an unknown successor. Emit `load_fpr_single_hw<false>` / `scalar_*<..., Dup=false>` variants without the ps1 store. Falls out for free under T-FTYPED.
- **Risk** (low): Failure mode: a ps1 read the analysis misses (an interior entry, or an exception or OSSaveContext psq_st reached through a path not treated as a read) sees a stale ps1, usually the previous value of the same register. Treating every call, transfer, interior entry and exit as a read makes it conservative. Unit-testable on emitted text.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.017-0.11 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). 8.3 % x 60-90 % dead = 0.05-0.075 stores per guest instruction (0.6-0.9 % of x86 instructions; stores are cheap but take store-buffer slots and code bytes); f = 0.3-1 %. Low = 0.625*0.003*8.9 = 0.017; high = 0.625*0.01*16.9 = 0.11 ms.

### 97. R-WGPIPE-TEST-NONSTACK: The gather-pipe address test on every non-stack RAM store

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: gather pipe (generated-code side). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--wgpipe-proven-sites <file>`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md instruction table (gather-pipe address test on stfs and stw) and 'What closes the gap' item 1
- **What:** Under fastmem every fast::store and psq/stfs store that is not proven stack-relative carries '(address & 0x3FFFF000) != 0x0C008000' (and, cmp, jcc) before the flat movbe store, so the pipe stays off the fault path. r1-relative stores (store_stack) and _savegpr r11 stores (--stack-r11-stores) already skip it.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:199-206, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:224-245, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:2921-2924
- **Why paid:** A store through a dynamic pointer might target the pipe. A pipe store that faulted would cost ~1 us (bench/fault_cost.cpp), and the pipe takes tens of thousands of stores per frame.
- **Frequency** (derived; Instruction mix and the 60 % stack share in docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md): Every non-stack store: 40 % of the hot stores (8.9 % integer + 3.2 % float + part of 3.7 % paired), about 5 % of hot guest instructions.
- **Design:** Two parts. (a) Use the must-analysis (R-WGPIPE-STORE-INLINE) to prove pipe sites. (b) Ship a site list ([wgpipe-site] pc counter from a runtime recording). With --wgpipe-proven-sites <file>, stores not in the list and not proven drop the test. A missed pipe store faults into the existing vectored handler, which completes it through write_device -> write_wgpipe_bytes (correct, just slow) and logs [wgpipe-unlisted-site] so the next translation adds it.
- **Risk** (medium): Correctness holds through the fault path. A hot site missing from the list costs ~1 us per store: a GX loop with 10k stores per frame would add ~10 ms. The list must come from full-route coverage (boot, title, gameplay, Home menu, movies), and an unlisted-fault counter must fail CI above a threshold.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.2 ms/frame. About 5 % of hot guest instructions x 3 of ~8 x86 instructions = ~1.9 % of game-code instructions; game code is 62.5 % of the sim thread, giving ~1.2 % of instructions. Cycle share is lower (well-predicted, macro-fused), so assume 0.4-1.5 % of the sim thread x 12.9 ms = 0.05-0.2 ms. Smaller code also helps the 4 MB L3 (not counted).

### 98. T-PRESERVE-NONE: Win64 callee-saved register traffic at every translated function entry and exit

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: function entry/exit, calling convention. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--preserve-none-calls`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md instruction table ('Function entry and exit ~40: 8 host register pushes and pops'); the cost is listed but no design is
- **What:** Every translated body is an ordinary Win64 C++ function. Its prologue and epilogue push and pop up to 8 callee-saved GPRs (rbx, rbp, rdi, rsi, r12-r15) and save xmm6-15 when float temporaries need them. Yet after any call the residency pass reloads every guest register from context, so a caller keeps almost nothing live across a call except context, memory, services and flat_guest_base.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:10280-10339, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3522-3537, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:665-687, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:935-950, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:10888-10935
- **Why paid:** Translated functions use the plain C++ calling convention so the MSVC-built runtime can call them through NativeGameFunction pointers (the kFunctions table, the direct dispatch table and call_guest_resolved).
- **Frequency** (derived; instruction mix measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md; 'Function entry and exit ~40 ... 8 host register pushes and pops' (CPU_GAP_REPORT)): Once per translated call. Function executions are roughly the returns share, 4.2% of hot guest instructions.
- **Design:** - Declare translated bodies __attribute__((preserve_none)) for module-internal direct calls. Clang 19+ supports it; cr0_fusion.rs cites the pinned clang-cl 23.1.2.
- Emit a C-ABI wrapper fn_X_abi for every function the runtime can reach: kFunctions, galaxy_lookup_function, call_guest_resolved/call_guest_cached arguments and direct-dispatch entries. The MSVC runtime then never calls preserve_none code.
- context, memory and services live in a frame slot and are reloaded after calls.
- Pair with T-BCTRL-GATE so bctrl calls a preserve_none table directly.
- **Risk** (high): Toolchain support for preserve_none on x86_64-windows (SEH unwind data, and the /EHsc GuestTransfer fallback unwinding through such frames) must first be proven on the pinned clang-cl; local clang 18 ignores the attribute. A missing wrapper crashes on the first runtime-to-module call. Gate it on a throw-through unwind test plus diff harnesses.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.1-0.4 ms/frame. On Win64, 16 push/pop minus about 3-5 post-call reloads of the pinned arguments leaves 11-13 instructions per call. The asm-probe call_direct disassembly (SysV) shows 6 pushes plus push rax. Calculation: 4.2% x 11-13 = 0.46 to 0.55 x86 per guest instruction. Push and pop are cheap stack-engine uops, so count them at 40-70%: 2.3 to 4.8% of game code, giving 0.19 to 0.39 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms. The low bound is widened to 0.1.

### 99. H-WPAD: WPAD/WUD Bluetooth stack and the 500 Hz BTU alarm still run as translated code under synthetic input

- **Status:** open. Area: PAD/WPAD/KPAD natives. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--host-wpad / GALAXY_HOST_WPAD=1`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 9; docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md section 6
- **What:** KPADRead (0x804506D8) is a host boundary that fills KPADStatus from the host input model in keyboard, mouse and controller modes. Below it, the translated WPAD driver, the WUD/BTE Bluetooth stack and HID report parsing still run, fed through the emulated /dev/usb/oh1 IPC path at the raw-report boundary. The BTU timer alarm still fires at 500 Hz through the decrementer alarm pass. WPADRead 0x804D93C8 and WPADProbe 0x804D8ACC are additionally withheld from the direct table, and WPADRead's five memcpy calls keep the translated memcpy and a checkpoint for the input trace.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:25213-25352, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7764-7770, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15856-15857, docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md:389-410, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:331
- **Why paid:** Input was first brought up at the HID report boundary so that a real Wii Remote over Bluetooth works unchanged. The synthetic modes were added later at KPADRead, without removing the stack below.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 9 (WPadAcceleration 1.8 %); docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native16 guest split (BT/WPAD/KPAD/SC 0.7 %), Native12 ('the 500 Hz BTU alarm')): 500 BTU alarms per second (8.3 per frame), plus per-report WPAD processing at 100-200 Hz. Measured shares: WPadAcceleration 1.8 % of game-code samples (gap report) and Bluetooth/WPAD/KPAD/SC 0.7 % of guest samples (Native16).
- **Design:** Translator --host-wpad adds WPADInit, WPADRead, WPADProbe, WPADGetStatus, WPADSetDataFormat, WPADControl*, WPADGetInfoAsync and the WUD entry points to the host-call list. Runtime GALAXY_HOST_WPAD=1 (default 0) applies only when the input mode is synthetic. WPADInit registers channel 0 as connected without starting the BTU alarm or the WUD thread. WPADRead and WPADProbe answer from the host model. KPADRead stays as today. A real Wii Remote, or GALAXY_HOST_WPAD=0, keeps the full stack.
- **Risk** (medium): The game reads WPAD state outside KPADRead: connection callbacks, the extension type (Nunchuk), battery, speaker and rumble. A missing callback is a concrete failure: the Nunchuk error screen seen with F20 or a 'connect the Wii Remote' prompt. The SC/SYSCONF BT.DINF lookup in SCCheckStatus must still be satisfied.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.05-0.2 ms/frame. est_ms = share x T_sim with share = guest fraction x 0.625. Low bound: 0.7 % of guest x 0.625 x 12.9 ms = 0.056 ms. High bound: (0.7 + 1.8) % x 0.625 x 12.9 ms = 0.20 ms. Add 8.3 alarm dispatches per frame x 2-5 us (alarm pass, handler on the fiber, DEC re-arm, clock reads; hypothesis) = 0.02-0.04 ms. WPadAcceleration is game code reading KPADStatus, so only the part caused by multi-sample reads disappears; the synthetic KPADRead already returns 1 sample.

### 100. G-BCTRL-SERVICE-LOADS: Each bctrl re-reads five services fields and chases two atomic pointers before the table load

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: services table. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--dispatch-globals (translator); runtime publishes the combined gate word when the module asks for it`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:82 ('dispatch-table checks' in the ~68-instruction bctrl)
- **What:** call_guest_indirect loads services->direct_dispatch, direct_dispatch_base and direct_dispatch_count. It then loads the table entry and dereferences services->pending_event_mask (the latch mask_) and services->direct_dispatch_suspended (ExternalDispatchTracker flag). That is three extra lines (services, latch, tracker) on the critical path of every indirect call, and the table address depends on two of the services loads. --checkpoint-globals already snapshots the checkpoint gate into the module global g_module_checkpoint_gate, but the dispatch fields were not moved.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:2047-2057, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:484-494, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:15160-15177, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:33481-33487
- **Why paid:** ABI 24 published the table through the services struct, and the per-call checks keep the audited boundary when events are pending.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Derived from the instruction mix, CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:86-99; bctrl share assumed): Once per bctrl, an estimated 20 k-80 k per frame (see G-DISPATCH-DENSE).
- **Design:** In galaxy_module_init, snapshot table, base, count and the suspended-flag pointer into a module global (next to g_module_checkpoint_gate). Better, have the translator emit base and count as constexpr from the function records it already writes (translate.rs:11469-11478) and read the table pointer RIP-relative. Merge pending_event_mask and direct_dispatch_suspended into one word the runtime keeps updated (OR of both), so the fast path does one atomic load.
- **Risk** (low): The module global must be refreshed when the runtime rebuilds or withdraws the table (diagnostic modes disable it at startup only today). A combined mask word must be updated wherever either source changes, or calls would bypass the boundary while an interrupt dispatch is live.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.013-0.11 ms/frame. About 2-4 cycles saved per bctrl on the dependent path (L1-hit loads removed from the address chain) x 20 k-80 k bctrl/frame = 40 k-320 k cycles = 0.013-0.11 ms at 3 GHz.

### 101. R-SUBMIT-FIXED: Fixed per-submission overhead on the simulation thread: two queue-mutex sections, count_if scans, chunk construction and move, unpooled event vectors, condvar wake

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: FIFO submission / allocation. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_SUBMIT_LEAN (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Each GxBackend::render_frame does the following: builds a FrameChunk (pointer-depth shared_ptrs, a MemorySnapshot member, fifo marker arrays); locks queue_mutex_ and count_if-scans frame_queue_ to admit; pops pooled vectors; drains and coalesces dirty ranges (sort); runs invalidate_dependency_range_cache and the display-list invalidation passes; locks queue_mutex_ again to issue the token and push_back the large FrameChunk into the deque; then calls queue_cv_.notify_one(), a kernel wake when the render thread is waiting. preclassified_pe_events is not pooled, so a chunk with an event allocates. Streaming adds another lock and count_if in render_queue_has_space and a deque entry in pending_draw_done_submissions.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5593-5612, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5670-5686, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5700-5799, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5936-5992, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/gx_backend.h:523-580, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:6273-6286
- **Why paid:** Generality. The same function serves the synchronous, snapshot, worker-snapshot and live-memory modes and the diagnostics. Its queue admission recounts on each call instead of keeping a counter.
- **Frequency** (derived; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:287 (about 5 chunks per frame); code paths in native_runtime.cpp:17037-17199): Once per submission, about 6-8 per frame (5 streamed + boundary + sync and pointer-depth captures).
- **Design:** Keep an atomic count of non-present queued chunks, replacing both count_if scans and the render_queue_has_space lock. Pool preclassified_pe_events with the other chunk vectors. Store FrameChunk in a fixed ring of preconstructed slots instead of a deque of moved objects. Notify only when the render thread has announced it is waiting, as the idle-wait publication already does. In live-memory mode skip invalidate_dependency_range_cache when dependency_range_cache_ is empty.
- **Risk** (low): A missed notification would stall the render thread until the next submission. Keep a bounded wait_for timeout on the render side, as for idle wakes.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.1 ms/frame. An assumed 3-12 us per submission on the 6500U (two mutex round trips, deque move of a large struct, a futex/condvar wake syscall of ~1-3 us when the render thread sleeps, sort of a few ranges) x 6-8 submissions = 0.02-0.1 ms. Anchor: the Native11 desktop 'simulation thread submit cost 0.16-0.28 ms per frame' (docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:287) included the then-full classifier parse, so the fixed part is unknown.

### 102. R-LATCH-LOCK: Latch publications call SetEvent while holding the per-kind spin lock (and the broker mutex)

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: event latch. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_LATCH_WAKE_OUTSIDE_LOCK (default 0)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Event latch lock 0.53 %' (row only; the SetEvent-under-lock cause is not written down)
- **What:** PendingEventLatch::publish, publish_periodic and publish_replaceable call wake_idle_waiters -> SetEvent (a kernel call, 1.34 us measured on the desktop) before unlock_slot. The broker publishes under its mutex_, so both locks are held across the syscall. The simulation thread, woken by that SetEvent, immediately takes the same slot (take_*, clear_replaceable) and spins in lock_slot with _mm_pause (about 140 cycles per pause on Skylake, up to 1,024 pauses before yield), and replace_once/cancel_once from the simulation thread block on the broker mutex for the same span. lock_slot's contention statistics use two more CAS loops per contended acquisition.
- **Where:** runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:871-902, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:904-974, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1003-1042, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1062-1117, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1984-2033, runtime/include/galaxy/runtime_timeline.h:301-460
- **Why paid:** Each publication (count, periodic identity, wake bit) is made atomic by a per-kind spin lock; the idle wake was added inside the publication so a waiter can never miss it; the broker holds mutex_ while publishing so replace_once cannot return while an obsolete event is in flight.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md ('Event latch lock 0.53 %', 420 broker wakes/s); docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native15/16 (SetEvent 1.34 us)): per latch publication and per consumer take; VI 60/s, input reports, AI, DSP, IOS publications plus 420 broker wakes/s
- **Design:** Move wake_idle_waiters after unlock_slot in all three publish functions (the mask bit is already set with release before the idle_waiting seq_cst load, so the wake protocol is unchanged), and in the broker collect the kinds to wake and signal after releasing mutex_. Optionally pack count and wake bit so pending/take of non-periodic kinds use a single atomic exchange without the slot lock.
- **Risk** (low): The idle waiter re-tests the mask after announcing itself (native_runtime.cpp:18878-18887); a wake issued after the unlock still lands after the mask bit, so no wake is lost. A late SetEvent can wake a later wait spuriously (auto-reset event), which only costs one extra pass.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.03-0.09 ms/frame. Measured share 0.53 % -> est_ms = 0.0053 * 8.9-16.9 = 0.047-0.09 ms/frame; the spin is waiting for a syscall that the design moves out of the critical section, assume 65-100 % removable -> 0.03-0.09 ms.

### 103. T-PSLAYOUT: Paired operands are rebuilt from two split context arrays on every ps_* instruction

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--float-typed-locals (paired part) / --paired-vector-locals`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** ps0 lives in context->fpr_bits[32] (offset 0x80) and ps1 in context->ps1_bits[32] (offset 0x180). Every paired operand is make_pair: two 8-byte loads plus vunpcklpd or vpunpcklqdq. Every paired result is write_pair: vmovsd plus vpextrq/vmovhpd (a shuffle uop). Measured in the scratchpad probe: psq_l, psq_l, ps_mul, ps_madd, ps_sum0, ps_merge00, psq_st runs 103 hot x86 instructions, about 15 per guest instruction. The vector arithmetic itself is 1-2 instructions per ps op.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1003-1025, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1078-1114, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:249-252, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4801-4812, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4877-4888
- **Why paid:** PpcContext keeps the two lanes in separate arrays (runtime ABI layout). The fast helpers are index-based against the context, so every paired instruction round-trips both lanes through memory.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT instruction mix (measured category, split hypothesised); per-op cost from the probe disassembly (derived)): Every paired arithmetic, move and merge instruction. These are part of 'paired single, other float' 3.7 % (measured category); the paired-arithmetic subset is about 2.5-3 % of hot guest instructions (hypothesis).
- **Design:** Part of --float-typed-locals. Every register touched by a paired instruction in a function becomes one `__m128d pN` local (lane 0 = ps0, lane 1 = ps1). ps_merge/ps_mr/ps_neg become shuffles or xors, and scalar reads use _mm_cvtsd_f64. The two context halves are written only at sync points. Do not change the PpcContext layout (runtime ABI 27, read everywhere).
- **Risk** (medium): Same failure mode as T-FTYPED (a stale context at non-ABI readers such as OSSaveContext's psq_st of all FPRs). A missed lane write-back at a sync point would corrupt ps1 seen by a callee or after an exception. Gate with diff_sdkmath/diff_tvec3 and frame dumps.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.028-0.21 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). About 2.5-3 % of guest instructions x (2-3 operands x 2 extra instructions + 1 extra on the result) = 0.15-0.2 x86 per guest instruction, about 2 % of game-code instructions; time f = 0.5-2 %. Low = 0.625*0.005*8.9 = 0.028; high = 0.625*0.02*16.9 = 0.21 ms. Counted apart from T-FTYPED, which covers scalar traffic only.

### 104. T-FEST: fres/frsqrte/ps_res/ps_rsqrte stay on the exact out-of-line path: two library calls with FPSCR status commit, and ps_res forces a full GPR sync

- **Status:** done (--fast-float-forms). Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--inline-float-estimates`. Already listed: False Documented as behaviour, not as a cost: NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:34 and :64-65
- **What:** Even in fast-float modules, fres and frsqrte emit ppc_f64_reciprocal(_sqrt)_estimate (an out-of-line call into galaxy_ppc_float returning a struct through memory) and then ppc_commit_scalar_result (a second out-of-line call applying FX/FPRF/FI/FR and checking enabled exceptions). The probe shows 2 calls, 18 caller-side instructions plus the callees. ps_res/ps_rsqrte also call require_paired_single_mode and ppc_commit_paired_estimate_result. That callee is on neither residency list, so it is classified as a Full sync point: every written resident GPR local is stored before it and every used one reloaded after it.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4665-4684, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4968-4982, runtime/src/ppc_float.cpp:1632-1727, runtime/src/ppc_float.cpp:1761-1771, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:88-155, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:226-241
- **Why paid:** The estimates are table-defined Gekko results. Fast float kept the exact tables (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:64-65; '33 exact commits'), and the table lives in ppc_float.cpp, not in a header.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Static count measured (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:34); the dynamic rate is a hypothesis): 33 static sites (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:34, measured). Executions: hypothesis 500-5,000 per frame (vector normalize and sqrt in actor, camera and collision code not covered by the lean PSVECNormalize body).
- **Design:** Header: move estimate_detail::kReciprocal/kReciprocalRoot and the bit-exact interpolation into an inline constexpr table in native_fast_paths.h. Add `fast::fres/frsqrte/ps_res/ps_rsqrte` (and _r forms) that write the widened single with no FPSCR status or commit call, and add them to the residency 'leaves register file alone' list. The translator emits them under native_float + fast_float.
- **Risk** (low): Estimates stay bit-identical. FPSCR FI/FR/ZX/VXSQRT/VXSNAN are no longer set (fast float already drops status), and an enabled-exception trap is no longer raised, which RMGE01 does not enable. NaN payload handling must keep the quieted payload.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.0056-0.11 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). About 120-160 instructions per execution (2 calls + status + GPR sync) x 500-5,000 per frame = 0.06-0.8 M instructions per frame, against an assumed game-code budget of about 15-50 M instructions per frame; f = 0.1-1 %. Low = 0.625*0.001*8.9 = 0.0056; high = 0.625*0.01*16.9 = 0.11 ms.

### 105. R-HID-REPORT-RATE: 100 Hz virtual HID report rate drives per-report runtime and guest Bluetooth/WPAD work and idle wakes

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: input / timing. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_VIRTUAL_HID_REPORT_HZ (default 100)`. Already listed: False Plan item 9 (WPAD/KPAD as host calls) would remove the guest part entirely; the report rate itself is not discussed
- **What:** The broker publishes an InputReport edge at a provisional 100 Hz. Each edge does three things: it wakes the idle simulation thread, builds a snapshot (R-INPUT-REPORT-SYSCALLS), and makes the translated Bluetooth/WPAD stack process one report. KPAD then consumes the samples at the 60 Hz frame rate.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_input.h:22-27, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:33600-33617, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5940-5957
- **Why paid:** kProvisionalVirtualHidReportRateHz = 100 approximates a real Wii Remote in continuous reporting mode. It was chosen as provisional and decoupled from VI, so that input does not become a VI clock.
- **Frequency** (derived; native_input.h:24-27): 100 edges/s, about 1.67 per frame
- **Design:** Make the rate configurable and try 60 Hz, or 120 Hz phase-locked to VI (two per VI). 60 Hz removes 40 % of the reports, their snapshot builds and their idle wakes; phase-locking merges the input wake into the VI wake. This needs a check of KPAD pointer smoothing and shake detection at the lower rate, with a recorded-input replay A/B against the frame dumps.
- **Risk** (medium): KPAD smoothing and acceleration integration count samples, so pointer feel and shake thresholds could change at 60 Hz. WPAD connection or timeout logic could also assume a minimum report rate.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.04-0.19 ms/frame. Guest Bluetooth/WPAD/KPAD/SC is 0.7 % of guest time (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:468); WPadAcceleration alone is 1.8 % of game-code samples (CPU_GAP_REPORT plan item 9). Treat 0.7-1.8 % of game code (62.5 % of the simulation thread) as per-report work: 0.44-1.1 % of the thread. est_ms = share * thread_ms: 0.0044 * 8.9 = 0.04 ms to 0.011 * 16.9 = 0.19 ms. Moving to 60 Hz would save about 40 % of that plus 40 wakes/s. That this work scales with report rate is a hypothesis.

### 106. T-CONTINUATION-STUBS: Call-return continuations make every call site an interior entry with its own cold stub, label and switch case

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: interior entries, code size. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--coop-continuations-only (translator); runtime GALAXY_TRACE_RESUME_PCS=0 for validation`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT plan item 4 ('...their shared cold blocks and interior entries')
- **What:** call_return_continuations adds every direct and indirect call return address of every function as an external interior entry. Each one gets a `case` in the entry switch, a label that splits the hot block after the call (a join point), a `call_return_X:` stub with its own checkpoint test and goto, and in resident bodies a resident_entry stub and a shared-block switch case. In the compiled synthetic body 123 of 273 instructions (45 %) were this cold machinery, placed between hot blocks rather than after them.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1215-1254, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1819-1863, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3550-3582, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:6248-6275, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:811-843, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:989-1013
- **Why paid:** A decrementer or external interrupt taken at a checkpoint could freeze a thread at any call depth, and the old LR-trampoline resume unwound through every outer frame's return address, so every return site had to be callable. Flat transfers and OSLoadContext/RFI continuation also use them.
- **Frequency** (measured; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md (Leaf calls, F6); CPU_GAP_REPORT code-size table; compiled synthetic body): Static: 283,457 call-return sites, 178,776 interior entries; dynamic cost is front-end and cache footprint (7.7 MB hot code vs 4 MB L3), not executed instructions.
- **Design:** Under the cooperative scheduler (fibers keep native stacks, 4 flat transfers per run, all in OSInit): (1) keep call-return continuations only in functions the static RFI/flat-transfer taint reaches (rfi_unwind_continuations plus the OSInit path) and drop them elsewhere, which also removes the labels so the post-call code is no longer a join. (2) For the remaining ones, make the stub a plain `goto resident_entry_X` with no checkpoint (with --checkpoint-free). (3) Emit cold stubs after the final return inside `if (__builtin_expect(...))` regions, or build with clang PGO plus -fsplit-machine-functions where COFF supports it, so hot blocks are contiguous.
- **Risk** (high): If any runtime path still resumes a frame mid-function after an unwind (flag-transfer resume, OSLoadContext continuation, the Home Button sidecar's flag returns), a missing case hits guest_execution_fault('invalid interior function entry'). Needs the runtime to prove that with GALAXY_COOP on no resume targets a dropped continuation (count resumes by pc in a validation build).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.42 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G. Stub bytes are about 7 % of .text (283k sites x ~50 B against 194 MB) and up to ~45 % of a call-dense function's instructions, but cold. The measured 1.5x CPU from a 32 MB vs 96 MB L3 shows footprint matters; assume a 0.5-4 % game-code gain on a 4 MB L3: 0.005 x 5.56 = 0.03; 0.04 x 10.56 = 0.42.

### 107. R-WGPIPE-STORE-INLINE: Each gather-pipe store pays the flat-path miss, a second address test and a 3-pointer append protocol

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: gather pipe (generated-code side). Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--wgpipe-direct-append (translator); runtime publishes the cursor fields unconditionally`. Already listed: True docs/PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P10 item 5 (inline the WGPIPE check out of fast::store); docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md stage B
- **What:** A GX command or vertex store goes through fast::store. First the fastmem test (address & 0x3FFFF000) != 0x0C008000 fails. Then wgpipe_address() does two compares. Then wgpipe_append loads gather, count and flush from GuestMemoryV1 and null-tests all three, loads *count, does a bound check, memcpy and store count, and compares against 4096. That is roughly 18-22 x86 instructions for a 4-byte GX word, compared with ~5 for a cursor bump.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:109-138, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:190-222, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:2811-2924
- **Why paid:** The translator cannot tell a store to the pipe from any other. Only multi-store runs whose address it proves locally get special handling (and those take the slow host path, R-WGPIPE-BATCH-SLOWPATH). The append protocol tolerates the buffer being unpublished when diagnostics are on.
- **Frequency** (derived; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:167 (61-126 KB/frame) divided by an assumed mean store width of 3-4 bytes): Once per GX store: 61-126 KB per frame at 1-8 bytes per store, about 15k-42k stores per frame.
- **Design:** Translator: reuse the existing CFG must-analysis for device bases (NATIVE_EXECUTION 'Device accesses under fastmem', constants met at joins), together with is_wgpipe_address, to prove single WGPIPE stores, including loop bodies whose lis/addi base is hoisted. Emit galaxy::fast::wgpipe_put<T>(memory, v). Runtime: publish a cursor/limit pair (std::byte* wgpipe_cursor, *wgpipe_limit) in GuestMemoryV1. The put is 'if (cursor + N <= limit) { store bswap(v); cursor += N; } else wgpipe_put_slow()', and the slow path flushes or falls back to write_device. Diagnostics set limit = cursor so every put takes the slow path.
- **Risk** (low): Only proven pipe addresses use it. An ABI bump is required, because GuestMemoryV1 gains two fields and the host must keep cursor and count coherent at every drain. If the publication rules are mishandled, diagnostic observers see bytes out of order.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.03-0.08 ms/frame. About 14 x86 instructions saved per store x 15k-42k stores per frame / (2.5 IPC x 3.0 GHz) = 0.03-0.08 ms. This cost is counted in the game-code 62.5 %, not in the runtime 0.55 %.

### 108. T-FSTACK: stfs/stfsx/psq_st addressed from r1 still run the gather-pipe address test (stfd and stw already use store_stack)

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--stack-float-stores`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'What closes the gap' item 1 (r1/r11-relative accesses skip the gather-pipe test); done for stw/stfd/_savegpr, not for stfs/psq_st
- **What:** stfs lowers to fast::store_fpr_single_hw, which uses store<uint32_t> with the `(address & 0x3FFFF000) != 0x0C008000` test (mov, and, cmp, je) and inlines the gather-append slow path: 230 bytes per site in the probe, against 35 for store_stack. stfd uses flat_store_helper(base) and so store_stack for r1. psq_st has the same test inside psq_store_impl. --stack-r11-stores covers only the _savegpr expansion.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4315-4327, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4328-4334, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3086-3092, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:190-245, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:374-381, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:510-512
- **Why paid:** The float store helpers were written before store_stack existed, and the translator never passes the base register to them.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT (stack share of all stores measured; the float-store stack share is a hypothesis)): Float stores are 3.2 % of hot guest instructions (measured). Hypothesis: 30-60 % are r1/r11-relative (60 % of all hot stores are stack-relative, measured; float locals spill with stfs; paired nonvolatile saves use psq_st from r1).
- **Design:** Add `fast::store_fpr_single_hw_stack` (narrow + store_stack) and a stack bool on psq_store_hw. In translate.rs, select them when rA == 1, and for r11 under the existing --stack-r11-stores proof. A one-line choice per lowering, like flat_store_helper.
- **Risk** (low): None for guest state: r1 is always RAM and never the gather pipe. A non-RAM address still faults into the device path, as stfd/stw store_stack already do.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.017-0.085 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). 4 x86 x (3.2 % x 30-60 %) = 0.04-0.08 x86 per guest instruction (0.5-1 % of instructions), plus about 195 bytes of inline cold code per site; f = 0.3-0.8 %. Low = 0.625*0.003*8.9 = 0.017; high = 0.625*0.008*16.9 = 0.085 ms.

### 109. T-I2F: CodeWarrior int<->float idioms bounce through the stack: two 4-byte stores feeding an 8-byte lfd fail store forwarding
