- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--forward-stack-conversions`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** CodeWarrior converts int to float with `xoris r0,rX,0x8000; stw r0,d+4(r1); lis r0,0x4330; stw r0,d(r1); lfd fY,d(r1); fsub(s) fY,fY,fMagic`. Translated, that is two separate 4-byte movbe stores and then an 8-byte movbe load of the same slot (translate.rs:4285-4290). On Skylake a load spanning two stores cannot forward and stalls about 12+ cycles until the stores retire. Float to int is `fctiwz f0,f1; stfd f0,d(r1); lwz rX,d+4(r1)`: fast::fctiwz writes the context (about 11 instructions in the probe), then an 8-byte store and a dependent 4-byte reload through store forwarding (about 5 cycles).
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4222-4302, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4514-4531, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:866-876, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1819-1840
- **Why paid:** The translator lowers instruction by instruction, so the guest's memory round trip is reproduced literally, and x86 store forwarding cannot merge two narrow stores into one wider load.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Idiom shape from CodeWarrior codegen conventions; stall from Intel store-forwarding rules; the frequency is a hypothesis): Every int->float and float->int conversion in game code. Hypothesis: the lfd of the idiom is 0.05-0.3 % of hot guest instructions (6-instruction idiom); not in the measured mix.
- **Design:** Translator peephole within a basic block: when `lfd fY,d(r1)` is preceded, with no intervening r1 write, label or other store to [d, d+8), by `stw rA,d(r1)` and `stw rB,d+4(r1)`, keep both stores but emit `fpr[Y] = (u64(rA_value) << 32) | rB_value`. Similarly forward `stfd fX,d(r1); lwz rZ,d+4(r1)` to `gpr[Z] = u32(fpr[X])`. With typed locals this becomes `(double)(int32)` / cvttsd2si directly.
- **Risk** (low): The stores stay, so memory contents are unchanged and only the load source changes. Failure mode: a pattern-match bug (wrong offset or a missed intervening store) yields a wrong float, which is visible quickly in UI and timers. Unit tests on emitted text; the differential harnesses (diff_tier2) cover the conversions.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.0056-0.095 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). About 12 stall cycles x 0.05-0.3 % frequency, against about 4-5 cycles per guest instruction (8 x86 at IPC 1.5-2), gives f = 0.1-0.9 %. Low = 0.625*0.001*8.9 = 0.0056; high = 0.625*0.009*16.9 = 0.095 ms.

### 110. R-CALL-IMPL: Host-routed direct calls walk the whole call_guest_impl_body interceptor chain

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: calls through the runtime. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_HOST_CALL_KIND_TABLE (runtime, default 0); --host-call-kinds (translator)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Calls through the runtime 1.8 %' (row only, no design); PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P9 'lookup_function 425 samples'
- **What:** A generated call to any target in requires_host_guest_call (about 50 listed addresses plus the entire 0x8041xxxx range) goes call_guest_resolved (module: two trace gates with thread-safe statics) -> services->call_guest_resolved -> call_guest_impl (InputReport latch test, normalize_executable_address = lookup_function hash-cache probe, record_vi_post_rfi_handoff x2, external-dispatch test) -> call_guest_impl_body: a ~2,870-line function with 73 guest_address comparisons, a second normalize_executable_address, coop_call_intercept switch, record_call (pc_history ring + trace_guest_call), mouse/movie/scene marker tests, PreparedGuestSetCurrentContext, ~20 trace-gate locals (trace_native_input_call, trace_dvd_result, trace_home_button_resolver...), run_with_optional_exception_handler, commit_guest_set_current_context and the post-call trace tail, before and after the one interceptor (if any) that the target actually needs.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7020-7081, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7633-7703, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:13339-13408, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30759-30766, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27045-27075, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:24129-26996, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:29140-29150, runtime/include/galaxy/flat_dispatch.h:119-141, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:23929-23964
- **Why paid:** Interceptors were added one by one inside one generic body keyed on guest_address (OS sleep/wake, VI wait, scheduler, KPAD, DSP commands, movie and mouse transactions, diagnostics); the translator routes a whole region (0x8041xxxx) and every listed address through it so the runtime can decide at run time.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; code: translate.rs:7643-7703; measured total 'calls through the runtime 1.8 %' in docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md covers this and R-CALL-CACHED together): per call to a host-routed target; per-target rates not measured (SelectThread, OSSleepThread, VIWaitForRetrace, KPADRead, DSP commands and the 0x8041xxxx range are the candidates)
- **Design:** Build a per-target HostCallKind table at boot (direct-indexed by (address - base) >> 2, like the direct dispatch table) and switch on it at call_guest_impl entry so a call runs only its own interceptor plus a common minimal prologue (pending_transfer test, InputReport test, context->pc). Hoist all trace locals behind one 'any call diagnostics' gate. In the translator, stop routing targets whose only runtime work is a diagnostic or a static range rule: emit them as direct calls unless the kind table says host (generate the list from the runtime's kind table so the two cannot diverge), and pass the precomputed kind to a new call_guest_kind helper so the runtime does not normalize the address twice.
- **Risk** (medium): Each interceptor depends on exact entry conditions (lr, external-dispatch ownership, coop state); moving it to a kind switch must keep its order relative to input service and the pc publication (the long comment at 24203-24215 records a real bug from a stale context->pc). Dropping 0x8041xxxx from host routing needs the reason for that range (not documented in the code read) checked first. Differential: Home menu, save, movie and mouse routes plus flag-return counts.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.17 ms/frame. Measured 'calls through the runtime' 1.8 % -> est_ms = 0.018 * 8.9-16.9 = 0.16-0.30 ms/frame for the whole row. Assume 30-70 % of it is this path and 60-80 % of that is removable overhead: low 0.3*0.018*8.9*0.6 = 0.03, high 0.7*0.018*16.9*0.8 = 0.17 ms.

### 111. R-CALL-CACHED: Indirect calls to withheld targets pay the full cached-call service boundary

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: calls through the runtime (bctrl). Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_DIRECT_DISPATCH_CLASSES (runtime, default 0 = today's all-or-nothing withholding); --dispatch-condition-bytes (translator, module tests the per-target condition byte)`. Already listed: True PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P10.4 'shrink the withheld set'; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Calls through the runtime 1.8 %'
- **What:** A bctrl whose target has a null direct-dispatch entry (2,149-3,331 withheld targets: everything in direct_dispatch_target_requires_host, the 0x8041xxxx range, every address the cached service compares against) goes call_guest_indirect_boundary -> call_guest_cached (module: cached_call_trace_enabled + profile gate) -> runtime call_guest_cached: pending_transfer test, InputReport latch test, RuntimeBoundarySelfScope, the scene-marker test (lr/r2 compare), cached_call_has_required_entry_hook, needs_call_guest_intercept (~12 tests incl. 3 thread-safe statics, external-dispatch evaluation), a second InputReport test when external dispatch is active, frame_activity_recording_enabled, cached_call_diagnostics_enabled, and record_vi_post_rfi_handoff before and after the call.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1863-1905, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14119-14143, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30795-30896, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15821-15864, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15921-15975, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:3669-3713
- **Why paid:** The cached-call path is the audited boundary for targets with host transactions keyed on the call (mouse capture, movie markers, scene markers, scheduler) and for diagnostics; the per-site cache only skips the address lookup, and the intercept decision is re-evaluated on every hit because input and exception ownership can change between calls.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md (326,540 published, 3,331 withheld at F19; 2,149 withheld per P10.4)): per bctrl to a withheld target, plus every bctrl while the direct table is withdrawn (R-DISPATCH-WITHDRAW); rate not measured
- **Design:** Split the withheld set by reason at build_direct_dispatch_table time into a per-target byte table: (a) needs host every call, (b) needs host only while a live condition holds (external dispatch, mouse frame, trace window), (c) diagnostic-only. Publish (c) targets directly when diagnostics are off and (b) targets behind their condition word (the module tests one extra byte before calling directly). In the runtime fast hit, test one precomputed 'warm hit is plain' bit instead of re-running needs_call_guest_intercept, and collapse the two record_vi_post_rfi_handoff calls and the diagnostics tests behind one hot-path diagnostics word.
- **Risk** (medium): A target misclassified as (c) would skip a real transaction on its warm hits (the comment at 30824-30828 notes that input service and intercept decisions must never be cached away). Classification has to be derived from the same predicates the runtime uses, and the condition words must be republished when the conditions change (mouse mode, external dispatch). Validate with the mouse/FileSelect, movie and Home menu routes.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.03-0.17 ms/frame. Same measured 1.8 % row as R-CALL-IMPL (0.16-0.30 ms/frame on the 6500U by est_ms = 0.018 * 8.9-16.9); assume this path is the other 30-70 % and 60-80 % of it removable -> 0.03-0.17 ms.

### 112. T-DEVICE-FAULTS: Device accesses the static scan cannot prove still fault through fastmem

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: memory access (static device classification). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--device-pcs FILE (with --interprocedural-device-pointers)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 8 (DVD, IOS, NAND, SC as host calls; 65,000 faults)
- **What:** Loads and stores whose effective address is MMIO but not provable at translation time go through the flat view. Each one takes a host page fault: the vectored handler decodes the instruction, performs the device access and resumes.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:2999-3055, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3112-3296, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:2930-2985
- **Why paid:** Device-pointer discovery covers only r13 SDA globals and local constant chains. Pointers held in absolute globals, struct fields or arguments (DI/IPC/SI register blocks passed around) stay unknown.
- **Frequency** (measured; measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 8 ('about 65,000 handled faults per run'); per-frame figure derived): About 65,000 handled faults per run, roughly 500 per second over the 124 s route, or about 8.7 per frame.
- **Design:** - Extend discover_device_pointer_globals to absolute (lis/addi) globals that are only ever stored device constants, and interprocedurally to arguments where every direct caller passes a device constant.
- --device-pcs FILE accepts the runtime's [fastmem-hot-address] fault pcs, generated locally and never shipped, and forces those sites onto the checked helper.
- **Risk** (low): A RAM site misclassified as a device is slower, never wrong: the checked helper also admits RAM.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.02-0.08 ms/frame. 3-10 microseconds per Windows vectored-exception round trip on the 6500U (assumed) x about 8.7 faults per frame = 0.026 to 0.087 ms per frame.

### 113. H-GX-SETTERS: GX state setters and GXBegin run as translated register-encode code (GX stage B)

- **Status:** open. Area: GX API as host code. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-gx-setters`. Already listed: True docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md section 3 stage B; docs/PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P10.5; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Not started: GX stage B'
- **What:** The GX library (198 GX* and 25 __GX* functions) is translated. Per draw, game and 2D-layout code calls GXSetVtxDesc (0x264 B), GXSetTexCoordGen2 (0x228 B), GXSetTevOrder (0x15C B), GXSetTevColorIn/AlphaIn/ColorOp/AlphaOp, GXLoadTexObj, GXSetChanCtrl, GXSetBlendMode, GXSetZMode and others. Each of these encodes BP/CP/XF register words into the __GXData shadow and sets dirty bits. GXBegin (0x804BA6B0, 0x14C B) then runs __GXSetDirtyState (0x278 B), which re-encodes them through fast::store into the gather pipe, and the render thread decodes them again. The retired hand-written GXBegin natives (native_gx_begin_804BA6B0, native_gx_send_zero_primitive_804BA7FC) used the exact model.
- **Where:** docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md:234-256, docs/sdk/RMGE01_SDK_FUNCTIONS.txt, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:15768-15840, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:11235-11386
- **Why paid:** Nebula translates the SDK and emulates the hardware below it. The FIFO byte stream is the only interface between the simulation and render threads, so every state change is encoded on one thread and decoded on the other.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native16 guest split (GX 2.5 %); docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md 'Not started: GX stage B'): Per draw and per state change; not counted. GX is 2.5 % of guest samples (measured, approximate attribution).
- **Design:** Step 1, cheapest: under translator flag --lean-gx-setters, give lean fast-float host bodies with contracts to the 10-15 hottest setters, chosen by rva_profile. Each writes the same __GXData fields and dirty bits as the translated body, and __GXSetDirtyState stays translated, so the byte stream is unchanged and diff_gx can validate it. Step 2 is the full stage B (host GxState, no FIFO encode or decode for direct-mode state), which is a render-thread change outside this area.
- **Risk** (medium): Each setter must reproduce __GXData's field layout and dirty-mask bits exactly, because __GXSetDirtyState and game code read them. One wrong bit gives wrong TEV, texgen or vertex formats on some materials, which only per-frame dumps reveal. diff_gx needs extending to compare the __GXData shadow after random setter sequences.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.15 ms/frame. GX = 2.5 % of guest x 62.5 % = 1.56 % of the simulation thread: 0.14-0.26 ms over T_sim 8.9-16.9 ms. I assume lean setters remove 35-60 % of the per-call cost (entry and exit of about 40 instructions, context traffic, checked or WGPIPE stores), giving 0.05-0.15 ms. Not additive with H-GXIMM.

### 114. T-ARG-REGS: Arguments and results travel through PpcContext memory (store-to-load forwarding on every call's critical path)

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: calls (host ABI). Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--register-arguments`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Even with perfect ABI trust, a call writes r3..rN to context and the callee reads them back at entry, and r3 comes back the same way. On Skylake each round trip costs ~4-5 cycles of store-forwarding latency on the dependency chain (argument computation to callee use, result to caller use). The synthetic call-dense body has this on each of its 2 calls (mr r3,r31 then bl; then cmpwi r3).
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:10215, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:935-941, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1881-1899
- **Why paid:** NativeGameFunction is `void(PpcContext*, GuestMemoryV1*, const NativeServicesV1*)`, shared by direct calls, the dispatch table, function records and runtime resumes, so all state passes through the context struct.
- **Frequency** (derived; code reading; latency from Skylake store-forwarding characteristics): Per call: 1-3 argument registers plus the r3 result.
- **Design:** After T-ABI-*, give each translated function a fast twin `std::uint32_t fn_X_r(ctx, mem, svc, uint32_t r3, uint32_t r4, uint32_t r5)` that returns r3 in eax. It is called from translated direct calls whose callee reads at most r3-r5 (from its GuestAbiContract) and is not on the opt-out list. The existing fn_X becomes a thunk that loads the arguments from context and stores the result, for the runtime, the dispatch table and interior resume. Keep floats in context for the first step.
- **Risk** (high): Two entry points per function doubles symbol and records work and can grow code if both bodies are kept (the thunk must call the twin, not duplicate it). Interior-entry resume must still enter through context. A callee's contract misread (r6 read through an inline wrapper) would read garbage.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.06-0.32 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G. Roughly 2 forwarded round trips x 4-5 cycles per call, partly overlapped by out-of-order execution. With calls about every 12 guest instructions (~100 x86 instructions, ~40-70 cycles), this is assumed to be 1-3 % of game-code time: 0.01 x 5.56 = 0.06; 0.03 x 10.56 = 0.32.

### 115. T-CR-LAZY: Unfused integer compares and record forms still build and store a CR field

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: CR/XER handling. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lazy-cr (with --assume-xer-so-clear)`. Already listed: False Adjacent-only fusion is shipped (NATIVE_EXECUTION gen21/gen23). The generalisation is not listed.
- **What:** An integer compare (cmp/cmpi/cmpl/cmpli) or record form whose CR field is not consumed by the very next bc still calls compare_signed/compare_unsigned/update_cr0. Each call builds the LT/GT/EQ mask branchlessly, loads XER for SO, and does a read-modify-write of context->cr; the branch then re-extracts the bit with cr_bit. CR logic (cror/crxor/crnor) adds two cr_bit reads and a set_cr_bit read-modify-write. Today's fusion (fusible_compare_branches/fusible_record_branches) applies only when the bc sits at pc+4, is not a branch target, and the field is dead on both successors within a 256-instruction walk. The older text pass cr0_fusion.rs is compiled but never called.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1626-1704, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1750-1788, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1400-1617, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5706-5750, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5450-5476, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5865-5900, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:11511-11558, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12526-12556, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12763-12808, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:90-131, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/lib.rs (cr0_fusion module comment: not yet called from the lowering loop)
- **Why paid:** CR and XER stay in PpcContext and are not resident registers (residency.rs treats compare_*/cr_bit as plain context accessors). Fusion is adjacency-based, so CodeWarrior's scheduled code does not fuse: a compare hoisted above an li/mr/lwz, one compare feeding two branches (blt then beq), or cror after a compare. All of these keep the full materialisation.
- **Frequency** (derived; measured, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md generation 21 'Compare fusion' (54,521 of 67,523 fused) and generation 23 'Record fusion' (3,681 of 4,752); instruction mix measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md): 13,002 of 67,523 static integer compares and 1,071 of 4,752 record forms are still unfused. Integer compares are 4.4% of hot guest instructions, so about 0.85% of hot guest instructions pay the full CR path if the static unfused share holds dynamically.
- **Design:** Lazy CR, behind --lazy-cr:
- At every integer compare, snapshot the operands into typed C++ locals (crl_F/crr_F, signed or unsigned).
- Every bc on field F whose unique reaching definition is that compare tests the locals. This applies along straight-line code or an extended block with no label, no interior entry and no other writer of F between the compare and the branch, so it also covers the second branch of a blt/beq pair.
- cror/crnor/creqv of two bits of the same snapshot field become one combined predicate (<=, >=, !=).
- The compare_* CR write is emitted only where cr_field_dead_from (or the untracked ppc_dataflow.rs liveness work in progress) says a non-branch reader may see F: mfcr, other CR logic, CR2-CR4 across calls, or an untraceable path.
- Companion --assume-xer-so-clear: every OE=1 form is already rejected (translate.rs:5141-5153), so SO can only be set by mtxer. When a scan finds no mtspr XER from a non-mfxer source, drop the XER load and OR in compare_*/update_cr0.
- **Risk** (medium): If a second writer of the field, or a join, between compare and branch is missed, the branch takes the wrong direction and game logic diverges. Snapshotting at the compare makes later writes to the operand registers harmless. The straight-line restriction bounds the analysis. Validate with diff_tier2/diff_sdkmath and route frame dumps.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.14 ms/frame. Measured with clang 18 at the module flags in scratchpad/micro/micro.cpp: 'cmpwi; li; beq' unfused costs 21 hot x86 instructions (24 static, 91 B); fused it costs 3. About 18 saved per site. Calculation: 0.85% x (0.6 to 0.9 convertible) x 18 = 0.09 to 0.14 x86 per guest instruction. Divided by 8 x86 per guest instruction this is 1.1 to 1.7% of game code. est_ms = share_of_game_code x 0.625 x 12.9 ms (= share x 8.06 ms) gives 0.09 to 0.14 ms. The low bound is 0.05 because these ALU ops retire faster than the average instruction.

### 116. H-HOSTCALL-DISPATCH: Every remaining host call walks a 162 KB linear interceptor chain

- **Status:** open. Area: host transition cost. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_HOST_CALL_TABLE=1`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** call_guest_impl_body (native_runtime.cpp:24129-26996) is a single function with 73 `guest_address == 0x...` tests and 38 trace-gate calls, executed in source order for every host call. Before it, call_guest_impl calls record_vi_post_rfi_handoff twice and normalize_executable_address, and runs an input-deadline check and an external-dispatch check. The header wrapper call_guest_resolved checks trace_scenario_opening_state_enabled() twice and trace_resource_helper_call/return. Inside the body, record_call writes a PC history ring and calls trace_guest_call. Only SelectThread and OSCreateThread leave early, through coop_call_intercept at the top. The rest walk the whole chain: OSSleepThread, OSWakeupThread, VIWaitForRetrace, KPADRead, DCZeroRange, DVDOpen, OSReport, the GXSetDrawSync pointer boundary, and everything in H-TRACEONLY and H-JUT-RANGE. On a 4 MB L3 shared with 7.7 MB of hot guest code, most of this code is probably cold in the instruction cache at each call.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:24129-26996, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27045-27205, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30759-30766, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:2994-3010, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:13347-13416
- **Why paid:** Each interceptor was added in place during bring-up as an `if (guest_address == X)` block (mouse, movie, JAudio host mixer, THP, KPAD, DVD and IOS traces, heap traces). There is no dispatch table, so the cost grows with every diagnostic added.
- **Frequency** (measured; 'Calls through the runtime 1.8 %' (measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md); compare counts derived by grep over native_runtime.cpp:24129-26996): Every host-routed call, legitimate ones included. The rate is not separately measured; the whole runtime-call bucket is 1.8 % of the simulation thread.
- **Design:** Runtime GALAXY_HOST_CALL_TABLE=1 (default 0, today's chain). At boot, build a small open-addressed table from guest address to handler id, generated from the same address list, plus a per-state 'any call diagnostic active' bit. call_guest_impl_body does one lookup. Addresses with no handler and no active diagnostic go straight to function(context, memory, services) after record_call; the others jump to a handler that holds the existing block unchanged. All trace-only blocks move into one [[unlikely]] cold function. The wrapper gates in call_guest_resolved fold into the same bit. A debug mode (GALAXY_HOST_CALL_TABLE_VERIFY=1) runs both paths and compares the dispatch decision.
- **Risk** (medium): Order matters. Several blocks mutate guest_address or resolved_function: the FileSelect pane replacement 0x803A29B8 -> 0x8034C184, the scene markers, OSSetCurrentContext preparation, and the mouse depth capture. A table that splits these incorrectly would skip an interceptor. Concrete failure modes: the mouse pointer losing its depth, or a missing scene marker breaking motion vectors. Mitigation: keep every block that reads LR or mutates state in the handler path for its address, and run the verify mode on the gameplay, Home menu and FileSelect routes.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.05-0.14 ms/frame. est_ms = share x T_sim. The measured bucket is 1.8 % x 12.9 ms = 0.23 ms (0.16-0.30 ms over T_sim 8.9-16.9 ms). I assume the chain and gates are 30-50 % of the per-call path, with the rest being the translated callee entry, residency sync and checkpoint. That gives 0.05-0.14 ms, not additive with the part of H-TRACEONLY and H-JUT-RANGE that removes calls outright.

### 117. T-FSEL: fsel/ps_sel lowered as integer NaN/sign tests with data-dependent branches

- **Status:** done (--fast-float-forms). Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--native-fsel`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** fsel emits f64_is_nan plus sign and zero tests on the 64-bit pattern: 3 movabs constants, about 13 instructions and 3-4 conditional branches in the probe (today_fsel), against 3 branchless instructions (vxorpd, vcmplesd, vblendvpd). ps_sel does this twice and adds a HID2 test. fsel is the compiler's branchless clamp/min/max, so its selector is often unpredictable and the translated branches mispredict.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4651-4664, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4983-4996, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12586-12589
- **Why paid:** The exact lowering was written on bit patterns so that NaN and -0 handling is explicit. It was never given a native form.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Measured category in CPU_GAP_REPORT; the fsel share is a hypothesis): Hypothesis 0.1-0.5 % of hot guest instructions (part of 'paired single, other float' 3.7 %).
- **Design:** Emit `fpr[D] = (std::bit_cast<double>(A) >= 0.0) ? C : B` (per lane for ps_sel; a native_float/fast_float branch in translate.rs). IEEE `>=` is false for NaN (so it selects frB) and true for -0.0 (so it selects frC), exactly the fsel rule. clang emits vcmpsd + vblendvpd.
- **Risk** (low): Bit-identical except under the fast-float DAZ MXCSR, where a negative subnormal selector compares as -0 and selects frC instead of frB. Selector subnormals are vanishingly rare.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.0056-0.085 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). About 10 x86 saved x 0.1-0.5 % = 0.01-0.05 x86 per guest instruction, plus removed mispredicts (about 15 cycles each) on unpredictable selectors; f = 0.1-0.8 %. Low = 0.625*0.001*8.9 = 0.0056; high = 0.625*0.008*16.9 = 0.085 ms.

### 118. T-PC-PROTOCOL: Every direct call stores context->pc and every callee loads and compares it

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: function entry, interior entries. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--entry-pc-argument`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT 'Function entry and exit ~40: ... base and interior-entry checks' (cost only, no design)
- **What:** emit_resolved_generated_direct_call writes `context->pc = target;` before every rmge01::fn_* call, and call_guest_indirect stores it before the table call. Every function with any interior entry (every non-leaf, because each call return is an interior entry) begins with `if (context->pc != address) [[unlikely]] switch(...)`. Compiled, that is a memory store per call plus a load, compare and branch at the callee's entry, a dependency through memory on the call's critical path.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7106-7124, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3557-3582, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1819-1849, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:10206-10265
- **Why paid:** One C++ function per guest function serves both the normal entry and every resume alias (backward-branch targets, call-return continuations, mtmsr/mtdec resumes, OSSaveContext continuations, address-taken interior pointers). The runtime resumes a frame by setting pc and calling the owner.
- **Frequency** (measured; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md F6 (interior entries 287,930 to 178,776); compiled synthetic body): Once per call (store) and once per entry (load/compare): 178,776 interior entries exist module-wide, so almost every non-leaf function pays the entry test.
- **Design:** Pass the entry pc as a fourth register argument (`fn_X(ctx, mem, svc, std::uint32_t entry_pc)`). Direct calls pass the immediate, and clang folds the compare away when it can see the callee, otherwise it is one register compare. Runtime resume and the dispatch table pass context->pc. This removes the per-call memory store and the entry load. With a 4th argument, a module-ABI bump (27 to 28) is needed; keep writing context->pc only in call_guest_resolved/cached boundaries and trace hooks that read it.
- **Risk** (medium): Runtime code that reads context->pc after a translated call for attribution (fault handler, guest_execution_fault, profiler 'last call target') would see a stale pc. Audit every context->pc reader in native_runtime.cpp. This is a module ABI change: an old runtime refuses the new module unless it is versioned.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.02-0.16 ms/frame. est_ms = f x G. 3-4 cheap instructions per call (store immediate, load and compare, fused branch) out of ~150 hot instructions per 2 calls in the call-dense synthetic body is 2-2.5 % of instructions. These retire nearly free, so f = 0.3-1.5 %: 0.003 x 5.56 = 0.02; 0.015 x 10.56 = 0.16.

### 119. R-DIAGGATES: Diagnostic gates and counters on the per-command and per-draw paths

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime render thread: FIFO parse / per-draw overhead. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_DIAG_MASK (always-on refactor; flag only selects compiled-in traces)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT plan item 14 lists 'diagnostic records and gates compiled into release (F18)' for the simulation thread; the render-thread gates are not listed
- **What:** Per top-level FIFO command: the fifo_history_enabled() and should_trace_fifo_offset() static-guard checks, plus profile pointer branches. Per draw: the static abort_after_draws check, capture_texaddr_target(), trace_thp_draw_frame_enabled() (two function-local statics), the ScopedFlushTiming modulo-64 counter, a dozen always-on frame_* counters, and the capture_draws_/capture_composites_ branches carrying large fprintf blocks that bloat on_draw's code.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:1895-1945, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:52-66, runtime/src/gx/fifo_parser.cpp:143-146, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:11785-11800, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:2338-2376, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:2397-2436, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:10840-10850, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:11990-12100
- **Why paid:** Diagnostics compiled into release. Each gate is cheap, but they sit on paths taken tens of thousands of times per frame and push hot code apart in a small i-cache.
- **Frequency** (derived; Code locations above; the counts follow from the per-command and per-draw call sites): Per FIFO command and per draw / cached draw run (tens of thousands per frame, gx_backend.cpp:752 comment).
- **Design:** Fold every diagnostic env gate into one per-chunk 'diag mask' computed at render_frame_on_thread entry (a single register-resident bitmask tested once per draw), and move capture, trace and THP logging into [[gnu::cold]]/noinline helpers so on_draw and run_window shrink. Optionally compile the trace blocks out under GALAXY_RELEASE_NO_TRACE.
- **Risk** (low): Diagnostics that read env per frame (poll_live_capture_request) must still flip the mask at chunk start. Otherwise no behaviour change.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.08 ms/frame. ~2-5 ns per gate x 3-6 gates x 10-20 K commands/draws per frame = 0.01-0.08 ms, plus an unquantified i-cache benefit.

### 120. R-RENDER-1MS-POLL: Render thread polls GPU fences on a 1 ms condition-variable timeout instead of waiting on fence events

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: worker wakes / render. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_RENDER_FENCE_EVENT_WAIT (default 0)`. Already listed: False Render 'waiting, condition-variable spin ~10 %' is measured in CPU_GAP_REPORT, but its cause and a design are not listed
- **What:** When idle_present (frame-rate decouple), pending frame effects, a pointer-depth capture or an async peek field is outstanding, the render loop parks with queue_cv_.wait_for(1 ms) and re-polls fences at the loop top. That is up to 1,000 timed wakes/s, each with a lock, get_render_config(), three poll functions, and a timer interrupt that keeps the package out of deep C-states.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7154-7196, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:7792-7809
- **Why paid:** Fence completion arrives on no CPU-visible event the loop waits on, so a short bounded park was the simple way to observe completions promptly.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Code path; the proportion of time a condition holds is unmeasured): Up to 1,000 wakes/s while any of the four conditions holds. Pointer-depth fields arrive at about 40/s, each pending until its GPU fence completes.
- **Design:** Use ID3D12Fence::SetEventOnCompletion(next_needed_value, fence_event). Have the render thread wait with WaitForMultipleObjects on {queue_event, fence_event} (or the frame-pacing waitable), replacing the condition variable's timed wait for these cases. The queue condition variable keeps its untimed path when nothing is pending.
- **Risk** (low): A missed signal would stall PE/peek delivery until the next queue push. Keep a 4 ms safety timeout.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.08 ms/frame. Up to 1,000 wakes/s * 2-5 us (timed SleepConditionVariableSRW wake under KVA shadow, lock, polls) = 2-5 ms/s = 0.03-0.08 ms/frame of render-thread CPU while active. The low bound assumes the conditions hold about a third of the time. The measured render 'waiting, condvar spin ~10 %' (CPU_GAP_REPORT) is 0.6-1.0 ms on the 6500U (0.10 * 6-10 ms) and includes this.

### 121. H-DCRANGE: DCFlushRange/DCStoreRange run a translated per-32-byte loop that publishes each line and ends in an `sc` service call

- **Status:** open. Area: OS cache natives (DCFlushRange/DCStoreRange/DCInvalidateRange). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-dc-range`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** DCFlushRange 0x804A2F20 and DCStoreRange 0x804A2F50 (0x30 B each) loop over the range one 32-byte line at a time. Each dcbf or dcbst lowers to fast::dcache_flush_line, which calls guest_notify_write on 32 bytes: a single-page dirty bit with an atomic load, a conditional atomic OR and a summary publish, plus the CPU-tracker window test. Each iteration also pays a budgeted backward-branch checkpoint decrement. The function ends with `sc`, which becomes galaxy::dispatch_system_call, then services->system_call in the runtime, which returns at once for PCs 0x804A2F48/0x804A2F78. That `sc` is a Full residency sync point followed by the transfer test. DCInvalidateRange (0x804A2EF4) loops over dcbi, which lowers to nothing, but still iterates. The old native_cache_maintenance_range body is retired (restartability list) and predates flush-based tracking: it publishes nothing.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4626-4636, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:247-255, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:5578-5630, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:6137-6209, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:31035-31055, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:15768-15840
- **Why paid:** F4 flush-based coherency: translated stores do not publish, so dcbf/dcbst are the publication points for GPU-visible data (display lists, vertex arrays, textures). The SDK's `sc` is the RVL write-buffer flush.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; code; no census of flushed bytes exists): Per DCFlushRange/DCStoreRange call. Bytes flushed per frame are unknown; my assumption is 20-200 KB, or 640-6,400 lines.
- **Design:** Translator flag --lean-dc-range. lean_dc_flush_range (DCFlushRange, DCStoreRange and the NoSync variants) computes the line-aligned span and calls guest_notify_write once per tracker page (a range mark), with no loop and no system-call service; contract (&[3,4], &[], &[], &[]). DCInvalidateRange becomes an empty leaf with the same contract. Keep the zero-length CR behaviour (cmplwi r4,0 sets CR0, which is dead after the call by the ABI).
- **Risk** (low): The same pages end up dirty, with one publication per page instead of one per line. The render thread's dependency validation sees identical dirty sets at the frame boundary. A range crossing out of RAM (locked cache, MEM2 end) must keep today's per-line behaviour; DCZeroRange's bounded-range policy at native_runtime.cpp:26380 shows the edge cases.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.08 ms/frame. Assumes 640-6,400 lines per frame x 25-40 x86 instructions per iteration (loop, notify fast path with atomics, budget decrement), about 5-15 ns each on the 6500U: 0.003-0.1 ms. Add per call the `sc` service call, full sync and transfer test, about 50-100 ns, times 50-300 calls per frame (assumed), for 0.0025-0.03 ms.

### 122. H-IOS: DVD/IOS/NAND/SC through emulated DI and IPC registers (handled faults and checked device sites)

- **Status:** open. Area: DVD/IOS/NAND/SC natives. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--host-ios / GALAXY_HOST_IOS=1`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 8; docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md section 5
- **What:** DVD, ISFS, NAND and SC requests run the translated SDK down to IPCWriteReg (0x804D1F10) and IOS_* calls, which write the emulated IPC registers. Pointer-based register accesses (__IPCRegs[i]) either take checked device helpers or fault into the vectored handler, at 1.0 us per fault measured on the desktop. Only DVDOpen, DVDConvertPathToEntrynum (traces) and the /dev/di read worker are host-side.
- **Where:** docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md:348-387, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:114, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:494, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:25420-25455
- **Why paid:** The SDK is translated and the hardware below it is emulated. Disc reads were moved to a worker, but the IPC protocol itself is emulated.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md plan item 8 ('about 65,000 handled faults per run; device reads and writes about 0.5 %'); fault cost 1.0 us (measured, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native17)): About 65,000 handled faults per 124 s run, roughly 524 per second or 8.7 per frame. Device reads and writes are about 0.5 % of the simulation thread.
- **Design:** Translator --host-ios adds DVDReadPrio, DVDReadAsyncPrio, DVDGetCommandBlockStatus, IOS_Open/Close/Ioctl/Ioctlv/Read/Write and the ISFS/NAND entry points to the host-call list. Runtime GALAXY_HOST_IOS=1 handles one host function per device path (/dev/di, /dev/fs, /dev/es, /dev/stm/*, /dev/usb/oh1, with /dev/net/* answering 'not available') and completes asynchronous requests through deferred callbacks. Bluetooth IPC disappears together with H-WPAD.
- **Risk** (medium): Save, load and NAND paths: a wrong ISFS return code corrupts or loses the save, and NWC24 threads must not start. Validate boot, file select, save and load, a full stage load and the THP intro, as the SDK plan's step 5 lists.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.06-0.12 ms/frame. Device accesses: 0.005 x T_sim = 0.045-0.085 ms (T_sim 8.9-16.9 ms). Faults: 8.7 per frame x 1.0 us x 2.81 (PassMark single-thread ratio 4,739/1,686) = 0.024 ms, scaled to 0.017-0.032 ms over the same T_sim range. Total 0.06-0.12 ms. Partly overlaps H-WPAD (the Bluetooth IPC traffic).

### 123. R-DCFLUSH-RANGE: DCFlushRange/DCStoreRange run as translated dcbf loops: per-line dirty publication plus a loop checkpoint per iteration

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: dirty tracking (producer side). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--host-cache-range-ops`. Already listed: True docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md cache table (DCFlushRange etc. as host no-ops; publication not discussed); native_api.h:14661 comment
- **What:** Each dcbf/dcbst in the translated DCFlushRange 804A2F20, DCStoreRange 804A2F50 and the NoSync variants 804A2F80/804A2FAC calls dcache_flush_line, which is guest_notify_write(addr & ~31, 32). That is a single-page admission (~15 compares), a load/test/lock-or of the page word, the summary publication and the CPU-tracker window test. The loop's back edge also takes a branch checkpoint every iteration. The runtime already had to add a repeat-PC throttle because these loops 'dominate 90 %+ of all full-callback escalations'.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:247-255, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:5578-5646, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14661-14687, docs/sdk/RMGE01_SDK_FUNCTIONS.txt:220-225
- **Why paid:** Translated stores do not publish (ABI 25). The cache-maintenance instruction is the publication point, and the loop is translated generically, like any other guest loop.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; Code path (native_fast_paths.h:251-255) and the checkpoint escalation note (native_api.h:14661-14676); the per-frame flush volume is unmeasured): Once per 32-byte line flushed by the game: J3D matrix and vertex buffers, display-list builds, texture uploads. Bytes per frame not measured.
- **Design:** Translator lowers bl to DCFlushRange/DCStoreRange (and the NoSync forms) to an inline host helper guest_publish_range(memory, addr, len). The helper rounds to 32-byte lines and fills bitmap words with masks, one word per 2 KB, with one summary publication per word. It issues no checkpoint, because the length is bounded by the call. DCInvalidateRange 804A2EF4 lowers to nothing, since dcbi already does. Add a [cache-range] counter to measure flushed bytes per frame first.
- **Risk** (medium): Must match the guest's line rounding (start & ~31, end rounded up) exactly. Otherwise a partial-line tail misses publication, and the renderer sees stale vertex or matrix data until the next flush. Dropping the per-iteration checkpoint delays event delivery inside a multi-megabyte flush by its host duration, which is microseconds.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.15 ms/frame. Assumed 64-512 KB flushed per frame = 2k-16k lines x ~40-50 x86 instructions per line (notify ~20, loop body and checkpoint test ~20-30) = 80k-800k instructions at ~2.5 IPC x 3 GHz = 0.01-0.11 ms, plus throttled checkpoint escalations. Wide because the flush volume is unmeasured.

### 124. R-INPUT-REPORT-SYSCALLS: Per-report Win32 focus, XInput and pointer queries while building the virtual HID report

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: input. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_INPUT_FOCUS_FROM_MESSAGES (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Each 100 Hz virtual Wii Remote report runs build_native_hid_input_snapshot on the simulation thread. It calls foreground_process_window (GetForegroundWindow syscall plus IsWindow, IsWindowVisible and GetWindowThreadProcessId) and XInputGetState on the connected slot (a DeviceIoControl to xusb). It also takes the switch-pad SRWLock, the host pointer provider (std::mutex plus steady_clock in runtime_host_pointer_state), GetTickCount64, the script overlay, and strcmp chains on pointer_source.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_host.cpp:8392-8531, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:6849-6857, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:6101-6106, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30692-30720, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:31585-31597
- **Why paid:** Reports are built at their deadline from live host state for input causality. Focus is re-queried every report so keys are never delivered to an unfocused window.
- **Frequency** (derived; runtime/include/galaxy/native_input.h:22-27): 100 reports/s (kNativeHidReportPeriodTicks = 60.75 MHz / 100)
- **Design:** Keep focus in an atomic maintained by the window thread from WM_ACTIVATEAPP, WM_SETFOCUS and WM_KILLFOCUS. The renderer already tracks s_pointer_focused (renderer_d3d12.cpp:2030-2037), so the per-report GetForegroundWindow and IsWindow* calls go away. Replace the pointer-cache std::mutex with a seqlock. Turn the pointer_source strings into an enum. XInputGetState on the connected slot stays (the value must be fresh).
- **Risk** (low): Missed focus messages (for example after a UAC prompt or a fullscreen mode switch) would leave keys enabled while unfocused. Fall back to GetForegroundWindow once per second as a check.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.013-0.067 ms/frame. Assumed 8-40 us per report on the 6500U: one syscall of about 1-2 us under KVA shadow, an XInput IOCTL of about 5-30 us, locks and clock reads of about 1 us. Times 100/s gives 0.8-4 ms/s = 0.013-0.067 ms/frame. Not profiled. The older laptop profile had GetAsyncKeyState at 2.3 % before keys moved to window messages (PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:79).

### 125. R-BROKER-WAKES: Deadline broker: mutex and SetEvent per re-arm, plus a separate worker wake, timer re-arm and vector allocation per deadline

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: timing broker. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_BROKER_LAZY_REARM (default 0)`. Already listed: False NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native12 mentions 'deadline-broker wakes on every DEC write' as a runtime item, but proposes no design
- **What:** Every DEC write and AI deadline change calls DeadlineBroker::replace_once on the simulation thread. That locks a std::mutex shared with the worker (the worker publishes while holding it), clears the latch slot, and calls SetEvent when the new deadline is earlier than the worker's wait. Each worker wake does SetWaitableTimer and WaitForMultipleObjects (two syscalls) and allocates a std::vector from collect_due. For each event it makes two extra timeline_.now_ticks() reads, each a CAS on the shared last_ticks_ line that the simulation thread also writes. The worker is MMCSS Games High, so its wakes preempt the render thread or a sibling hyperthread.
- **Where:** runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1711-1781, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1951-2041, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:264-331, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:142-256, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5827-5858, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:22481-22499
- **Why paid:** An independent producer thread publishes VI/AI/input/DEC edges so device time advances while the guest runs or sleeps. Publication happens under the mutex so replace_once/cancel_once linearize against in-flight events. The lateness statistics (pre/post publication clock reads) were added for diagnosis.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md rates line; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:419 (broker replacements 3,800/s, wakes 1,630/s, SetEvent 1.34 us)): 420 worker wakes/s (Native22); about 3,800 schedule replacements/s (Native16, current rate unknown)
- **Design:** (1) Lazy re-arm: while the simulation thread is running (not idle- or VI-waiting), replace_once only updates the schedule and sets a 'rearm-pending' flag, with no SetEvent. Checkpoint bodies already sample the clock to decide 'DEC not yet due' (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:418). Before blocking, the simulation thread does one notify. (2) In the worker, collect into a fixed std::array<DeadlineEvent, kEventKindCount*N> instead of a vector. Take the pre/post publication clock reads only when the lateness diagnostics are enabled. Use a thread-local monotonic clamp for broker reads.
- **Risk** (medium): With lazy re-arm, a DEC due while the guest runs would be noticed only at the next checkpoint body (about every 106 us at 9,400 bodies/s) instead of the next inline mask test. A guest loop without checkpoint bodies could delay DEC delivery, which is the class of hang seen when backward-branch checkpoints were dropped (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:453-457).
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.05-0.11 ms/frame. Simulation-thread side: 3,800 replacements/s * 50-150 ns (mutex and latch clear) = 0.19-0.57 ms/s. Plus 30-60 % of the 420 wakes initiated by SetEvent from the simulation thread at 1.34 us * 2.8 = 3.8 us each = 0.48-0.96 ms/s. Subtotal 0.011-0.025 ms/frame. Broker thread: 420 wakes * 5-12 us (2 syscalls, a context switch, a vector alloc, 3 CAS clock reads per event) = 2.1-5 ms/s = 0.035-0.084 ms/frame, on the core shared with render. Total 0.05-0.11 ms/frame.

### 126. T-HOST-RANGE-8041: All direct calls into 0x8041xxxx (413 functions) and the host list go through call_guest_resolved and the runtime

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator + runtime: host call boundary. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--narrow-host-range (translator); GALAXY_DIRECT_DISPATCH_8041=0 default (runtime)`. Already listed: False PERFORMANCE_PLAN P10.4 covers only the withheld indirect entries
- **What:** requires_host_guest_call returns true for every target with (target>>16)==0x8041 (413 functions in metadata/rmge01/functions.csv) and for ~45 listed addresses. Every direct call to them emits an inline call_guest_resolved (null tests, trace gates, two resource-trace calls) and enters the runtime's call_guest_impl. The runtime also withholds the whole range from the direct dispatch table. Its actual blanket 0x8041 intercept applies only while dispatching_vi_retrace (JUT VI callbacks); the rest is trace-only.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7633-7703, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7053-7070, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:13339-13410, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:15817-15864, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:16016-16018, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30751-30766
- **Why paid:** Historical intercepts and traces in the JUT/resource layer (resource-helper traces at 0x80414104..., the VI retrace callbacks at 0x80419554/0x80419308), and the requirement that a cached call never bypasses a host transaction.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT runtime items (calls through the runtime 1.8 %); range size from metadata/rmge01/functions.csv): Per call to those targets. 'Calls through the runtime' are 1.8 % of the simulation thread in total; the share due to the 0x8041 blanket is unknown.
- **Design:** Translator: narrow the 0x8041 rule to the addresses the runtime actually intercepts unconditionally. For the VI-retrace-only case, emit `if (services->runtime_flags & kViRetraceDispatchBit) [[unlikely]] call_guest_resolved(...) else direct call;` (or the module-global snapshot). Make the inline call_guest_resolved trace gates out of line (one copy, as was done for call_guest_cached). Runtime: publish direct dispatch entries for the range outside VI retrace dispatch, re-using the existing withdraw mechanism. Print '[direct-call-host] target count' per run to rank the rest of the list.
- **Risk** (medium): A JUT-layer function that genuinely needs a host transaction outside VI retrace (resource loading validation, movie markers) would run without it. Build the narrowed list from the runtime's needs_call_guest_intercept and cached_call_has_required_entry_hook, not by hand. Gameplay/Home/boot/save routes plus flag-return counts validate.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0-0.15 ms/frame. est_ms = share x sim_ms. All runtime calls are measured at 1.8 % x 12.9 = 0.23 ms (0.16-0.30 over 8.9-16.9 ms). The fraction caused by the over-broad range is unknown, taken as 0-50 % of the high end: 0 to 0.5 x 0.30 = 0.15 ms.

### 127. R-FIFO-RING-MIRROR: Every gather burst is also copied into the MEM1 CPU FIFO ring and published as dirty, although nothing reads the ring in live mode

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: gather pipe / dirty tracking. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_WGPIPE_RING_MIRROR (default 1 = today)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** drain_wgpipe_gather_bursts and flush_wgpipe_gather_burst memcpy every complete burst into the guest CPU FIFO ring in MEM1 and call notify_guest_write for it, which marks renderer and CPU dirty pages and summary bits. They also append the same bytes to gx_fifo_, which is what the render thread actually parses. In live-FIFO mode only the write pointer and the PI_CP_WRITE register value are guest-observable. The ring bytes are never read by the renderer, and their dirty ranges then flow through every drain and into render-side texture-cache invalidation.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_host.cpp:11853-11883, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:11911-11945, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:32106-32119, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:5361-5412
- **Why paid:** Hardware fidelity: on the Wii the GP reads the ring from RAM. The dirty publication was added because a gather pipe redirected to a display-list buffer writes RAM the GP later reads (comment at native_host.cpp:11869-11871). That case is the non-live path, but the live path got the same treatment.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:167 (61-126 KB, 2,000-4,000 bursts per frame); :185 (4 KB buffer)): 61-126 KB per frame copied and published (2,000-4,000 bursts); about 15-31 host flushes per frame at 4 KB per flush.
- **Design:** When wgpipe_feeds_live_fifo() and cpu_fifo_is_live(), advance cpu_fifo_wr_ptr_ and the wrap flag arithmetically and write PI_CP_WRITE, but skip the memcpy and notify_guest_write. Keep both for the memory-backed FIFO (GXBeginDisplayList recording), where the bytes are real guest data.
- **Risk** (low): If any guest code reads the CPU FIFO ring contents (GXSaveCPUFifo plus replay, debugger dumps, GXRedirectWriteGatherPipe users that read the ring back) it sees stale bytes. Expected none in RMGE01; verify with a one-run guard that write-protects the ring range and logs reads.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.015-0.06 ms/frame. memcpy of 61-126 KB into a cold ring with read-for-ownership (assumed 5-10 GB/s on the 6500U) = 6-25 us. 2,000-4,000 extra 32-byte dirty bits processed bit by bit in drain_guest_memory_writes at ~3-5 ns each = 6-20 us. Plus L3 pollution of ~0.1 MB/frame on a 4 MB L3 (hypothesis, not counted). The copy part sits inside the measured 'gather pipe 0.55 %' (0.05-0.09 ms); the bit part inside 'guest write drain 1.0 %'.

### 128. T-CACHE-RANGE: SDK cache-range loops run one translated iteration per 32-byte line

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: memory access (cache operations). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-cache-ranges`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** DCFlushRange, DCStoreRange, DCInvalidateRange and ICInvalidateRange run as translated loops. Each 32-byte line costs a dcbf/dcbst (inline dirty-page marking through guest_notify_write, about 15-20 x86 instructions) or a dcbi (nothing), plus the loop's budgeted backward-branch checkpoint. These loops once dominated checkpoint escalations.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4431-4443, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:244-254, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:5561-5600, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:14661-14676
- **Why paid:** The SDK cache routines are translated literally; no lean body exists for them.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; assumed; the escalation history is in the native_api.h comment): Once per 32-byte line flushed or stored. Assumed 2,000 to 10,000 lines per frame (matrix, display-list and vertex buffers).
- **Design:** Add lean host bodies (native_function_body plus lean_call_contract: reads r3/r4, writes nothing) for the RVL SDK DCFlushRange(NoSync), DCStoreRange(NoSync), DCInvalidateRange and ICInvalidateRange. Each does one guest_notify_write over the 32-byte-rounded range, or nothing for invalidate, plus the sync. Call sites then skip the full sync and the checkpoint.
- **Risk** (low): The bodies must publish exactly the same lines (start rounded down, end rounded up to 32) and must not deliver events.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.06 ms/frame. 2,000 to 10,000 lines x about 25 x86 instructions = 50K to 250K x86 instructions per frame, about 0.01 to 0.06 ms at 1.5 IPC and 3 GHz.

### 129. G-TIMESTAMPS: GPU timestamp queries shipped on: 2 queries, a resolve and a readback Map per chunk

- **Status:** open. Area: Presentation / diagnostics. Thread: render. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GPU_TIMESTAMPS=0 in installer/runtime-env.json (existing flag)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** installer/runtime-env.json sets GALAXY_GPU_TIMESTAMPS=1. Every chunk then records EndQuery at begin_frame, plus EndQuery and ResolveQueryData at end_frame, and the next use of the slot Maps and Unmaps the readback to accumulate telemetry_gpu_ms_. That value is printed only when GALAXY_TRACE_PRESENT_STATS is set, and goes to the timeline only when it is enabled. On Intel a timestamp is a PIPE_CONTROL with post-sync write, which waits for earlier work at that point.
- **Where:** installer/runtime-env.json:87-90, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:1419-1432, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:4454-4480, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:5664-5746, runtime/src/gx/renderer_d3d12.cpp:6290-6308
- **Why paid:** Release builds report GPU time in telemetry and recordings.
- **Frequency** (derived; Chunk rate as in G-CHUNK-SUBMIT; timestamps on every begin_frame/end_frame.): About 3.25 times per frame (per chunk).
- **Design:** Default GALAXY_GPU_TIMESTAMPS to 0 in runtime-env.json and enable it with recordings (Detailed). Or sample one chunk in 30, recording queries only when frame_index_ % 30 == 0.
- **Risk** (low): Loses the always-on gpu-ms average in user logs, unless sampled.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.06 ms/frame. CPU: 3.25 x (3 query calls x 1-2 us + Map/Unmap 2-5 us) = 0.02-0.05 ms. GPU: 3.25 x pipeline-flush bubbles at command-list edges that mostly exist anyway, 0-0.01 ms.

### 130. R-LATCH-LOCK-97: Pending-event latch: per-slot spinlock and shared cache lines between broker and simulation thread

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: timing latch. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_LATCH_PACKED (default 0)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md runtime items (event latch lock 0.53 %)
- **What:** Every publish and take on PendingEventLatch acquires slot_locks_[slot] (atomic_flag test_and_set), updates counts_ and mask_, and releases it. counts_, replaceable_generations_, the slot locks, the diagnostic spin counters, mask_ and idle_waiting_ sit in adjacent unpadded arrays. The broker's writes invalidate the lines the simulation thread polls (mask_) and writes (idle_waiting_).
- **Where:** runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:881-974, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1003-1034, runtime/include/galaxy/runtime_timeline.h:451-475
- **Why paid:** Payload, identity (periodic sequence and deadline) and the mask bit must change atomically together so a consumer never drains a count without its identity, or leaves a stale bit.
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md: event latch lock 0.53 % of the simulation thread): Every latch publish/take: about 420 publications/s plus the simulation thread's takes at VI, AI, input and DEC service, and mask() reads at checkpoint gates
- **Design:** Pack each kind's {count:16, sequence-low:48} into one 64-bit atomic and publish with one CAS. Keep the deadline in a seqlock-protected side slot written only by the single producer. Put mask_ and idle_waiting_ on their own alignas(64) lines and move the contention diagnostics to a separate cold struct. The simulation thread's take becomes a CAS instead of lock, three ops and unlock.
- **Risk** (medium): The publication protocol is subtle: prefix take, replaceable generations, and Backlog detection. A packed CAS must keep the 'never drain without identity' invariant. The existing runtime_timeline_tests are the gate.
- **Estimate** (measured): 0.047-0.09 ms/frame. 0.53 % of the simulation thread measured (CPU_GAP_REPORT). est_ms = share * thread_ms_6500U: 0.0053 * 8.9 = 0.047, * 12.9 = 0.068, * 16.9 = 0.090 ms.

### 131. R-GUEST-TB: Game mftb/mfdec reads cross into the host through services->time_base_ticks

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: clock / generated-code host helpers. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `--inline-time-base (translator); GALAXY_MODULE_TIME_BASE_VIEW (runtime, default 0 = view left invalid so modules keep the host call)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Every translated mftb/mftbu/mfdec (read_spr -> read_time_base / read_decrementer / raw_time_base_ticks) calls services->time_base_ticks (an indirect, CFG-checked call out of the module) -> host_time_base_ticks (second census atomic) -> runtime_time_base_ticks with all of R-CLOCK's per-read work. OSGetTime/OSGetTick are used by the game's frame timing, alarms and profilers.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12810-12860, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30388-30395, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5465-5532, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5091-5098
- **Why paid:** The guest time base is defined by the runtime's timeline (epoch, discarded host-suspension ticks, per-context time_base_offset), so the module asks the runtime for every read; no module-side view of the timeline exists.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md (9,600 of the 56,000 clock reads/s are by the game)): 9,600/s = 160 per frame
- **Design:** Publish a read-only TimeBaseViewV1 snapshot the module can read like g_module_checkpoint_gate (optional export, refreshed by the runtime at start and at every host-suspension rebase): tsc_epoch, scale_fixed64, epoch_ticks, discarded_ticks, plus a 'valid' word. Under a new translator flag the generated mftb/mfdec helpers compute ticks inline (rdtsc, _umul128 high half, add, subtract) and fall back to services->time_base_ticks when the view is invalid (QPC source, rebase in progress). DEC writes (write_decrementer_and_notify) keep the host call because they re-arm the broker.
- **Risk** (medium): A rebase after host suspension changes discarded_ticks; a module reading a stale view would see time jump forward then back. Refresh happens inside a host call on the simulation thread (the rebase runs there), so translated code never sees a half-written view, the same argument as --checkpoint-globals. Reads lose their per-read host-suspension sampling (R-CLOCK step 3 moves it anyway). Monotonicity across guest reads must be kept: clamp against a module-local last value.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.04-0.09 ms/frame. Game share of the 3.3 % clock row = 3.3 % * 9,600/56,000 = 0.57 %; est_ms = 0.0057 * 8.9-16.9 = 0.05-0.10 ms/frame. Inline read is ~10-15 x86 instructions against ~80 ns desktop per host read, so 80-95 % removable -> 0.04-0.09 ms.

### 132. R-BROKER-WAKE: Decrementer and AI deadline replacements take the broker mutex and wake its thread from the simulation thread

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: deadline broker. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_BROKER_WAKE_SLACK_US (default 0 = wake on every earlier deadline)`. Already listed: True docs/PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md P9 'Deadline broker wake per DEC replace'
- **What:** Every guest mtdec (PPCMtdec, alarm re-arm) and AI-DMA deadline change calls DeadlineBroker::replace_once from the simulation thread: schedule_changes_ atomic, std::mutex lock (contended while the broker publishes with SetEvent under the same mutex, see R-LATCH-LOCK), schedule_.replace_once, latch_.clear_replaceable (slot lock), and SetEvent when the new deadline is earlier than the broker's current wait target.
- **Where:** runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1711-1716, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1762-1781, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1951-2041, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12862-12890
- **Why paid:** The broker thread is the clock that publishes DEC/AI edges into the latch; an earlier deadline must shorten its current wait, which needs a wake.
- **Frequency** (measured; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md rates (420 broker wakes/s); docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native15/16 (SetEvent 1.34 us)): 420 broker wakes/s
- **Design:** Skip the wake when the new deadline is within the broker's wake-up slack of its current target (P9: 100 us) and when the simulation thread will poll the DEC deadline itself before then (synchronize_decrementer_deadline already settles a due DEC at every body; the idle wait can fold the DEC deadline into its own timer target). Batch DEC rewrites made within one checkpoint interval into one replace.
- **Risk** (low): A skipped wake lets a DEC edge be published up to the slack late; the simulation-thread fallback (synchronize_decrementer_deadline) bounds it by the body interval, which grows if R-CKPT-BODY lands, so the slack must be chosen together with that change. DEC lateness counters (decrementer_deadline_max_lateness_ticks) are the check.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.01-0.05 ms/frame. 420/s * 1.34 us (desktop SetEvent) = 0.56 ms/s = 0.009 ms/frame on the desktop; on the 6500U a kernel transition with KVA shadowing is assumed 2-4 us -> 0.84-1.7 ms/s = 0.014-0.028 ms/frame, plus mutex contention (hypothesis) -> 0.01-0.05 ms.

### 133. G-LOOKUP-CACHE-SIZE: Runtime function lookup cache is 384 KB of 24-byte padded entries, larger than Skylake's 256 KB L2

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: runtime data footprint. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `none (internal layout), or GALAXY_LOOKUP_CACHE_PACKED for A/B`. Already listed: False PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:575 lists lookup_function per flat hop, not the cache size
- **What:** RuntimeState::lookup_cache holds 16,384 LookupCacheEntry {u32 address; NativeGameFunction function; bool valid}, which pads to 24 B, for 384 KB. It serves call_guest_cached on the boundary path (withheld targets, pending events, interior entries). The hash index (word ^ word>>7 ^ word>>13) spreads entries over the whole array, so on a 256 KB-L2 Skylake almost every lookup is an L2 miss and usually a different 4 KB page.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:1569-1573, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:2193-2194, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:2861-2899
- **Why paid:** The entry layout was written for clarity, and the size was chosen on a desktop with a 1 MB L2.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:52 (calls through the runtime 1.8 %)): Each call through the runtime boundary that is not satisfied by the per-site static cache. The rate is unknown; 'calls through the runtime' is 1.8 % of simulation samples.
- **Design:** Pack the entries to 8 B {u32 address (0 = invalid), u32 rva}, 128 KB, or share the G-DISPATCH-DENSE hash so there is one structure. Keep the key compare.
- **Risk** (low): The valid flag folds into address 0. Guest address 0 is never a function, but that must be asserted.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.05 ms/frame. Formula: est_ms = share x 12.9 ms. I assume the lookup-cache misses are 5-20 % of the 1.8 % 'calls through the runtime' bucket: 0.0009-0.0036 x 12.9 = 0.012-0.046 ms.

### 134. R-DISPATCH-WITHDRAW: The direct-dispatch table is withdrawn whenever any event bit is pending

- **Status:** open. Area: Generated-code header: call_guest_indirect. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--dispatch-input-mask-only`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** call_guest_indirect calls the published native body only if atomic_load(pending_event_mask) == 0 and direct_dispatch_suspended == 0. Any latched VI, AI, DEC, DSP, IOS or transfer bit therefore sends every bctrl through the cached service boundary (R-CALL-CACHED) until the event is consumed, although that boundary only acts on InputReport (and refuses a pending transfer); other events are delivered at the next call-return or branch checkpoint either way.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:1881-1905, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30803-30814, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:27052-27061
- **Why paid:** F19 kept indirect calls at the audited boundary whenever a device event was pending, so an indirect call would observe the event at the same point it did before direct dispatch existed.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md instruction mix (calls 8.0 %); code native_fast_paths.h:1894): every bctrl executed while the mask is non-zero; virtual calls are ~8 % of hot guest instructions (calls row), duration of non-zero mask windows not measured
- **Design:** Test only the bits the boundary acts on: (mask & (bit(InputReport) | bit(Transfer))) == 0. Expose the bit mask as a constant in native_api.h. Keep direct_dispatch_suspended for external-dispatch ownership.
- **Risk** (low): An indirect callee would start before a pending VI/AI/DEC edge is delivered instead of at the boundary; the callee's own first backward branch or call return delivers it (event_pending escalates every counted checkpoint), so the delay is a few hundred instructions. Input keeps its boundary service.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.005-0.05 ms/frame. Subset of the cached-call share (R-CALL-CACHED); mask windows are short (events are consumed at the next checkpoint) except EE-masked ones (R-CKPT-MASKED). Assume 0.05-0.3 % of the simulation thread -> est_ms = 0.0005-0.003 * 8.9-16.9 = 0.005-0.05 ms.

### 135. H-MEMSET: memset is translated (native body unwired); lean memcpy still resolves spans through the region table

- **Status:** open. Area: libc natives (memset/memcpy). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--lean-memset`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** MSL memset 0x80004388 runs as translated code: word and byte store loops, each non-stack store with the gather-pipe test, plus budgeted branch checkpoints. native_memset_80004388 exists in the header but no translator table references it, and it predates F4 (it calls guest_notify_write). lean_memcpy (0x80004338) calls resolve_guest twice (a region-table lookup) even in flat-view-only modules, and the five WPADRead memcpy sites keep the translated memcpy for the input trace.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:6623-6636, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:10356-10390, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7764-7770, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7535-7637
- **Why paid:** memset was never migrated when the string, GD and copy bodies became lean (generation 20). memcpy's RAM test uses resolve_guest because it was written before assumed_flat_view existed.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; code; memset is not separately named in the measured profiles): Per memset call and byte; rate unknown. Object construction, heap clears and GX/J3D buffer initialisation are the likely callers.
- **Design:** Translator flag --lean-memset. lean_memset: if [r3, r3+r5) is inside a RAM alias, std::memset through assumed_flat_view with no publication, like translated stores; otherwise the original byte path. Contract (&[3,4,5], &[]), r3 unchanged. In lean_memcpy, replace resolve_guest with assumed_flat_view when GALAXY_FLAT_VIEW_ONLY_ACTIVE. Add both to a diff_strings-style harness.
- **Risk** (low): Stores without publication are what translated memset does today, so GPU visibility is unchanged and still requires DCFlushRange. A destination in the locked cache or a device range keeps the byte path.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.005-0.05 ms/frame. Assumes 5-50 KB memset per frame: translated at about 1-2 ns per byte (a store loop with tests at 3 GHz) versus about 0.05 ns per byte host memset gives 0.005-0.1 ms. Capped at 0.05 ms because libc as a whole was 2.1 % of guest samples (measured, Native16) and most of that was the string routines, which are already lean.

### 136. T-CMAKE-ISA: The AVX2 module is built without BMI1/BMI2/LZCNT

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: generated CMake flags. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--native-isa-bmi2 (CMake GALAXY_NATIVE_ISA=AVX2_BMI2)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** clang-cl builds the module with -mavx2 -mfma -mmovbe only. Consequences:
- variable shifts and rotates (slw/srw/sraw/rlwnm) use shl/shr/rol r,cl, which is 2-3 uops on Skylake, instead of shlx/sarx (1 uop);
- cntlzw uses bsr plus cmov;
- andc cannot use andn.
The host probe tests only AVX2, FMA and OSXSAVE.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:11236-11262, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:11300-11331, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5478-5530, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:5686-5700
- **Why paid:** The ISA selection predates these extensions being considered.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; assumed): Once per variable shift, rotate or cntlzw. Assumed under 1% of hot guest instructions.
- **Design:** Extend the CMake host probe to CPUID.7.EBX bit 3 (BMI1) and bit 8 (BMI2) and CPUID.80000001.ECX bit 5 (LZCNT). On such hosts add -mbmi -mbmi2 -mlzcnt and include the result in the module compatibility key.
- **Risk** (low): An AVX2 CPU without BMI2 would hit an illegal instruction; the probe prevents that.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0-0.05 ms/frame. Under 1% of guest instructions x 1-2 uops saved = up to 0.02 x86 per guest instruction, about 0.25% of game code, at most about 0.02-0.05 ms (est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms).

### 137. T-ENTRY-PRUNE: Call-return interior entries are created for calls that can never be resumed

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: control flow, code size. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--prune-leaf-continuations`. Already listed: False docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md lists interior entries only as removed by checkpoint-free generation (item 4)
- **What:** call_return_continuations turns every bl return address into an interior entry. That includes:
- bl _savegpr_N/_restgpr_N, which are lowered inline;
- calls to checkpoint-free leaves, which have had no call-return checkpoint since generation 21;
- lean bodies and native pure helpers.
Each entry costs a case in the owner's 'switch (context->pc)', a resident_entry reload stub (one load per used register) and, for non-resident bodies, a call_return_X block. Any function with entries also pays 'context->pc != address' on every call, and callers store context->pc before every call.
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1824-1849, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1797-1822, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:1338-1367, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3549-3572, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:6232-6262, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:6853-6866, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:7106-7124, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:826-840, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/residency.rs:990-993
- **Why paid:** After a flag return, cooperative resume re-enters at every outer frame's return address. The continuation set is computed without asking whether the callee can raise a transfer at all.
- **Frequency** (derived; measured counts, docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md (F6: 178,776 entries; gen21 call-return sites 304,463 to 283,457) and docs/TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS.md 1.4 (11,719 _savegpr sites)): Today there are 178,776 interior entries. 11,719 _savegpr sites plus a similar number of _restgpr sites, and 21,006 leaf call sites, make roughly 44-55K of them provably never resume points. Every call into an owner pays a pc store and check.
- **Design:** - Exclude from call_return_continuations any return site whose callee is gpr_save_restore_entry, is_checkpoint_free_leaf, a lean body or a native pure helper, unless the address is also a branch target, a data pointer, or an rfi/OSSaveContext continuation.
- Optional second step: omit 'context->pc = target;' before direct calls to callees with no interior entries. This needs an audit of the runtime's context->pc readers (about 340 sites in runtime/src).
- **Risk** (medium): If the runtime ever resumes at a pruned pc (a transfer raised inside a leaf through a device callback, or the LR trampoline walking through a leaf), the owner reaches guest_execution_fault 'invalid interior function entry'. That is a loud crash, not silent corruption. Removing the pc store could mislead runtime diagnostics or policies that key on context->pc.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.08 ms/frame. Code size, derived: a stub is about used registers x 6 B plus a jump-table slot, roughly 70-150 B per entry, so 44-55K entries are about 3-8 MB of the 147.6 MB module. Per-call work: 2-3 uops of pc store and check, removed only for owners that lose every entry (assumed 5-15% of calls): 8% x 0.1 x 2.5 = 0.02 x86 per guest instruction, about 0.25% of game code, 0.02 ms by est_ms = share x 0.625 x 12.9 ms. The cache and TLB gain is a hypothesis.

### 138. G-LATCH-LAYOUT: PendingEventLatch packs all kinds' locks, counts and the hot mask into shared cache lines; Skylake PAUSE makes its spin bound 14x longer

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: false sharing. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_LATCH_SPIN_NS (spin bound; layout change unconditional, or behind GALAXY_LATCH_PADDED=1 for A/B)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:56 lists 'event latch lock 0.53 %' as a cost; the layout cause and the Skylake PAUSE bound are not listed
- **What:** The latch stores slot_locks_ for all 8 kinds in 8 contiguous bytes, which share a line with contended_acquisitions_. counts_ for all kinds is one 64-byte line, and replaceable_generations_ is another. mask_ (read by every bctrl, native_fast_paths.h:2052, and every checkpoint test, native_api.h:15172-15173) sits on the line that also holds max_spins_, idle_wake_event_ and idle_waiting_ (written seq_cst by the simulation thread around each idle wait). It is embedded in RuntimeState next to last_timebase_ticks/last_timebase_qpc, which the simulation thread writes on clock reads. Producers on the broker, input, audio and IOS threads publishing different kinds still bounce the same lock and count lines, and each publish costs about 3-5 cross-core line transfers. lock_slot spins up to 1,024 _mm_pause before yielding. On Skylake PAUSE is about 140 cycles instead of about 10, so the bound is about 143 k cycles (48 us) rather than the intended short spin.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/runtime_timeline.h:301, runtime/include/galaxy/runtime_timeline.h:451-475, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:870-901, runtime/src/runtime_timeline.cpp:1003-1042, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:2356-2366, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:33440
- **Why paid:** Correctness-first publication protocol (per-kind spin lock, then count, then mask bit). No alignas or cache-line padding exists anywhere in runtime/ (grep for alignas(64) and hardware_destructive_interference_size finds nothing).
- **Frequency** (measured; CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:57 (rates); event latch lock 0.53 % of simulation samples, CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:56): Each publication and each consumer take: about 450 idle wakes/s, 420 broker wakes/s, plus VI (60/s), AI DMA and input edges, roughly 1,000-2,000/s, against a mask read on every bctrl and checkpoint (9,400 checkpoint bodies/s; many more inline tests).
