- **Design:** Give each kind an alignas(64) slot holding its lock, count, replaceable generation and period. PeriodicBatch is 40 B, so the slot needs 128 B or a moved field. Move the contention diagnostics into a separate cold array. Put mask_ alone on its own line, and idle_waiting_ with idle_wake_event_ on another. Declare the RuntimeState member alignas(64) so the sim-written timebase fields cannot share mask_'s line. Replace the fixed 1,024-PAUSE bound with a TSC-time bound (e.g. 2 us, then yield) so Skylake and pre-Skylake spin for the same time. mask_address() stays valid for the module, so this is not an ABI change.
- **Risk** (low): Layout-only change plus a spin-bound change. The publication ordering is unchanged. The only risk is a missed wake if the mask or idle_waiting_ Dekker pair loses its seq_cst ordering during the refactor; runtime_timeline_tests cover publish and take.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.005-0.04 ms/frame. Upper anchor: event latch lock 0.53 % of the simulation thread x 12.9 ms = 0.068 ms/frame (measured share, projected). The false-sharing part is 1,000-2,000 publications/s x 3-5 extra line transfers x ~80-150 cycles = 0.24-1.5 M cycles/s, which is 0.0013-0.008 ms/frame at 3 GHz. Add the avoidable spin time inside the 0.068 ms (assumed 5-50 %): 0.005-0.04 ms.

### 139. R-CLASSIFY-TAIL-COPY: Classifier copies the whole next chunk into pending_fifo_ whenever a streamed chunk ends mid-command

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: PE pre-parse. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_FIFO_TAIL_STITCH (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** FramePeEventClassifier::classify keeps an incomplete trailing command in pending_fifo_. On the next call it inserts the entire new chunk behind that tail, parses the pending buffer, then erases the consumed prefix. Streamed chunks are cut at 32-byte burst boundaries (take_gx_fifo takes only complete bursts), which almost never coincide with command boundaries. So nearly every streamed chunk after the first goes down the copy path: one extra memcpy of the whole ~38 KB chunk, plus an erase/memmove of the leftover.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:2948-2970, runtime/include/galaxy/native_host.h:992-999, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:11841-11900
- **Why paid:** The parser needs contiguous bytes for a command that straddles two chunks. The simplest implementation concatenates whole chunks.
- **Frequency** (derived; Code path at gx_backend.cpp:2957-2969 plus 'about 5 chunks of 38 KB per frame' measured in docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:287): About 4-5 times per frame (streamed chunks after the first), ~38 KB each, so about 150-190 KB of extra copying per frame.
- **Design:** Stitch only the straddling command. Copy the old tail plus exactly the bytes the command needs (its length is already computed before parsing; NATIVE_EXECUTION Native20 'FIFO chunk boundaries') into a small scratch buffer, parse that one command, then parse the rest of the new chunk in place from its span. Apply the same change to the render and dependency parsers' pending_fifo_ (outside this area).
- **Risk** (low): If the stitched length were computed wrongly, command boundaries would shift. The render-side preclassified/actual event comparison (gx_backend.cpp:8695-8720) and GALAXY_PE_CLASSIFY_VERIFY=1 catch that.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.01-0.03 ms/frame. 150-190 KB/frame memcpy at an assumed 8-15 GB/s on the 6500U = 10-24 us, plus the erase memmove. This is a subset of R-PE-PREPARSE's measured 2.5 %, not additional to it, and only worth doing if stage A is delayed.

### 140. G-DIRTY-SUMMARY-XCHG: Dirty drain does a locked exchange on all 704 RAM summary words every submission, even empty ones

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: dirty summary words. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_DIRTY_DRAIN_LOAD_FIRST (default 0 = exchange every group)`. Already listed: True CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:53 lists 'guest write drain 1.0 %' as a whole; PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11.md:574 proposes tracking only dependency ranges; the locked-exchange detail is not listed
- **What:** drain_guest_memory_writes visits every summary group of MEM1 (192 groups of 128 KB) and MEM2 (512 groups), skipping the physical hole. For each group it does dirty_word_summary_[group].exchange(0), a locked xchg and full fence (~18-25 cycles on Skylake that also drains the store buffer), whether or not the word is zero. It does this on the simulation thread at each FIFO submission. The summary array also ends adjacent to the render-thread-hot ps_key_/vs_key_/render_state_key_ in GxBackend, so the exchange on the last MEM2 group writes a line the render thread reads per draw. The 32-byte-granular page bitmap is 1.31 MB (0x14000000/32 bits), of which about 0.9 MB covers the hole and is never drained. That costs memory only.
- **Where:** runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:4851-4952, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5586, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5610-5612, runtime/include/galaxy/gx/gx_backend.h:1175-1206, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:5307-5321
- **Why paid:** An exchange is the simplest way to clear the summary without losing concurrent producer bits. The drain was optimized to skip 64 words per empty group, but not to skip the locked op itself.
- **Frequency** (derived; Group counts from kMem1Size/kMem2Size and 32 B x 64 x 64 = 128 KB per group; chunk count from NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:287): Per GX submission: about 5 streamed chunks per frame (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:287), plus the frame-end submission, so about 5-6 drains x 704 locked exchanges = 3,500-4,200 per frame.
- **Design:** Load the summary word with a relaxed load and exchange only when it is non-zero. The semantics are identical: a producer bit set after the load stays for the next drain, the same as one set right after an exchange that returned zero. Optionally add a third level (11 x 64-bit 'group has bits' words) so the drain touches only flagged groups. Pad the summary array to its own line, away from ps_key_.
- **Risk** (low): If the load-first change were done with a plain store instead of keeping the exchange on non-zero words, concurrent bits from the async disc/DMA producers could be lost and stale textures or display lists would render. Keeping the exchange for non-zero words avoids that.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.01-0.03 ms/frame. 3,500-4,200 xchg/frame x 18-25 cycles = 63 k-105 k cycles, which is 0.021-0.035 ms at 3.0 GHz. Most groups are empty, so most of that is removed: 0.01-0.03 ms. Upper anchor: guest write drain 1.0 % x 12.9 ms = 0.13 ms (measured share, CPU_GAP_REPORT.md:53). The ps_key_ false sharing is about 5 render misses/frame, which is negligible.

### 141. R-PE-IRQ-DELIVERY: PE finish/token delivered as emulated interrupts: wake, external-interrupt dispatch, translated PE handlers

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: PE/CP/PI device emulation. Thread: simulation. Kind: both. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_STAGE_A_SYNC (default 0)`. Already listed: True docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md item 10 (PE register emulation); docs/SDK_REPLACEMENT_PLAN.md section 3 stage A
- **What:** When the render thread consumes a chunk carrying BP 0x45/0x48, it calls raise_pe_finish/raise_pe_token. These set atomics and call wake_idle_simulation, a WaitOnAddress wake. The simulation thread then runs dispatch_pe_interrupts, which enters the translated external-interrupt vector: __OSDispatchInterrupt, then GXFinishInterruptHandler 804BA354 / GXTokenInterruptHandler 804BA28C. Those read and acknowledge PE registers through device accesses, run the draw-done/draw-sync callback and OSWakeupThread for the GXDrawDone sleeper, followed by a cooperative-scheduler switch.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_host.h:628-642, runtime/include/galaxy/native_host.h:673-692, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:17207-17223, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:17037-17096, docs/sdk/RMGE01_SDK_FUNCTIONS.txt:507-525
- **Why paid:** The guest GX library waits for PE interrupts, so the runtime must synthesize the PE status-register levels and the PI interrupt and let translated code acknowledge them.
- **Frequency** (derived; Call sites in native_runtime.cpp; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:290 (PE/IOS host wakes 85/s)): About 2-3 per frame: GXDrawDone once per frame from endFrame (native_runtime.cpp:7189-7190 labels) and GXSetDrawSync from the 0x80385ADC caller (native_runtime.cpp:24273-24292). Total PE/IOS host wakes were measured at 85/s on the title route.
- **Design:** GX stage A host calls. GXDrawDone pushes the FIFO and blocks the calling fiber on the capture's receipt, then runs the draw-done callback directly on that fiber. GXSetDrawSync records the token mark (shared with R-PE-PREPARSE). The render thread posts a deferred sync callback. No PE status register, no PI cause bit and no translated interrupt handler run.
- **Risk** (medium): Callback context changes from interrupt context (EE off, OS interrupt nesting) to the calling fiber or deferred delivery. Code that relies on the callback running with interrupts disabled, or on GXGetDrawSync reading the PE token register, must be audited. A missed OSWakeupThread would hang the frame loop.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.06 ms/frame. 2-3 deliveries/frame x an assumed 500-1,500 guest instructions per dispatch (OS interrupt entry/exit, handler, OSWakeupThread, reschedule) x ~8 x86/guest instruction (CPU_GAP_REPORT) = 12k-36k x86 instructions per delivery, at ~1-1.5 IPC at 3 GHz = 3-12 us each, so 0.01-0.04 ms, plus wake latency. Not separately profiled.

### 142. R-FIFO-EXTRA-COPY: FIFO bytes are copied gather -> gx_fifo_ -> chunk.fifo although the capture already owns a vector

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: FIFO submission handoff / allocation. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_GX_FIFO_MOVE_SUBMIT (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Generated stores fill the 4 KB gather buffer. The drain appends it to gx_fifo_ (copy 1) and memmoves the tail. take_gx_fifo moves gx_fifo_ into the submission owner. render_frame takes (const std::byte*, size) and copies again into a pooled chunk.fifo (copy 2). The owner's vector is then recycled back to the producer. The const-pointer API forces copy 2.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_host.cpp:11884-11899, runtime/include/galaxy/native_host.h:992-1014, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:17135-17151, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5764-5768, runtime/src/gx/gx_backend.cpp:5787-5789, runtime/src/gx/gx_shim.cpp:20-34
- **Why paid:** render_frame's API serves several callers: the sync capture passes the live gx_fifo_ without taking it (native_runtime.cpp:17171-17199). The ordered submission owner keeps its vector until mark_submitted.
- **Frequency** (derived; Code path; FIFO volume from docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:167): Once per submission: 61-126 KB per frame copied a second time.
- **Design:** Add render_frame(std::vector<std::byte>&& fifo, ...) that moves the vector into chunk.fifo, and return the render thread's retired chunk vectors to the producer through fifo_pool_ to gx_fifo_spare_. Optionally publish the gather buffer as a window into gx_fifo_'s spare capacity (reserve 256 KB), so the drain only advances the size and the gather-to-vector copy disappears too.
- **Risk** (low): The ownership change must keep the submission owner's 'released_fifo' contract and the recycle caps (256 KB). The windowed-gather variant must handle vector reallocation, by reserving and failing over to the copy path.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.005-0.03 ms/frame. One extra memcpy of 61-126 KB from a warm source at an assumed 8-15 GB/s = 4-16 us per frame; up to twice that with the windowed-gather variant. Part of the measured 0.55 % gather-pipe bound for the host side.

### 143. T-PSMOV: ps_mr/ps_neg/ps_abs/ps_nabs/ps_merge/ps_cmp/ps_res/ps_sel still load and test HID2 (not covered by --assume-paired-singles)

- **Status:** done (--assume-paired-singles drops require_paired_single_mode). Area: Translator: floating point and paired singles. Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--assume-paired-singles (extend to require_paired_single_mode sites)`. Already listed: False TRANSLATOR_CPU_RESULTS.md 1.5 lists the three HID2 paths it removed; these sites are not among them
- **What:** These lowerings emit galaxy::require_paired_single_mode(context, false, services, pc) unconditionally. It is an out-of-line-capable check (`test byte [rdi+0x2b7],0x20; je`) with a cold fault call, which keeps services and pc live. It is defined in native_api.h without the kAssumePairedSingles fold that hid2_admits/duplicates_single got. The moves themselves are 64-bit integer copies through the context (ps_merge00: 2 loads + 2 stores).
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4685-4756, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:4952-4996, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:12143-12157, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:42-67
- **Why paid:** --assume-paired-singles (done on this branch) switched only the fast:: helpers to hid2_admits; the textual lowering of the paired move, merge and compare instructions still emits the exact admission call.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; CPU_GAP_REPORT category (measured); the split is a hypothesis): Hypothesis 0.5-1.5 % of hot guest instructions (ps_merge* and ps_mr are common in vector code; part of the measured 3.7 % category).
- **Design:** Under assume_paired_singles, the translator stops emitting require_paired_single_mode for these opcodes. Alternatively, guard its body in native_api.h with `if constexpr (fast::kAssumePairedSingles) return;`, keeping the LSQE variant for quantized loads. Under T-PSLAYOUT the moves become shuffles.
- **Risk** (low): The same argument as --assume-paired-singles: RMGE01 sets HID2.PSE in __init_hardware before any game code and never clears it, and on hardware a paired instruction with PSE clear is an illegal-instruction exception. Only a boot-path paired instruction before __init_hardware would differ.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.0028-0.032 ms/frame. est_ms = 0.625 x f x T_sim(8.9/16.9 ms). 2 x86 (+ register pressure from the cold call arguments) x 0.5-1.5 % = 0.01-0.03 x86 per guest instruction; f = 0.05-0.3 %. Low = 0.625*0.0005*8.9 = 0.0028; high = 0.625*0.003*16.9 = 0.032 ms.

### 144. T-WGPIPE-BATCH: Proven gather-pipe runs go through the host write_device callback behind three trace gates

- **Status:** open. Area: Translator: memory access (wgpipe batching). Thread: simulation. Kind: translator. Proposed flag: `--wgpipe-batch-append`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Statically proven gather-pipe store runs (2 to 16 stores) build a std::array of bytes. In release builds they first test three trace gates (function-local statics initialised by getenv), then call memory->write_device, which is the host's general device dispatch. A single proven pipe store instead uses the cheap inline append to the gather buffer (F21).
- **Where:** crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:2811-2919, crates/nebula-recomp-core/src/translate.rs:3298-3365, runtime/include/galaxy/native_api.h:5735-5810, runtime/include/galaxy/native_fast_paths.h:119-140
- **Why paid:** The batch path predates the in-place gather buffer (F21) and was never moved onto wgpipe_append. The scalar-trace alternative is kept for diagnostics.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; gather pipe 0.55% of the simulation thread measured, docs/CPU_GAP_REPORT.md; batch count assumed): Once per proven pipe run: GX register writes inside the SDK GX functions, assumed thousands per frame.
- **Design:** Make guest_write_wgpipe_bytes append into memory->wgpipe_gather_fast in place, splitting at the 32-byte burst boundary and flushing through wgpipe_flush exactly as wgpipe_append does. Fall back to write_device only when the fast buffer is unavailable. A translator flag drops the guest_wgpipe_batch_requires_scalar_path alternative in release modules.
- **Risk** (medium): A mistake at the burst boundary or in flush ordering corrupts the GX FIFO. It would show in diff_gx and in frame dumps.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.01-0.05 ms/frame. The gather pipe is 0.55% of the simulation thread (measured), i.e. 0.0055 x 12.9 = 0.071 ms on the 6500U (est_ms = share_of_thread x 12.9 ms). Assuming the batch path is 15-70% of that and is mostly removed gives 0.01 to 0.05 ms.

### 145. R-COOP-SWITCH: Cooperative thread switches and idle passes copy the whole register file and do map lookups

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: coop scheduler. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_COOP_LEAN_SWITCH (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Scheduler::switch_to saves and loads the full guest register file (32 GPRs, 32 FPR + 32 PS1 words, 8 GQRs, SPRs: about 700 bytes each way), does several fibers_ map lookups (fibers_[running_thread_], fibers_[next], running_chain() find), exchange_chain republishes the checkpoint gate, and SwitchToFiber with FIBER_FLAG_FLOAT_SWITCH saves/restores MXCSR. call_switch_callback copies the register file again around the SDK switch callback when one is installed. Every idle pass (coop_idle) also saves and restores the register file.
- **Where:** runtime/src/coop_scheduler.cpp:45-75, runtime/src/coop_scheduler.cpp:137-172, runtime/src/coop_scheduler.cpp:193-312, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:29950-29966, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:29976-29992
- **Why paid:** Guest threads share one PpcContext; each fiber keeps its own copy so a switch is exact without OSSaveContext/OSLoadContext. Float switching keeps FTZ/DAZ per fiber.
- **Frequency** (hypothesis; code; idle passes from docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md Native21 (61 k per run)): per guest thread switch (counted by switches_, not published in the docs) and per idle pass (~500/s)
- **Design:** Give each fiber its own PpcContext and switch the context pointer the module receives instead of copying (needs the module to re-read context after a host call that may switch, which generated code already does after calls), or copy only GPRs/FPRs actually written since the last switch. Replace the fibers_ map with an array indexed by a small thread slot stored next to the OSThread address. Drop FIBER_FLAG_FLOAT_SWITCH when every fiber runs the same MXCSR (set once at start). Skip the idle-pass save/load when the pass delivers nothing.
- **Risk** (medium): Switching context pointers changes the contract with generated code (the services/context pointer passed down a native call chain must not be cached across a switch); the copy-reduction alternative is local to the runtime. MXCSR must still be restored if a guest thread ever changes FPSCR rounding (fast float ignores it).
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.003-0.05 ms/frame. Assumed 300-2,000 switches/s plus ~500 idle passes/s at 0.5-2 us each on the 6500U = 0.4-5 ms/s -> 0.007-0.08 ms/frame; take 0.003-0.05 ms after allowing that part of it is in the idle-wait row. Needs [coop] switches= from a recording.

### 146. R-AI-SUBMIT: Per-AI-buffer heap allocation, mutex and SetEvent on the simulation thread

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: audio output. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_AUDIO_SPSC_RING (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** Each AI DMA buffer handed to the output runs these steps on the simulation thread. It allocates a SubmittedBuffer (make_unique plus a vector resize of about 2,240 B), converts and gain-scales it, and takes pending_mutex_ with 2-3 steady_clock reads. It then calls SetEvent(buffer_end_event_), which wakes the MMCSS Pro Audio queue thread. That thread preempts whatever runs on the logical CPU it lands on, then frees the buffer on another thread.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_host.cpp:3393-3410, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:4167-4283, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:4774-4857, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:2367-2400
- **Why paid:** Strict audio-proof bookkeeping: an anomaly ledger, measurement windows and backpressure without dropping. A simple producer/consumer design was built with a mutex-protected deque and an explicit wake per buffer.
- **Frequency** (derived; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:309 (8 buffer periods = 140 ms)): About 57 buffers/s (17.5 ms AI buffer period)
- **Design:** Preallocate a ring of 32 fixed-size buffers as a lock-free SPSC queue (simulation thread to audio thread). Drop the SetEvent while XAudio2 still has 2 or more queued buffers: the worker already wakes on OnBufferEnd (buffer_end_event_) every 17.5 ms and can drain the ring then. Signal only when the queue_miror shows the voice starving (queued is 0 or 1). Stamp accepted_ns from the timeline tick the AI service already sampled, so no extra steady_clock read is needed.
- **Risk** (low): With the SetEvent dropped, a buffer could wait up to one OnBufferEnd interval at startup or after an underflow. The starvation rule above covers that. The ledger ordering must be kept for the strict-audio proof.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.005-0.02 ms/frame. Per buffer on the 6500U: waking SetEvent 1.34 us measured on the desktop (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:419) * 2.8 PassMark ratio = 3.8 us, plus alloc/free, convert and mutex of about 1-4 us, so 5-8 us. Times 57/s gives 0.29-0.46 ms/s, or 0.005-0.008 ms/frame. The Pro Audio thread's own wake (5-10 us * 57/s) on the other core brings the high bound to 0.02 ms/frame.

### 147. R-THREADTIMES: GetThreadTimes syscall up to once per millisecond for host-suspension detection

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: OS syscalls. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_SUSPENSION_CPU_SAMPLE (default ms; vi = sample at VI and wait returns)`. Already listed: False PERFORMANCE_PLAN_NATIVE11 listed GetThreadTimes 0.9 % before the 1 ms cache; the residual is not listed
- **What:** runtime_time_base_ticks refreshes the thread CPU time with GetThreadTimes whenever the sampled counter is 1 ms or more past the cached sample. A busy simulation thread therefore makes about one kernel call per running millisecond.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:184-201, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5476-5493, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:5414-5449
- **Why paid:** Host-suspension detection (laptop sleep, debugger) compares elapsed ticks against elapsed CPU time. The 1 ms cache bound keeps the over-report small enough never to invent a candidate.
- **Frequency** (derived; qpc_per_millisecond cache rule at native_runtime.cpp:5482-5493): Up to 1,000/s, about 800/s when the simulation thread runs ~80 % of the time
- **Design:** Sample CPU time only at the points where a suspension can be acted on: VI boundaries (60/s) and the return from each blocking wait (idle, VI, THP join). Elsewhere carry the last value forward. A candidate interval spans at least one retrace, so a per-VI baseline still bounds it. Alternatively raise the cache window to 4 ms behind a flag and check the suspension tests.
- **Risk** (low): A coarser baseline can over-report CPU time across an interval and suppress a real suspension candidate. That leads to an AI/VI backlog recovery instead of a rebase, not a crash. Covered by the existing host-suspension unit tests.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.008-0.017 ms/frame. 800-1,000 calls/s * (162 ns desktop measured, NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:419, * 2.8 PassMark ratio plus KVA-shadow syscall overhead on Skylake: 0.45-1.0 us) = 0.45-1.0 ms/s = 0.008-0.017 ms/frame.

### 148. R-WGPIPE-REG-DRAIN: Every CP/PE/VI/PI register read or write forces a gather drain and a fast-path republish

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime GX on simulation thread: CP/PE/PI/VI device emulation. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_WGPIPE_DRAIN_FIFO_REGS_ONLY (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** read_device and write_device call drain_wgpipe_gather_bursts() for any access in 0xCC000000-0xCC003FFF: CP, PE, VI and PI, including PI_INTSR reads during interrupt dispatch and VI polling. Writes additionally run refresh_wgpipe_fast_publication(). Each drain lands the partial buffer (ring memcpy, notify, vector insert) and memmoves the sub-32-byte tail, which fragments the 4 KB batching into small flushes.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_host.cpp:13150-13163, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:13539-13550, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:11824-11839, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:11895-11899
- **Why paid:** F21 batch invariant: nothing may observe a stale FIFO. Applying it to the whole 16 KB device window was the simple conservative choice.
- **Frequency** (derived; docs/NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:222 (IPC, PI, VI register accesses at 1-2 thousand per second each); exact current rates not re-measured): Per CP/PE/VI/PI register access, about 1-2 thousand per second per device, so roughly 50-100 per frame.
- **Design:** Drain only for registers that observe FIFO bytes or pointers: CP FIFO registers 0xCC000020-0xCC00003F, PI FIFO registers 0xCC00300C-0xCC003017, and the PE token/finish registers only when R-PE-PREPARSE is off. Republish only when a write changed cpu_fifo_base_/top_/cp_fifo_base_/end_.
- **Risk** (low): If a register that does observe FIFO state (for example PI_CP_WRITE read by GXGetFifoPtrs) were left off the list, the game would see a stale write pointer and could misjudge FIFO space. Keep the list conservative and assert in the diagnostic build.
- **Estimate** (hypothesis): 0.003-0.02 ms/frame. 50-100 drains per frame x ~50-200 ns each (partial memcpy, notify, vector insert, tail memmove) = 3-20 us. Part of the measured 'gather pipe 0.55 %' (0.05-0.09 ms on the 6500U), together with R-FIFO-RING-MIRROR and R-FIFO-EXTRA-COPY; do not add those beyond that bound.

### 149. R-XINPUT-PROBE: Empty XInput slots enumerated in a burst on the simulation thread every 2 s

- **Status:** open. Area: Runtime: input. Thread: simulation. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `GALAXY_XINPUT_ASYNC_PROBE (default 0)`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** poll_native_hid_controller runs inside the 100 Hz virtual Wii Remote report build on the simulation thread. A failed slot is retried 2 s later. All empty slots fail in the same report, so they all retry together, and XInputGetState on an empty slot enumerates devices. Every 2 s one report pays up to 4 enumerations back to back.
- **Where:** runtime/src/native_host.cpp:6579-6626, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:8501-8520, runtime/src/native_host.cpp:31585-31597
- **Why paid:** XInput has no arrival notification, so hot-plug was handled by periodic retry. A per-report retry was already measured as too costly and was cut to 2 s.
- **Frequency** (derived; s_slot_retry_at logic at native_host.cpp:6588-6601): One burst of up to 4 empty-slot probes every 2 s in Auto/Controller input mode (the default Auto mode polls)
- **Design:** Move empty-slot probing off the simulation thread. The controller-HID worker already registers CM_Register_Notification for HID arrivals (controller_hid.cpp:686-706); add the XUSB interface GUID and run XInputGetState on empty slots there, at device arrival or at most every 2 s with the slots staggered. Publish a connected-slot bitmask atomically. The simulation thread calls XInputGetState only for slots in that mask.
- **Risk** (low): A pad plugged in could be seen up to one notification or 2 s later, the same as today. If the notification is missed, the stagger fallback still finds it.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.002-0.021 ms/frame. Code comment measurement (native_host.cpp:6584-6587): p50 274 us, p90 913 us per report that probes the empty slots. On the 6500U * 1.0-2.8 gives 0.27-2.6 ms in a single frame every 120 frames. Averaged that is 0.27/120 = 0.002 to 2.6/120 = 0.021 ms/frame. The real harm is the spike: with the simulation thread at about 12.9 ms/frame, a 0.3-2.6 ms hitch every 2 s can drop a frame.

### 150. G-PE-FLAG-LINE: Render-written PE flags share a cache line with sim-read FIFO streaming fields

- **Status:** open. Area: Skylake memory hierarchy: false sharing. Thread: all. Kind: runtime. Proposed flag: `none (unconditional layout change) or GALAXY_PE_FLAGS_PADDED for A/B`. Already listed: False 
- **What:** In GuestAddressSpace, pe_finish_pending_, pe_token_pending_ and pe_token_value_ are written from the render thread through the gx_pe_finish/gx_pe_token callbacks. They are interleaved with fifo_stream_threshold_ and fifo_stream_next_, which maybe_stream_gx_fifo reads on the simulation thread after each gather-pipe burst append, and with idle_wake_callback_ and pi_interrupt_cause_latch_. Each PE publication invalidates the simulation thread's copy of the streaming fields. Every other cross-thread structure I checked (frame queue under queue_mutex_, timing_frame_index_) is accessed at most a few times per frame.
- **Where:** runtime/include/galaxy/native_host.h:655-666, runtime/include/galaxy/native_host.h:1829-1834, runtime/include/galaxy/native_host.h:1875-1884, runtime/src/native_runtime.cpp:30418-30430
- **Why paid:** Members were added next to related state without regard to which thread writes them.
- **Frequency** (derived; NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:167 for burst count; PE events per frame assumed tens): PE finish and token publications: tens per frame (render thread); streaming-field reads: 2,000-4,000 bursts per frame (NATIVE_EXECUTION.md:167). Each publication causes one simulation-thread miss.
- **Design:** Group the render-written PE atomics into one alignas(64) struct placed away from sim-hot members.
- **Risk** (low): Layout only; no ordering change.
- **Estimate** (derived): 0.0005-0.003 ms/frame. 10-60 PE publications/frame x ~80-150 cycles per cross-core miss = 0.8 k-9 k cycles = 0.0003-0.003 ms at 3 GHz. Listed for completeness; the value is very small.
